# Decisions

Newest first inside each section. A decision names the options considered, the choice, and the reason. The Lead adds entries as it decides; Mohammad reverses any entry by adding a dated line under it.

## Awaiting Mohammad's review

### A1207 2026-10-27 The folder pick is checked against the engine saved in Settings; /Volumes is not warned; a deleted sandbox is not pointed at Rebuild (M5.1e, builder)
Three small choices. (1) At pick time the engine is already saved in Settings (the engine step comes first), so `folderBlockers` uses `savedEnginePath` from `createUnrealServices`; `create` uses the engine it is given, which wins. Options: send the engine path with the pick request (a new IPC argument, an app-shell change); check only in `create`. Chosen: the saved engine, so the pick answer needs a one-line change and `create` is the final guard. (2) On a Mac `/Volumes/...` holds external drives as well as network shares and there is no way to tell them apart without asking the disk, so it is not warned about; only Windows UNC paths are. (3) Nothing in the app can set up a deleted sandbox again: Settings' Rebuild sandbox needs an existing `Dojo.uproject`, and first-run is one-time. So the sandbox-gone message says only "put the folder back where it was (or reconnect its drive)", and the older `sandboxNotReady` message no longer points at Settings either. The missing action is logged in `docs/problems.md` and planned as M5.1k. The engine and sandbox messages are worded for every reason a read can fail, not only a move. Reverse cost: edit one message, or add the checks.

### A1206 2026-10-27 Folder comparison ignores letter case and slash style on Windows only; an engine inside the picked folder is ignored (M5.1e, builder)
`C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.8` and `c:/program files/epic games/ue_5.8/` are the same folder on Windows, so the blocker compares through `PlatformPaths.pathKey` (lower case, separators and trailing slash normalised) and then part by part, so `UE_5.8.1` is not taken for being inside `UE_5.8`. A Mac keeps exact case (APFS can be either, and an exact match is always safe). The engine root inside the chosen folder is not a blocker: the sandbox is created in a new subfolder and never touches the engine. Options: block that too; compare as plain text prefixes. Chosen as stated, to block only what can break. Reverse cost: one function.

### A1205 2026-10-27 A failed preflight is a rejected call, not a RunResult (M5.1e, builder)
A moved engine or deleted sandbox means no run happened, so there is no result to describe. Options: resolve a `RunResult` with `unrelatedFailure` set; reject with the `AppError`. Chosen: reject, through a new `RunPreflight` used first by `runQuest`, `checkSnippetSyntax` and `rebuildSandbox`. The renderer already shows a rejected run, Submit already stops on a rejected `runQuest` without reviewing or recording (A1208), and `checkSnippetSyntax` and `rebuildSandbox` have no result shape for it. A locator failure that is not a version problem, even a raw `ENOENT`, becomes `engine-not-found` with the raw text kept only as `detail`; a too-old engine keeps `engine-version-unsupported`. Reverse cost: wrap the rejection into a result in `QuestRunService.runQuest`.

### A1204 2026-10-27 Warn about five risky places, block only inside the engine folder (M5.1e, builder)
A bad sandbox location should be explained before a build fails, but a warning that blocks a folder that works would only annoy. Options: block everything risky; warn about everything. Chosen: warn (the folder can still be used) about a Windows path over 100 characters (as before), non-ASCII letters (some build tools mishandle them), cloud-synced folders (OneDrive, Dropbox, Google Drive, iCloud Drive; sync can lock files mid-build), network paths (slow, drops mid-build) and Program Files (Windows protects it, so builds can fail with permission errors). Block only a folder inside the engine folder: an engine update, repair or uninstall can wipe it, and the build would mix sandbox files with engine files. Each is one plain sentence in `SandboxFolderRules`. Reverse cost: one entry per rule.

### A1214 2026-10-27 The fake's outside failure is a marker comment line, and a rejected Submit clears the other call's error (M5.1h, builder)
The screen needed a way to show an outside failure without a real engine. Options: a special quest id; a settings switch; a marker line in the learner's file. Chosen: a `// dojo:fake-outside-failure` line in any file (`FakeOutsideFailure.ts`), so a test or a curious learner triggers it from the editor on the fake data source (the real app ignores it), and it works on every quest. The fake's Run then answers with `unrelatedFailure` (code `build-environment-failed`, Live Coding active, no tests) and its Submit throws that same error before anything is reviewed or recorded, as the real submit does (A1208). Related screen rule: `useQuestRun` gained `clearError`, and a new Run or Submit clears the other call's error, so a rejected Submit's message does not stay beside a newer result. When a rejected Submit shows the same failure a Run result also carries, only the Submit's box shows. Reverse cost: delete one fake file and one method.

### A1213 2026-10-27 The headline "Not your code" and which errors get it (M5.1h, builder)
The learner must see at once that a failure is not their fault. Options: reword each message; a headline prop on `AppErrorView`; a separate view for the Build screen. Chosen: an optional `headline` prop on the shared `AppErrorView` (a bold line above the message, used here as "Not your code"), always set when a Run result carries `unrelatedFailure`, and set on a rejected Run or Submit only when the code is an environment code (`build-environment-failed`, `toolchain-missing`, `engine-not-found`, `engine-version-unsupported`, `sandbox-not-ready`, `sandbox-path-too-long`, `sandbox-path-unsupported`, `sandbox-copy-failed`, `editor-launch-failed`, listed in `outsideFailureCodes.ts`). Other rejections (invalid payload, unexpected) get the plain compact error with no claim about whose fault it is. The label beside a result says "could not build (not your code)" instead of "did not compile". The error box offers Run again or Submit again. Reverse cost: one prop and one small file.

### A1212 2026-10-27 A failed start shows a plain dialog and quits; a crashed window reloads once (M5.1g, builder)
Before, a failed `start()` only wrote to the log and left the app running with no window. Options: leave it; show the error with its stack; show a plain dialog, log the detail, and quit. For a crashed page process (`render-process-gone`): leave it; reload every time; reload once. Chosen: the plain dialog (title "Unreal Dojo could not start", no stack, no error text) then quit, even if the dialog itself fails; and one reload per window, with later crashes only logged, so a page that crashes on load never loops. A clean exit is ignored, and a destroyed window is left alone. In a hidden e2e run the failed-start box is skipped (log only) so no modal native window appears (A77, A970). Reverse cost: delete one call in `index.ts` each.

### A1211 2026-10-27 Quit waits up to 3 s for pending saves (M5.1g, builder)
The last change should reach the disk before the app closes, but a stuck disk must not freeze the quit. Options: no wait; wait without a limit; wait up to 3 s through the bounded `whenIdle` (A1195). Chosen: 3 s (`QUIT_SAVE_WAIT_MS`), held once in `src/main/lifecycle/holdQuitForSaves.ts`, separate from the Unreal quit sequence so each does one job. The quit is always held for the length of one `whenIdle` call, which answers at once when nothing is pending. The second `app.quit()` is deferred to a later turn (`setImmediate`, injectable) so it never runs inside the first quit's own event; on macOS that could leave the window closed and the app in the Dock. That case (Cmd+Q with nothing pending) is unverified on macOS (`docs/problems.md`). The real limit: saves queued before quit are awaited for up to 3 s. A change made after that first wait, while the Unreal stop (up to 5 s for runs plus 15 s for the editor) is still running with the window open, is lost, and the file stays intact because the write is atomic. Accepted, because the window is closing and the learner just pressed quit; a second hold was considered and left out to keep this small. Reverse cost: change the constant or remove one call in `index.ts`.

### A1210 2026-10-27 The toolchain check is cached for the session (M5.1g, builder)
Title refetches the environment status on every window focus, and the check runs vswhere (Windows) or xcode-select (macOS). Options: check on every read; check once per session and refresh on request; check on a timer. Chosen: once per session; `index.ts` starts it in the background at startup (`void refreshToolchain()`), so it is usually done before the Title first asks. `refreshToolchain()` runs it again and replaces the kept answer, and the existing `checkToolchain` IPC call (the "Check again" button) goes through it. A build failure outside the learner's code should call the same refresh (M5.1d's side). A read that comes while the check is still running waits for it (bounded by the checker's own timeout), and concurrent reads share one check. A check that throws, even before it returns a promise, or cannot run caches null for the session until a refresh. Reverse cost: drop the cache field.

### A1209 2026-10-27 The detail tail is capped at 40 lines and comes from the build output only (M5.1f, builder)
A rejected Submit shows the learner why the run failed, so the end of the build output goes into `detail`. Options: the whole output; the last 40 lines; the last 40 lines plus the test messages. Chosen: the last 40 lines of `compilerOutput`, after the failure's own detail and a blank line (named constant `UNRELATED_FAILURE_DETAIL_LINES`). The cause of a crash is at the end of the log, a full build log can be megabytes, and test messages belong to the learner's code, not to a failure outside it. When the failure has no detail and the output is empty, `detail` stays undefined. Reverse cost: change one constant.

### A1208 2026-10-27 A Submit that failed outside the quest rejects with the AppError and records nothing (M5.1f, builder)
A UBT crash, timeout, missing engine, Live Coding being active, a full disk or a broken earlier quest is not the learner's code, so it must not break the spree or count toward the two-failed-submit offers. Options: record it as today; reject and record nothing; resolve with `recorded: false`. Chosen: reject with the run's `unrelatedFailure` after the cancel check, with no review and no `recordCodeAttempt` call. It matches the cancel rule, needs no `SubmitOutcome` change, and the renderer already has an error path. A cancel still wins. A normal failed compile or failed test is recorded as before. `progressSchema.ts` drops unknown keys on load, which is harmless because such attempts are never recorded. Reverse cost: record the attempt again by removing one call in `QuestSubmitService.submit`.

### A1198 2026-10-27 The load log line and the injectable clock (M5.1c, builder)
The catalog needs to say how long a load took, in the app and in the test. Options: log from `startLoading` in `index.ts`; log inside `ContentCatalog.loadAndIndex`; time only in the test. Chosen: one `console.info` line, `[content] loaded N quests in X ms`, written by `loadAndIndex` after the lookups are built, with an optional `now` in `ContentCatalogDependencies` (default `performance.now`). Logging inside the catalog covers every start path (startup, first request, a retry after a failure) and the time includes the folder check, the loader, and indexing. The fake clock lets a test check the line without waiting. Reverse cost: delete one line and one optional field.

### A1197 2026-10-27 The large fixture clones real quests instead of synthetic ones (M5.1c, builder)
The older 300-quest check (`writeLargePack`) cycles through 8 format examples, which are small and have no prerequisites. Options: reuse it; generate synthetic quests; clone the real pack quests. Chosen: `tests/support/largeContentFixture.ts` reads every real quest file in order and writes `LARGE_QUEST_COUNT` (300) retagged clones, running through the real quests again and again. Each pass over the real quests gets its own id prefix, so ids are unique; prerequisites and the `pieces:` ids of bosses are rewritten to the clone of the same pass, and the build throws if any would dangle. Worlds are numbered across all clones, 25 quests each, so there are always `ceil(300 / 25)` = 12 whatever the real quest count is, and each world sits in the pack of the pass its first quest belongs to. The real glossary and patterns are copied; each glossary `firstQuestId` is pointed at the first pass's clone, and that pointer joins the dangling check. Each pattern card's `useCaseQuestIds` keep the original ids on purpose: only the verifier reads them, not the load path. Real quests carry the real sizes (starter files, tests, reference plans), the real prerequisite chains, and the real term counts, so the parse, the alias table, the drill bank, and the unlock graph all do real work. A synthetic quest would understate every one of those. Cost: the clones follow the real quest shapes, so the timing drifts as the real content changes, which is the point. If a clone cannot satisfy a pointer (a quest id the real content does not have), the fixture fails loudly instead of loading with problems.

### A1196 2026-10-27 What "content load" measures, and the budget (M5.1c, builder)
"Content load under 300 ms for 300 quests" needs one definition. Options: only `ContentLoader.load`; the catalog until `listPacks`; the whole first-request path. Chosen: from `startLoading()` on a fresh `ContentCatalog` (made by `createContentCatalog`, the app's own wiring) until `listPacks()`, `getGlossaryAliases()`, and `ProgressContentService.getQuestAvailability()` (a fresh in-memory save) have all answered. That last call builds the drill bank from every world, so it is the real cost of the World Map's first paint. Input: 300 retagged real quests with the real glossary and patterns on real disk (A1197). Gate: the median of three fresh loads is under 300 ms (`LOAD_BUDGET_MILLISECONDS` in `tests/contentLoadBudget.test.ts`). The first (cold) run is printed with the others and is not hidden by a warm-up, but it is not gated because other builders share the machine and one cold run can spike. Reverse cost: change the constant or the run count.

### A1195 2026-10-27 `whenIdle` is bounded and never rejects (M5.1b, builder)
The app should let queued saves finish before it quits, but a stuck disk must not freeze the quit. Options: `whenIdle()` with no limit; `whenIdle(timeoutMs)` that rejects on timeout; `whenIdle(timeoutMs)` that always resolves, with true when the queue drained and false when the time ran out. Chosen: the third. A failed save is already its own caller's error (the queue keeps going after one), so the wait does not repeat it, and a caller needs no try/catch on the way out. Reverse cost: one method on `ProgressService`.

### A1194 2026-10-27 The kill test runs a TypeScript child through tsx so it tests the real code (M5.1b, builder)
A save that survives a kill has to be shown with a real killed process, not only the in-memory crash points. Options: compile the app files first and run the output; run a small hand-written JavaScript copy of the write steps; run `tests/progress/support/killedSaveWriter.ts` with `node --import tsx`, importing only `AtomicJsonFile` and `NodeFileSystem` by relative path. Chosen: the third. It needs no build step, `tsx` is already a devDependency, and the child runs the very same classes the app does, so a change to the write steps changes what the test proves. The parent kills it with SIGKILL (TerminateProcess on Windows) at 20 seeded random moments (seed logged, `DOJO_KILL_SEED` replays it). Reverse cost: swap the spawn line.

### A1193 2026-10-27 The backup is written to `.bak.tmp` then renamed; a complete `.tmp` is ignored on load (M5.1b, builder)
Copying straight onto `progress.json.bak` could leave a torn backup when the process is killed mid-copy, which would remove the fallback exactly when the main file is also bad. Options: copy in place (the old way); copy to `progress.json.bak.tmp` and rename it over `.bak`; also promote a complete `.tmp` on load. Chosen: the second, and no promotion. A leftover `.tmp` or `.bak.tmp` is never read; the next save overwrites it. A complete `.tmp` belongs to a call that never answered, so loading the old file is consistent with what the app told the learner. Reverse cost: one rename in `AtomicJsonFile.writeNow`.

### A1203 2026-10-27 A failed sandbox build always gets an explanation; a failed quest run only gets one with outside evidence (M5.1d, builder)
A sandbox build runs no quest, so any failure of it is about the setup or about quest code already in the sandbox. Options: the same conservative default as runs (no `failure` without evidence); always answer. Chosen: always answer. After the known signatures and the listed pipeline errors, `SandboxSetupResult.failure` names quests whose code in the sandbox has errors (a rebuild keeps them), then support code, then a blocking toolchain problem (only when no compiler or linker line exists), and last a generic `build-environment-failed` that says the setup on this computer is the cause and quotes the first error line.

A quest run is stricter (review fix round 1). Pipeline texts count as outside only when they cannot be the learner's doing: a program that could not start, none of the quest's tests running, and, before the code compiled, a build timeout or a build tool that crashed while starting. After a compile, a test timeout or a missing or unreadable test report may be the learner's crash or endless loop, so those, and any pipeline text nobody listed, stay with the quest (`RunResult.error` still shows them). Reverse cost: add the text to `outsidePipelineFailure`.

### A1202 2026-10-27 Signature strings confirmed on Windows UE 5.8.3, not run on macOS (M5.1d, builder)
The build output strings in `buildFailureSignatures.ts` come from three places. Confirmed by reading the UE 5.8.3 UnrealBuildTool source on Windows (HotReload.cs, MicrosoftPlatformSDK.cs, AppleToolChainSettings.cs): "Unable to build while Live Coding is active", "Unable to find valid ... C++ toolchain", "UnrealBuildTool requires at minimum the MSVC", "Unable to find requested Windows SDK", "Your Mac is set to use CommandLineTools", "Selected Xcode ... doesn't exist", "requires Xcode ... or newer". Taken from the compilers and operating systems (not reproduced from a real log here): LNK1180, LNK1104, C1081, "No space left on device", "being used by another process". No failing build was provoked on either machine. The Xcode and clang shapes were not run on macOS and are logged as unverified in `docs/problems.md`. Options: provoke each failure on the real engine (a full disk, a locked DLL); read the source and write fixtures in the real tool shape. Chosen: the second, because provoking a full disk or a locked DLL is unsafe on the learner's machine and the engine lock cost is high. Reverse cost: replace a fixture with a captured log.

### A1201 2026-10-27 The toolchain check runs only for failures nothing else could place (M5.1d, builder)
`checkToolchain` starts `vswhere` or `xcodebuild`, which takes a moment. Options: check on every failed run; check only when rules 1 to 5 of the explainer found no cause. Chosen: only then, and (review fix round 1) only when the output has no compiler or linker error line, because such a line proves the toolchain ran (Apple's linker output such as `Undefined symbols for architecture arm64:` counts too). A pass, a cancel, a run that compiled and only failed its tests, any line naming the learner's quest, and any output with a compile error never reach it, so an ordinary red test costs nothing extra and a toolchain problem cannot be blamed for the learner's typo. Reverse cost: move the call to the top of `explainRun`.

### A1200 2026-10-27 Failures are explained in main; the renderer's string guessing is only a fallback (M5.1d, builder)
Options: keep `lib/sandboxFailure.ts` guessing from the compiler text in the renderer; classify in main where the paths, the prerequisite sources and the toolchain check are known. Chosen: main. Only main knows which earlier quests were filled from a reference and can run the toolchain check, and strings are matched once, in data (`buildFailureSignatures.ts`), with fixtures. The renderer shows `unrelatedFailure` and `failure` when present and falls back to `lib/sandboxFailure.ts` when they are absent (an older main, or no explainer). Reverse cost: delete the explainer wiring; nothing else depends on it.

### A1199 2026-10-27 Blame the quest unless there is outside evidence (M5.1d, builder)
When a run fails and nothing points outside the learner's code, the learner sees the ordinary compile or test failure. Options: tell the learner "this may not be your code" whenever the cause is unclear; say nothing unless there is evidence. Chosen: say nothing. Evidence means a known outside line, a pipeline error, errors only in an earlier quest or support code, or a blocking toolchain problem. A wrong "it is not your code" sends a learner hunting for a fault they cannot fix and hides their real bug, which is worse than a missing hint. Unplaced linker lines, mixed earlier-quest and support errors, and a healthy toolchain all stay with the quest. Reverse cost: a one-line change in `explainRun` returning a generic failure.

Review fix round 1 tightened it: any line of the output that names the quest's folder or test file (a note, an include chain, a "requested here") keeps the blame with the quest, so an engine error the learner caused is never refunded; a signature phrase on a line that names a quest file is the learner's own text and is ignored; quest paths are anchored on `Source/Dojo/`.

Review fix round 2: an error located in an engine header counts as outside only when it says a file is missing (`Cannot open include file`, C1083, `file not found`, `No such file or directory`); any other engine-header error, which MSVC prints without an include chain, stays with the quest. An error only in a quest that is not an earlier quest (a later or dependent one) stays with the quest too. Signatures for failures that stop a build before compiling (Live Coding, Visual Studio or SDK, Xcode) are ignored when the output names the quest, and clang code excerpt lines never match a signature.

Review fix round 3: once the code compiled, signatures are matched only against the pipeline error, never against the kept warning lines, so a coincident warning cannot refund a test-time crash or loop. A support header (`Support/*.h`) is treated like an engine header (no include chain, so the learner's header may be the cause); only `Support/*.cpp` and template files count as outside. "Only" in rules 4 and 5 is strict: an unknown-located error line blocks them. In a sandbox build, any line naming a quest gets the open-or-reset message, and an engine error that is not a missing file gets the generic message.

### A1192 2026-10-27 The save notice rides on the environment status, not a new IPC method (M5.1a, m5-1 lead)
A restored-from-backup or refused save has to reach the learner. M5.1g: the reader builds the notice once and returns it on every read of the session (not only the first), so a refusal is never lost and a second window or refetch still sees it; the renderer hides it after the learner dismisses it, with a flag kept outside the shared query copy (M5.1i). Options: a new IPC method and channel; an optional `saveNotice` field on `EnvironmentStatus`, which the Title screen already reads. Chosen: the field. No new channel, the fake keeps compiling, and the Title and Settings screens show it through the existing `AppErrorView`. Reverse cost: move the field to its own method.

### A1191 2026-10-27 New friendly-failure fields are optional (M5.1a, m5-1 lead)
`RunResult.unrelatedFailure`, `SandboxSetupResult.failure`, `SandboxFolderChoice.blockers` and `EnvironmentStatus.engineProblem`, `toolchain` and `saveNotice` are all optional. Options: required fields (every producer and the fake change at once); optional fields (each system fills its own in its own task). Chosen: optional, so the renderer fake in `FakeDojoApi.ts` and every existing producer keep compiling, and a missing field means "nothing to say". Reverse cost: tighten to required once all producers fill them.

### A1190 2026-10-27 Two new error codes; every other failure reuses an existing code (M5.1a, m5-1 lead)
Friendly failures need names for "the build environment failed, not your code" and "this sandbox folder cannot work". Options: one code per cause (about ten); reuse `toolchain-missing`, `engine-not-found`, `engine-version-unsupported`, `sandbox-not-ready`, `sandbox-path-too-long` and add `build-environment-failed` and `sandbox-path-unsupported`. Chosen: the second. Codes stay few and the message and suggested action carry the cause. Reverse cost: splitting a code later is a search and replace.

### A1060 2026-10-26 W7 how the tracker's reset test observes a stale clock (builder, M3.2 Pack 3 World 6)
The reset mutation survived because a stale clock does nothing once the quest is no longer Active, so the state read the same either way. Options: restart the quest after the reset and check it is still Active past the old deadline; read the timer handle through the timer manager. Chosen: restart the quest, with its time limit set to zero first. Why: the handle is private to the tracker, so a learner's test cannot read it, and restarting a timed quest would replace the old clock under the same handle and hide the leak. With no limit on the restart, no new clock is set, so only a clock the reset left running can still fail the quest. The boss reference also renamed its local `bHidden` to `bHiddenOnBoard`, because `AActor` has a member of that name (MSVC error C4458).

### A874 2026-10-26 M3.2 fix round: where the deliberate failure is met (author-foundations, M3.2)
The Lead kept the tracker's deliberate failure (a completed timed quest failed by its uncleared clock) and moved its warning out of the brief and the timer card (Lead decision in the review). What replaces it: the card's early-finish question becomes a question on payloads being copied when the delegate is made, the tracker's second hint now explains that a clock keeps running whatever happened to its quest, and the tracker's new Parsons block practices starting a clock with a payload instead of `Complete` or `HandleTimeUp`, so it does not give away the fix. Options for that block: Complete, HandleTimeUp, the clock start. Chosen: the clock start, for the reason above. After the re-review, everything the learner sees before failing the tracker quest is neutral about the clock: its rubric line, the `timer-delegate` glossary text (shown on hover), the timer card's example, and the breakdown, which now asks the learner to say what happens to a quest's clock when the quest ends without giving the answer (Lead decision). Reverse cost: none.

### A873 2026-10-21 Pack 3 World 6 uses data assets before their concept quest is on main (author-foundations, M3.2)
The quest tracker reads quests from a data asset, and the concept quest that teaches data assets is in Pack 1 World 6, still in review. Options: define quests as structs on the tracker; use data assets and accept a link-only term for now. Chosen: data assets, because quests as assets is the point of the system (Type Object) and Pack 1 World 6 teaches the term earlier in course order once it lands. Until then the verifier shows three A72 warnings for `data-asset` in this world. Once Pack 1 World 6 lands, the warnings go away and `data-asset` gates the quests here that use it (A312, A72). Reverse cost: none.

### A872 2026-10-21 The Quest System boss: a quest day driven only by events (author-foundations, M3.2)
The research proposal lists several quests in parallel from data, chained unlocks, an ordered checkpoint race, timed failure, a secret quest, a quest log, and a finale listener. Options: one tracker that owns every quest's state with a director that only translates piece events into reports; one component per quest owning its own state. Chosen for the reference integration: the tracker, as a world subsystem, because resets and tests stay in one place. The breakdown quest carries both plans (tracker subsystem and component per quest); the boss's two plans are `cpp-director` and `blueprint-director`, both on the tracker. Pieces: an item that broadcasts its collection, gates and an ordered course (AddUniqueDynamic and RemoveAll), the tracker (per-quest clocks with payload timer delegates; the world's deliberate failure is a completed timed quest failed by its uncleared clock), and a giver whose label follows the tracker. Fixed numbers: 3 tools, 2 gems, 3 gates, 20 seconds, 200 to interact, 100 to a gate, a 0.1 second gate check; a giver wins a tie with an item and the earlier giver wins between givers. The quest log is a text board, because the sandbox has no widget and the research rule says not to assume UI. Reference game: Stardew Valley's help-wanted board. Reverse cost: medium.

### A871 2026-10-21 Pack 3 World 6 follows Pack 1 World 5 (author-foundations, M3.2)
Pack 3 World 6 is written first, as the M3 milestone item, and Pack 3 Worlds 1 to 5 do not exist yet. Options: no prerequisite; Pack 1 World 5's boss, the world that teaches delegates; the last Pack 2 quest. Chosen: `f5-boss-beacon-drill`, because this world applies delegates without teaching them again, so it should open after the learner has proved them in Pack 1. When Pack 3 World 5 exists, the first quest here should also require its last quest. Once Pack 1 World 6 lands, `data-asset` is taught earlier in course order and gates this world's quests that use it (A312, A72). Reverse cost: none.

### A870 2026-10-21 Pack 3 pack and World 6 shape (author-foundations, M3.2)
Pack 3 gets `content/packs/mechanics/pack.json` (id `mechanics`, order 3). Its World 6 folder is `06-delegates` so later worlds slot in before it; world id `delegates` (unique across packs, A73), quest ids `m6-<slug>`, classes `ADojoM6<Name>`, tests `Dojo.M6.<Quest>.<Case>`. Shape: the Quest System breakdown first (research rule 13: a system starts with its breakdown), four builds on the fade ladder that are the boss's pieces (worked item, faded ordered course, stub tracker, blank giver), one concept quest for the only new term (timer delegates with a payload, a new glossary entry `timer-delegate`), and the boss. Delegates, binding, and unbinding are not taught again; their terms point at Pack 1 World 5. The builds use the course's module 6 ideas only as concept coverage (items announcing pickups, plates and doors, quest givers and completion events) with original text, mechanics, and numbers; they do not follow the research's section 6.6 exercise list, because that list is a menu of standalone drills, while this world needed its builds to be the boss's pieces, so each build is shaped by the Quest System instead. The section 6.6 ideas not used (native delegates, return-value delegates, payload structs, logic gates) are left for a later pass. Checked for 6-word and 5-word overlaps against the GameDev.tv research: one phrase from our own author rules was reworded; the rest are engine macro names. Reverse cost: medium.
### A1050 2026-10-26 World 5's interface parameter is named Interactor, not Instigator (W6, builder)
The World 5 interface function `Interact(AActor* Instigator)` and its implementations failed to build on Windows with MSVC warning C4458 (treated as an error), because `Instigator` hides the member `AActor::Instigator`. Clang on macOS does not warn, so it was never caught, and since the verifier builds every solution together it stopped any whole-pack engine run. Options: suppress the warning in the sandbox project; rename the parameter. Chosen: rename to `Interactor` in the interface, the crate and lever implementations, the starters, the cards, the glossary example, and the hints, and add a hint telling learners not to use the old name. Suppressing the warning would teach a habit the learner should not copy. Reverse cost: a rename back.

### A722 2026-10-21 World 6 starts after World 5 (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 6)
World 6 was written while World 5 was in review, so its pieces use only Worlds 1 to 4 and its own builds, and it uses none of World 5's terms (Tick, timers, delegates). Options for its first quest's prerequisite: World 4's last build (`f4-fence`); World 5's last build (`f5-follower`). Chosen: `f5-follower`, now that World 5 is on main, so course order runs World 5 then World 6. Reverse cost: none.

### A721 2026-10-21 Range day: a boss run entirely from data (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 6)
The boss had to use every topic of the world. Options: a crafting bench reading recipes from a table; a wave-based shooting range; a quest board. Chosen: the range, because each topic has a visible job in it: waves and targets come from two data tables, weapons are data assets, levels follow an XP curve, the showcase loads the equipped weapon's mesh from a soft reference, and a wave naming a missing kind is logged in the director's own category and skipped. The tests build the data in code the way a designer builds assets, and a second test edits the target table between rounds to show the round follows the data. Fixed numbers stay in the director only where the rules need them (targets 150 apart, the upgrade at level 3, a reload press is not a shot); everything else is data. The pieces need no additions. Reference game: Zelda's shooting galleries. Reverse cost: low.

### A720 2026-10-21 Pack 1 World 6 shape (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 6)
World 6 uses the world id `data-and-tools`, unique across packs (A73), and the prefix `f6`. Six concept quests: data assets (with primary data assets); data tables; logging (UE_LOG, log categories, on-screen messages, console commands); curves; check and ensure; soft references and loading on demand. Four builds on the fade ladder: worked blaster reading a weapon data asset; faded spawner of targets from a data table, with its own log category for a missing row; stub level component reading XP per level from a curve, whose deliberate failure is levelling up once with an if when one big gain pays for several levels, and which reports a missing curve with ensure; blank showcase that loads a soft-referenced mesh on demand. Breakdown: a loot drop system, with a data table plan and a primary data asset plan. The product list for this world also names build tools (UBT, Build.cs, Live Coding); those stay for later because the Lead's brief names the six topics above. Reverse cost: medium.
### A1000 2026-10-26 The boss layout reader joins paths through the folder reader (W2, builder-unreal)
Four boss cases in `questRunService.test.ts` failed on Windows with "No reference solution for the earlier quest". `readBossSolution` joined paths with `node:path` while the file system it read from spelled them its own way: on Windows `node:path` gives backslashes, the test's fake (and `MacPlatformPaths`) use `/`, so the folder was never found. The real Windows app was not affected (`WindowsPlatformPaths` and `node:path` both use backslashes), so this was a latent mismatch that only the mixed test setup exposed. Options: change the fake to use `node:path`; make the test skip on Windows; add `join` to `BossFolderReader` so the reader spells paths like its file system. Chosen: the third. The app passes `PlatformPaths.join`, the verifier keeps `node:path`, and `tests/unreal/bossSolution.test.ts` uses a reader with a `>` separator, which fails before the fix on every OS. Reverse cost: low, one interface member and two call sites.
### A1022 2026-10-26 Node 24.22.1 on the Windows desktop comes from winget's OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS id (W0, Windows Lead)
Mohammad asked for `winget install OpenJS.NodeJS --version 24.22.1` if that version was available, otherwise the nodejs.org installer with its checksum checked. The `OpenJS.NodeJS` id lists 24.x only up to 24.10.0; 24.22.1 is listed under `OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS`, from the same publisher and the same nodejs.org MSI. Options: the LTS id; the nodejs.org installer by hand; the older 24.10.0. Chosen: the LTS id, which downloaded `node-v24.22.1-x64.msi` from nodejs.org and checked its hash ("Successfully verified installer hash"), replacing the 24.12.0 already installed. Reverse cost: none.

### A1021 2026-10-26 CI runs on push to main again (W0, Windows Lead; closes A80)
A80 paused the push trigger until the Windows unit tests passed. W1 (progress), W2 (prerequisite filler), W3 (review cleanup), and the `.gitattributes` in A1020 make `npm test` pass on this Windows machine, in both an LF checkout and a checkout made with `core.autocrlf=true` as on GitHub's windows-latest. Chosen: restore `push: branches: [main]` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` in the same change, as A80 asked. A79 (push in batches) still holds. Reverse cost: two lines.

### A1020 2026-10-26 A `.gitattributes` keeps LF line endings on every checkout (W0, Windows Lead)
GitHub's windows-latest runner checks out with `core.autocrlf=true`, so every text file arrived with CRLF and 8 tests in `tests/content/parseQuestFile.test.ts` failed: their fixtures are edited with `
` patterns that no longer match (the parser itself already splits on `?
`). Options: make every test CRLF-proof; normalize in the parser and the tests; tell git to keep LF. Chosen: `* text=auto eol=lf`, with images, fonts, and Unreal maps and assets marked binary, so a Windows clone has the same bytes as the Mac and the packaged content is LF everywhere. The index was already all LF, so no file changes. Reproduced and proven in a worktree made with `core.autocrlf=true`: 6 failing files before, `parseQuestFile` passing after. Reverse cost: delete one file.

### A980 2026-10-26 The World Map's stage path says when its layout has settled (M2.1f, builder-ui-core)
The locked-stage hover test flaked on GitHub CI (macos-latest) with "element is not attached / not stable". Cause: each stage path paints its first frame guessing six stages per row, because the card has not been measured yet. Once it is measured the rows regroup, and because each row is its own element, React remounts the stages into the new rows. That replaces the node the test had just located. Options: retry the scroll only; wait a fixed time; or expose the moment the layout settles. Chosen: both a signal and a retry. `StagePath` sets `data-measured="true"` once it knows its width. The test waits until no path is unmeasured, then scrolls and hovers inside the same retry, so a late resize cannot break it either. The attribute is not shown and changes nothing for the learner. Reverse cost: none.

### A973 2026-10-26 Hidden macOS e2e runs launch a background copy of Electron.app (M0.1c, builder-shell)
Even with A972, every launch spent its first tenth of a second as a regular app, before any app code runs, because Electron.app's Info.plist starts it that way; System Events lists it as visible then (never active, never the menu bar owner). Options: accept that; edit `node_modules/electron/dist/Electron.app` in place (lost on every install, and it would also change `npm run dev`); keep a separate copy with `LSUIElement` set and point Playwright's `executablePath` at it. Chosen: the copy. `e2e/support/backgroundElectron.ts` builds it once per Electron version under `node_modules/.cache/dojo-e2e/` (about 2 s, then reused), and `launchDojo()` uses it on macOS unless `DOJO_TEST_SHOW_WINDOW=1`. Electron's ad-hoc signature does not seal Info.plist, so no re-signing is needed. `-ApplePersistenceIgnoreState YES` was not used: it only skips window restoration and does not change activation. Reverse cost: none (delete the file and the cache folder).

### A972 2026-10-26 A hidden macOS test app is "prohibited", without dock.hide() (M0.1c, builder-shell)
Options: keep `app.dock.hide()` (policy "accessory"); set the activation policy to "prohibited"; both. Chosen: "prohibited" alone, as the first statement of `start()`: no Dock icon, no menu bar, and the app can never become active. Calling `dock.hide()` afterwards set the policy back to "accessory" (seen with a process watcher), so it was removed. Nothing in the e2e suite broke. Reverse cost: none.

### A954 2026-10-26 Follow-up timeouts say "Ask again"; the fake's first reflection times out (M3.4b fix round 1, builder-ui-quests; one line in ai-review's ReviewService)
A timed-out "Why?" or reflection fell back with the review error's suggested action, "Press Retry review to ask again", but the loot screen has no Retry review for a follow-up: the learner asks again from the same place. Options: reword in the renderer; reword where the reason is built. Chosen: where it is built. `ReviewService`'s failover options gain an optional `timeoutAction`, which follow-ups set to "Press Ask again to try once more. Your tests still count." (`FOLLOW_UP_TIMEOUT_ACTION`); code reviews keep the Retry review wording. This is a small edit in ai-review's file, named here; `tests/review/reviewExtras.test.ts` now checks the wording for both follow-ups. The fake's first reflection under Claude Code now times out the same way (a tests-only self-check with that reason and `retryReviewSuggested`), like its first review, so the e2e can show Ask again leading to an AI grade. Reverse cost: none.

### A953 2026-10-26 "Why?" can be asked again; the fake matches the real bridge under Tests only (M3.4b fix round 1, builder-ui-quests; Lead decision on the fake)
A fallback answer to "Why?" is never kept (A855) so that the next ask reaches a reviewer, but the button hid after the first answer. Now it stays as "Ask again (free)"; a kept AI answer comes back at once, still free. And under Tests only the real `ProviderSelector` gives no `fallbackReason`, while the fake added one to the review and the reflection: the fake now leaves it out and words the reflection self-check as main does ("Self-check: reread your sentence against the question and your code."). `ProviderSelector` is unchanged (Lead decision). Reverse cost: none.

### A952 2026-10-26 "Why?" stays as it was; the fake falls back like the review service under Tests only (M3.4b, builder-ui-quests)
`explainFinding` answers text whether an AI or a fallback answered (a fallback is the finding's detail plus why, never kept, A855), so the finding row shows any answer the same way and needs no new state; a second ask of a kept answer simply returns at once. The fake now matches main when Settings' review provider is Tests only: the code review is tests-only with the "set up a reviewer" reason, "Why?" answers the detail plus that reason, and the reflection answers a self-check with `fallbackReason`; neither is kept. Before, the fake answered every follow-up as an AI. Reverse cost: none.
Fix round 1: the fake no longer adds a fallback reason under Tests only, and "Why?" can be asked again (A953, A954).

### A951 2026-10-26 The reflection box shows the kept answer and lets the learner revise it (M3.4b, builder-ui-quests)
Options: keep the box locked after one answer; show the kept answer and allow a new sentence. Chosen: the second, as A852 grades a new sentence and replaces the kept one. On load the box is prefilled once from `PlayerProgress.attempts[].reflection` with its grade (the learner's typing is never overwritten). The grade shows while the box holds the graded sentence; editing hides it and offers "Send the new answer". An AI grade reads as before with the reviewer's name; a tests-only answer reads "Not graded: compare your sentence with the note" with its reason, and offers "Ask again" only when `retryReviewSuggested` (a timeout). Reverse cost: none.

### A950 2026-10-26 The loot screen's alternative comes from getQuestAlternative (M3.4b, builder-ui-quests)
The loot screen read `quest.alternative` and `quest.alternativeNote`, which the catalog sends before a pass. Now `AlternativePanel` asks `getQuestAlternative(questId)` after a pass (cached per quest as `alternative:<id>`): a placeholder while it loads, nothing for `null` or `alternative-locked`, the error view with a retry for anything else, and otherwise the markdown with the pattern chips and, when an AI reviewer wrote one, "Your reviewer suggests: ..." (A850). Retry review refreshes it, since a new AI review can bring a hint. No screen reads `alternative` or `alternativeNote` from the quest any more, so content-engine can stop sending them (A850 follow-up). Reverse cost: none.

### A971 2026-10-26 The app fits its starting size to the screen itself (M0.1b, builder-shell)
Options: keep asking for 1440x900 and let the OS shrink it; compute the size from the primary screen's work area before creating the window; loosen the smoke test for hidden windows. Chosen: compute it, 1440x900 shrunk to the work area and never below 1100x700, the same rule as `expectedWindowSize` in the e2e harness (A117). macOS only shrinks a window when it is shown, so a hidden test window stayed 900 tall on an 841-point work area and the smoke test failed; Windows never shrank it at all. Now a shown window, a hidden one, and a Windows window all start at the same fitted size. Reverse cost: none.

### A970 2026-10-26 E2e windows open hidden; DOJO_TEST_SHOW_WINDOW=1 brings them back (M0.1b, builder-shell, under A77)
Every `_electron` launch used to open a full-size window that came to the front, so Mohammad could not use the Mac while sessions ran e2e. Options: move the window off screen; hide it with a test-only switch; run e2e headless in a separate desktop session. Chosen: a switch read by the main process. With `DOJO_TEST_HIDDEN_WINDOW=1` (set by `e2e/support/launchDojo.ts` on every launch) the window is created with `show: false`, `paintWhenInitiallyHidden: true`, `focusable: false`, and `backgroundThrottling: false`, is never shown or focused, and on macOS `app.dock.hide()` runs before `whenReady`. `DOJO_TEST_SHOW_WINDOW=1` overrides it for someone who wants to watch. Packaged builds ignore both, so a stray variable can never make an installed app invisible. Proof on the M5 Air: during a full run, a window-list sampler (every 100 ms, 1,100 samples) saw no app window on screen and the front app never changed; with the opt-out it saw the window at once. Not tried on Windows, where there is no Dock and `focusable: false` behaves differently. Reverse cost: none.

(plural: "Compare them with the model answers; they stay in your drills until a reviewer can grade them."). The Tests-only e2e now answers every graded item right from the content's answer keys (matched by prompt) and asserts the score reads N/N, no due line, and the self-checked line, which is the M2.4 bug's case. Reverse cost: none.

The fake paid XP, the streak, and the daily credit for any finished drill, and tested a world out when 0 of 0 graded answers were right. When every item of a drill was self-checked (Tests only and an all-explain drill), the score would read "0/0". Options: hide the headline; show "0/0"; show the count explained. Chosen: the headline becomes the count with the word "explained" (for example "2 explained"), with no due line and the self-checked line below. Reverse cost: none.

### A818 2026-10-21 The stored-attempt path is tested end to end without a reload (M2.2b fix round 2, Lead request)
The fake's state lives in the page, so a reload cannot show A814. A true restart is covered by M2.4's real-bridge relaunch. Reverse cost: none.

### A817 2026-10-21 Next is known once and focused once (M2.2b fix round 2, builder-ui-quests)
A815 tied Next to `!availability.isLoading`, so every window-focus refetch disabled Next for a moment and then focused it again, pulling the keyboard away from Improve or the reflection box. Now Next becomes known after the first availability fetch since the loot screen opened (the same pattern as `BuildQuestView`'s fresh read) and stays known; background refetches neither disable it nor move focus. It is focused a single time, behind a ref guard. The e2e focuses Improve, sends a window focus event, and expects Improve to keep focus. Reverse cost: none.

A811's counts ("3 reviews tomorrow") claimed more than the data holds: `nextDueDates` carries only the three soonest times. Now the line reads "Next reviews due now, tomorrow and in 5 days": each distinct day once, in time order, no counts, keeping the "Next reviews" label so it never reads like the "Next: <quest>" button. Any past or current time reads "due now" (`lib/dueGroups.ts`; the shared `formatDue` used by the Studio is unchanged). The fake now answers `nextDueDates` as the progress service does, the three soonest schedule times with past ones kept. Reverse cost: none.

### A815 2026-10-21 Next takes focus once it is known (M2.2b fix round 1, builder-ui-quests)
A812 disabled Next until availability loads, so its `autoFocus` landed on nothing and Enter no longer moved on. Now a ref and an effect focus Next when it becomes known (and only then, so Retry review or Why? keep focus after they run). Reverse cost: none.

### A814 2026-10-21 The last Submit survives a reload: read from the stored attempt (M2.2b fix round 1, builder-ui-quests)
A810 took the last Submit only from the in-memory loot hand-off, so a reload or restart lost the compiler output again. ### A813 2026-10-21 A build quest opens from a fresh read of the save (M2.2b, builder-ui-quests)
The build workspace took its starting files from the renderer's cached progress, which can be older than a draft saved since, and a Submit then sent the older files. Options: read the draft again at Submit time (a second source of truth beside the editor); open the workspace only from a fresh read. Chosen: the second. `BuildQuestView` waits, once, for the progress refetch that opening a quest starts, so the editor starts from the draft as saved now; from then on the editor is what Submit sends (its own autosave keeps the save in step). Next went to the next quest by order even when it was locked. ### A810 2026-10-21 The run column shows the last Submit's result (M2.2b, builder-ui-quests)
After a Submit that did not compile, Improve showed only "not passed yet, score 0", and the loot screen sent the learner to compiler output that was not there.
