### A682 2026-10-21 Beacon drill: the director compares lever states on a shared event, and two piece additions (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 5)
The lever's OnPulled carries only the new position, so a listener bound to several levers cannot tell which one fired. Options: add a second delegate that passes the lever; bind a separate listener object per lever; compare every lever with its last known position on any pull. Chosen: compare, which needs no change to the lever's event and reads plainly in both plans. The pieces need two additions: the lever implements the interactable interface (lighting itself when dark and its own cooldown is ready), and the cooldown gains Cancel for a clean round start. The director owns the 4 second cooldown length and applies it to every lever's cooldown, so the number is pinned in one place. A round start puts lit levers out while the round is stopped, so the director's own handler ignores those pulls and starts no cooldown, then cancels every cooldown; a separate quiet flag was tried and dropped because a mutation showed it changed nothing. The keeper rides on the player's pawn, as in World 4 (A620). Reference game: the torch rooms of Zelda's dungeons. Reverse cost: low.

### A681 2026-10-21 Pack 1 World 5 shape (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 5)
World 5 uses the world id `communication` (A68). Six concept quests: delegates; binding and unbinding; interfaces; timers; Tick and DeltaTime; choosing a channel. Four builds on the fade ladder: worked lever and lamps (a dynamic multicast delegate; the Observer pattern named in the Alternative), faded interactable crate and prodder (an interface called through Execute_, reusing the World 1 dummy as a non-implementer), stub cooldown component (a timer; the world's deliberate failure is a start during the cooldown that restarts it, the dash breakdown's rule from World 1), blank follower (Tick scaled by DeltaSeconds, tested at 30 and 120 frames per second, ticking only while it has a target). Breakdown: a quest step tracker, with a tracker-listens plan and a beacons-report plan. Glossary first-quest links move to the earliest quest in course order that teaches each term (A379, A476): the delegate terms to `f5-delegates` and `f5-binding-and-unbinding`, Tick, DeltaTime, and tick groups to `f5-tick-and-deltatime`, EndPlay to `f2-destroy-and-lifetime`, and PrimaryActorTick to `f4-components`; they had pointed at Pack 2 quests, which come later. Reverse cost: medium.

### A646 2026-10-21 Bugs the flow found are marked per check, not on the whole test (M2.4, tester)
The flow should merge before its bugs are fixed (M1.6g, M2.2b, M2.1e). Options: `test.fail()` on the whole test, which expects the 35-minute run to fail and so hides every other regression in it; skip the affected checks; a per-check wrapper, `expectKnownFailure(task, description, check)`, that records a "known failure" annotation naming the task while the bug stands and fails the run once the check passes. Chosen: the wrapper. Every other expectation stays strict, and a fixed bug cannot go unnoticed. Reverse cost: remove the wrapper around each check after its fix merges, which the flow itself asks for.

### A645 2026-10-21 The harness can restart the app on the same data (M2.4, tester)
The flow must prove progress survives a restart. Options: a second fixture; let `launchDojo` take an existing userData folder and add `closeDojoKeepingData`. Chosen: the second, two small additions to `e2e/support/launchDojo.ts`; every other spec still gets a fresh folder per test. Reverse cost: none.

### A644 2026-10-21 The learner flow is recorded as a sequence of JPG proofs, not a GIF (M2.4, tester)
Options: a GIF (Playwright cannot record an Electron window to video; it would need a screen recorder or stitching screenshots with a tool that is not installed); numbered JPG proofs. Chosen: numbered `docs/iterations/m2-4-NN-<step>.jpg` at 1280x800 CSS pixels, written only by a passing run with `SAVE_PROOF=1`, embedded in order in the iterations entry. Reverse cost: stitch the same pictures into a GIF later.

### A643 2026-10-21 The flow never opens the windowed Unreal Editor (M2.4, tester)
"Open the editor" (first-run), "Open Editor" (build quest), and "See it run" (loot) all launch UnrealEditor and wait up to 3 minutes. Options: click them; skip them. Chosen: the first-run editor step uses its own "Skip for now", and the other two are not clicked, so the run stays headless and never steals the screen. Reverse cost: none.

### A642 2026-10-21 One build is solved by typing; the builds that only unlock the breakdown are solved from their reference solutions (M2.4, tester)
The breakdown sits behind builds 7, 9, and 10. Options: type every solution in Monaco (slow and fragile, Monaco re-indents pasted lines); save each reference solution (`tools/solutions/<quest>/`) as the learner's draft through the bridge, reload so the renderer's progress cache sees it, then Submit. Chosen: the training dummy shows the real learner path (Run fails, buy a hint, type both fixes, Run passes, Submit), and the other three use their reference solutions, which also proves every World 1 reference solution passes through the app's own pipeline. Reverse cost: none.

### A641 2026-10-21 The flow answers questions from the content's own answer key, matched by prompt (M2.4, tester)
Real drills pick items at random and choices are shuffled. Options: hard-code answers per quest; read every answer from `content/` (`tools/qa/printAnswerKey.ts`, run with tsx) and match the shown prompt by its words. Chosen: the answer key, so the flow keeps working as content changes. Multiple-choice answers use the choice's input value (the authored index), not its text. Reverse cost: none.

### A640 2026-10-21 The real-engine learner flow is an opt-in spec that locks the engine only around Unreal steps (M2.4, tester)
It needs Unreal Engine and takes many minutes. Options: part of `npm run test:e2e`; a separate script; the same Playwright suite, skipped unless `DOJO_E2E_ENGINE=1`. Chosen: `e2e/learner-flow.spec.ts`, skipped by default, so CI and the normal suite never run it. On a machine shared by several sessions, `DOJO_E2E_ENGINE_LOCK` names the lock folder; it is taken with mkdir only around the sandbox build, each Run, and each Submit, so other sessions can use the engine in between. `DOJO_E2E_ENGINE_PATH` picks the engine (default `~/Applications/UE_5.8`). Reverse cost: none.



### A680 2026-10-21 Pack 1 term coverage under A72 (author-foundations)
Main's verifier now warns when a quest uses a term no earlier concept quest teaches. Options per term: teach it in an earlier concept card where it fits, or drop it from a quest where it is incidental. Chosen: UE_LOG and CreateDefaultSubobject are taught in World 1's UObject and actor card; TArray in World 1's Cast and IsA card (its example already walks an array); RegisterComponent in World 2's creating objects card (its text already explained it); PrimaryActorTick in World 4's components card; the Character Movement Component and LaunchCharacter in World 4's pawns and controllers card. FMath is dropped from World 3's score board, where it was incidental. Left as they are: the World 1 dash breakdown's terms (character, movement, input, timers, delegates), which the brief places in World 1 on purpose and which only link there, and object-pool, left to the patterns pack's Object Pool world. Glossary first-quest links for ue-log, the movement component, LaunchCharacter, and RegisterComponent now point to those concept quests. Reverse cost: low.

### A612 2026-10-21 Engine integration test files run one at a time (builder-unreal, M1.4c)
Each engine test file creates its own sandbox, builds it, and may open an editor; vitest runs test files in parallel by default, so running the folder at once builds several sandboxes together and the runs fail or time out. Options: force serial runs in `vitest.config.ts` (slows every unit test run); an npm script with the flag (needs a `package.json` change); document it. Chosen: document it in `docs/systems/unreal-pipeline.md` (one file at a time, or `--no-file-parallelism`, under the engine lock) and correct the M1.4b entry's wording. There is no npm script for these checks, so none needed changing. Reverse cost: none.

### A611 2026-10-21 `openEditor` restarts an editor that is on another engine (builder-unreal, M1.4c)
After the engine in Settings changes, an editor still open on the same sandbox runs the old engine, and the fast loop would test with it. Chosen: the open editor is reused only when its engine folder, its project, and the build record all match (paths compared with the platform's `pathKey`); otherwise `openEditor` restarts it on the engine in Settings. Reverse cost: none.

### A610 2026-10-21 A reset never fails because the Unreal side is unavailable (builder-unreal, M1.4c)
A534 caught errors from removing the sandbox files, but building the quest run service can itself throw (an unsupported platform) before that catch applied, so a reset whose progress was already reset answered an error. Chosen: the whole sandbox step is inside one try and only logs a warning. Reverse cost: none.


### A620 2026-10-21 The course setup keeper rides on the player's pawn (Lead, Pack 1 World 4 fix round 1)
From the World 4 review: the keeper is a plain carrier actor and nothing moved it, so the boss could not be played; the tests hid this by teleporting it. Decided by the Lead: in BeginPlay the director (and the level Blueprint in plan 2) attaches the keeper to the player's pawn with AttachToActor, snapped on, so it walks with the default pawn and reuses the world's attach lesson with no piece addition. The director also takes an optional Follow Target, which the tests use because the test world has no player controller; the tests now move an ADefaultPawn instead of the keeper. Reverse cost: low.

### A562 2026-10-21 Course setup boss: no additions, a loose-only registry, and a fixed registration order (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 4)
The boss needed a way to keep placed dummies out of pickup and to make round 2 break ties exactly like round 1. Options: add a "placed" flag to the registry or the hit counter; use the registry as the list of loose dummies only. Chosen: placing a dummy unregisters it, and every round start unregisters all dummies and registers them again in the designer's order, so the registry's first-registered tie rule gives the same pickup every round. No piece needs an addition. Ties between free posts go to the lower post. Reference game: Moving Out. Reverse cost: low.

### A561 2026-10-21 The fence builds posts in a BlueprintCallable Rebuild that OnConstruction calls (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 4)
`RerunConstructionScripts` is editor-only in UE 5.8 (`#if WITH_EDITOR` in Actor.h), so tests and gameplay cannot rely on it. Options: test only through spawning; expose the layout as its own function. Chosen: `Rebuild` is BlueprintCallable and OnConstruction calls it, so the editor, a spawn, and gameplay all share one path, and the test calls Rebuild after changing the numbers. Reverse cost: low.

### A560 2026-10-21 Pack 1 World 4 shape (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 4)
World 4, "The gameplay framework", keeps the training yard. Six concept quests: pawns, characters, controllers, and possession; game mode, game state, player state, and game instance; actor, scene, and primitive components; the actor lifecycle; worlds, levels, and GameplayStatics; subsystems. The test world has no game mode or player, so the build quests teach the framework parts it can host: worked hit counter (an actor component added to World 1 classes; Component pattern named in its alternative), faded carrier (a scene component mount and attachment, carrying the World 1 dummy), stub registry (a world subsystem plus a self-registering component; the world's deliberate failure is forgetting to unregister in EndPlay), blank fence (posts made with NewObject and RegisterComponent in OnConstruction). Possession, game modes, states, and travel are taught in concept quests and in the respawn loop breakdown. New glossary entry: `register-component`. Reverse cost: medium.

### A506 2026-10-21 League night boss: the director feeds the sentry, and two Clear additions (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 3)
The sentry aims at whatever is in its Targets list, but the rack keeps its pool private. Options: give the rack a getter for its pool; have the director add each target to the sentry's list when it is lent and remove it when it goes back. Chosen: the director does it, so the sentry never aims at a resting target and the rack needs no change. The roster and the score board each gain `Clear` so R can start a new league clean, and (fix round 1, the Lead's decision) the sentry gains BlueprintCallable `AddTarget` and `RemoveTarget`, because its Targets list is only EditAnywhere and the Blueprint plan could not otherwise feed it; extended copies of all three ship in the boss's solution folder (A340), and the sentry build quest's own interface is unchanged. Reference game: the bowling in Wii Sports. Reverse cost: low.

### A505 2026-10-21 The announcer's test pins the culture to English (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 3)
FText formats numbers for the current culture, so "1,200" depends on the machine. Options: avoid numbers above 999 in the test; pin the culture inside the test and restore it after. Chosen: pin it to `en` with a culture state snapshot, so the grouping the quest teaches is asserted the same way everywhere. Reverse cost: low.

### A504 2026-10-21 Pack 1 World 3 shape (author-foundations, Pack 1 World 3)
World 3, "Containers and strings", continues the training yard with a league. Six concept quests: TArray; TMap, TSet, and TQueue; FString, FName, FText, and TEXT; FVector and FMath; FRotator and FQuat; FTransform. Four builds on the fade ladder, reusing World 1 and World 2 on purpose: worked score board (TArray history with a limit, reading the World 1 score keeper; the world's deliberate failure is an average computed with whole-number division), faded roster (TMap keyed by FName, with case-insensitive names), stub announcer (FText::Format with named arguments and culture-formatted numbers), blank sentry (nearest raised World 1 target by squared distance, yaw with Atan2). Breakdown: a league table, with a map-then-sort plan and a sorted-array-plus-index plan. Boss: league night, using the World 2 rack. TSet, TQueue, FQuat, FTransform, and the Kismet math library are taught in concept quests and drills only. Reverse cost: medium.

### A535 2026-10-21 The wiring's integration test drives the real handlers and a stand-in for Electron's app (builder-unreal, M1.4b)
Options: a Playwright run of the packaged app against a real engine; the real `createHandlers` and services in vitest with a fake `app` that replays `before-quit`. Chosen: the second, like M1.3b's `runQuest` test. It exercises the same handler code, services, and quit sequence; what it does not cover is Electron itself emitting `before-quit`. A Playwright engine run would need the app's userData seeded with an engine and sandbox, which is a larger change to the e2e setup. Reverse cost: medium.

### A534 2026-10-21 `resetQuest` also removes the quest's sandbox files (builder-unreal, M1.4b)
The IPC contract already said a reset removes the quest's files from the sandbox, but nothing did. Options: leave the files (the next run rewrites them); remove them. Chosen: remove the quest folder and its test file after the progress reset, and record it in the build ledger, so the fast loop does not trust an editor that loaded them. A sandbox that cannot be reached (no sandbox yet, an unsupported platform) only logs a warning: the reset itself never fails because of it. Reverse cost: none.

### A533 2026-10-21 `openEditor(questId)` opens the sandbox; the quest id is ignored for now (builder-unreal, M1.4b)
The contract mentions opening "that quest's map for See it run", but no quest ships a map. Options: refuse a quest id; accept it and open the sandbox. Chosen: accept and open the sandbox, so the renderer can pass it today and gain the map later without a contract change. Reverse cost: none.

### A532 2026-10-21 `openEditor` opens a windowless editor when the editor window cannot open (builder-unreal, M1.4b)
On this Mac the editor with a window quits at startup because the Metal toolchain is missing; the toolchain check reports that as a non-blocking problem. Options: open with a window anyway and fail; refuse and show the fix; open windowless. Chosen: windowless (`-NullRHI`) whenever the toolchain check lists a non-blocking problem, so the fast loop still works; the toolchain check's message already tells the learner how to get the window. Today the only non-blocking problem is the Metal toolchain. Reverse cost: low.

### A531 2026-10-21 Sandbox create and rebuild close an editor on that sandbox first and record the build (builder-unreal, M1.4b)
`SandboxProjectService` gains optional `buildHooks`. Before a rebuild deletes `Binaries` and the other build folders (and before create's first build), an editor the app opened on that sandbox is closed: a rebuild would delete the modules it loaded, and on Windows UBT cannot rebuild them while it runs. After a successful build the ledger records it, which makes any other open editor stale. Options: refuse to rebuild while the editor is open; close it. Chosen: close it; the learner reopens it with Open editor. Reverse cost: low.

### A530 2026-10-21 One quit sequence for runs and the editor (builder-unreal, M1.4b)
The app had one `before-quit` hook for the run registry (5 seconds) and M1.4 added one for the editor (15 seconds); each one's `quit()` would re-fire the other, which then let the quit through early. Options: keep two hooks and make them aware of each other; one sequence. Chosen: `stopEngineWorkBeforeQuit` replaces both: cancel runs first (a run may be using the editor), then dispose the launcher, holding every quit attempt until both are done. It never builds the Unreal services just to quit. Reverse cost: low.

### A286 2026-10-21 Engine smoke builds the cmd.exe command line by hand for Build.bat (QA harness fix round 1, tester) (superseded by A63)
Superseded 2026-10-21: after M1.3 merged, the smoke calls `SandboxProjectService`, `UnrealBuilder`, and `AutomationTestRunner`; `tools/qa/windowsCommandLine.ts`, its test, and the hand-written tree kill were deleted. The text below is kept as history.

Found in review: passing `Build.bat` and its arguments to `cmd.exe /d /s /c` as separate arguments lets Node quote each one for a normal program, which cmd.exe then splits again at the first space, so the default `C:\Program Files\Epic Games\...` path breaks. Options: `shell: true` with pre-quoted arguments (what `NodeProcessRunner` does); build the line ourselves. Chosen: build it ourselves with the same quoting (each part in double quotes, inner quotes doubled, the whole line in one more pair) and spawn `cmd.exe` with `windowsVerbatimArguments: true`, which is exactly what Node's shell mode does, without importing app code into `tools/qa`. A timeout kills the tree with `taskkill /T /F`. Unit tested; unverified on a real Windows machine. Superseded by the Lead's rule that only `src/main/services/unreal/` spawns Unreal: this code stays until M1.3 merges, then the smoke switches to `SandboxProjectService`, `UnrealBuilder`, and `AutomationTestRunner` and `tools/qa/windowsCommandLine.ts` and its test are removed. Reverse cost: low.

### A285 2026-10-21 Engine smoke passes on the report, and requires the sample test by name (QA harness, tester)
Options: trust the editor's exit code; read `index.json` and require no failures; also require `Dojo.Sample.Health.TakesDamage` to be present and passed. Chosen: the last. The pipeline spec already says the report decides (exit code 3 on a failed assertion, and a filter that matches nothing can still exit 0 with an empty report). "Succeeded with warnings" counts as passed, as in the spec. Reverse cost: one condition.

### A284 2026-10-21 Engine smoke prepares the sandbox the way SandboxProjectService will, without importing it (QA harness, tester) (superseded by A63)
Superseded 2026-10-21: the smoke now calls `SandboxProjectService.create` itself, so the sandbox is prepared by the app's own code.

SandboxProjectService is not on main yet. Options: wait for it; copy the template and write `EngineAssociation` and the plugin list in the script. Chosen: the script does it, following A41: `EngineAssociation` is the engine's major.minor (for example `5.8`), and the three 5.8 plugins are removed for older engines. It reuses `EngineLocator`, `createUnrealPlatform`, and `PlatformPaths` from `src/main/services/unreal/` read-only, so the paths match the app's. When SandboxProjectService lands, the script should call it instead. Reverse cost: low.

### A283 2026-10-21 Dialog stubs replace methods on Electron's `dialog` object from the test (QA harness, tester)
Options: an environment variable the app reads to skip dialogs (needs `src/` changes, and test code in the app); replacing `dialog.showOpenDialog`, `showOpenDialogSync`, `showMessageBox`, `showMessageBoxSync` through `electronApp.evaluate`, as research section 7 suggests. Chosen: the second. The app imports the same `dialog` object, so its calls hit the stub; nothing ships in the app. Save dialogs are not stubbed yet; add them when a screen uses one. Reverse cost: none.

### A282 2026-10-21 One failure screenshot per failing test; proof pictures copied only after a pass (QA harness, tester)
Options: keep each spec's own screenshot code (M0.6 smoke saved `window.png` in a `finally`; ui-core copied proofs the moment they were taken, even if a later check failed); move both into the harness. Chosen: the harness. The `dojo` fixture saves `failure.png` for any failing test, including tests that take no screenshots of their own; `ProofCamera` queues proof copies and makes them only when the test passed with `SAVE_PROOF=1`. This replaces the M0.6 smoke's "window.png on failure" with `failure.png`. Reverse cost: low.

### A281 2026-10-21 Every e2e test gets its own app launch (QA harness, tester)
The brief asks for a fresh userData folder per test, which needs a fresh launch per test. Options: one launch per spec file (the ui-core spec's old `beforeAll`); one per test. Chosen: per test, through a Playwright fixture. The ui-core spec goes from one launch to three; each launch costs about 0.4 s here, and the whole suite still runs in under 5 s. Tests can no longer leak state into each other. Reverse cost: a worker-scoped fixture.

### A280 2026-10-21 Fresh userData through Chromium's `--user-data-dir` switch (QA harness, tester)
Options: an app flag or environment variable that calls `app.setPath("userData", ...)` (needs `src/`, which this task may not touch); override `APPDATA` or `HOME` (works on Windows only, macOS ignores `HOME` for Application Support); pass `--user-data-dir=<temp folder>`. Chosen: the switch. Electron 44 points `app.getPath("userData")` at it, which `e2e/harness.spec.ts` checks. The folder path is resolved with `realpath` because the macOS temp folder is a symlink and Electron reports the resolved path. Unverified on Windows. Reverse cost: one line.

(The Lead appends here. Each entry: date, decision, options considered, why, what it would cost to reverse.)

### A839 2026-10-21 World 9 was built in this session's own worktree, on main at 66f98b6 (author-patterns, M4.P2.9)
As in A769 and A789: the session's file tools write only inside its own worktree, so `m4-p2-world9` is checked out there. It was started from main at d15462a and rebased onto main at 66f98b6, which includes 9545c18 as the Lead asked; nothing in between touches Pack 2 World 9. Reverse cost: none.

### A838 2026-10-21 Glossary and card changes for Component (author-patterns, M4.P2.9)
Added `findcomponentbyclass` (components; aliases GetComponentByClass and FindComponentByClass<T>; `AActor::FindComponentByClass` and `GetComponentByClass` confirmed in 5.8's `Actor.h`), `blueprint-function-library` (framework), and `initialize-component` (components; aliases bWantsInitializeComponent and OnRegister; the order OnRegister, InitializeComponent, BeginPlay confirmed in 5.8's `Actor.cpp` and `ActorComponent.cpp`, including that InitializeComponents runs only once actors are initialized for play, so not in the editor). The component card was audited item by item for the source chapter's examples. Rewritten: `problem` (a player class with input, physics, animation, and sound, the chapter's own split, is now the arena prop with flags and the crate that needs two parents), `unrealForm` (adds the two ways parts are made and the startup order), `whenNot` (adds when a part should be an actor), and `component-scenario-2` (dropped the line count and input, now an arena champion and a wall turret). Added `component-recall-4` (InitializeComponent before BeginPlay) and `component-scenario-3` (gladiators, mounts, and carts sharing stamina). Use cases: `p9-arena-parts`, `p9-sprint-parts`. Reverse cost: low.

### A837 2026-10-21 The Component boss: the prop gauntlet, with a compiled integration and three proven mutations (author-patterns, M4.P2.9)
A68 makes this boss half of the Pack 4 gate, so it follows A67 in full. Options: a boss that adds new parts to the pieces (needs A215 overrides); a boss that only assembles and listens; a boss around the gladiator alone. Chosen: the master assembles every prop at runtime from designer recipes (a scene root with a name tag, then health and loot parts as the recipe says, each filled in before it registers), strikes through `FindComponentByClass` on the target and on the champion, listens to every part's delegate, and gives a champion without stamina one. The pieces are `p9-arena-parts` and `p9-sprint-parts`, unchanged, so no override folder; the refactored gladiator is a prerequisite and compiles-against quest (refactor quests cannot be pieces). Reference integration `ADojoP9GauntletMaster` in `tools/solutions/p9-boss-prop-gauntlet/`, with three tests: two full rounds that play alike (with R ignored mid-round and round 1's props destroyed), a 45 second time-up with a full new round after R, and a plain actor as champion. Mutations each fail the two-round test on 5.8.3: no stamina refill at a round start, no reset of the broken count, and strikes that do not skip broken props (see M4.P2.9 in iterations). The Blueprint plan uses Add Component by Class with Deferred Finish and Finish Add Component, both confirmed in 5.8's `Actor.h`. Reverse cost: low.

### A836 2026-10-21 World 9's deliberate failure: a sibling looked up in BeginPlay (author-patterns, M4.P2.9)
Options: forgetting to turn on the stamina part's tick (a setting, given in the stub's constructor); casting the owner to one class (taught openly in the card); the sprint caching its stamina sibling in BeginPlay. Chosen: the cache. On an actor already playing, registering a part runs its BeginPlay inside that call, so a sprint added before the stamina finds nothing; `PartsAddedInEitherOrder` fails and the rest pass. The worked quest's marked line says that registering runs BeginPlay at once, about setting numbers first; the card gives the startup order without the consequence for siblings, and the stub's member comment does not say when it is found. Checked on 5.8.3 with the lookup moved to BeginPlay. Reverse cost: low.

### A835 2026-10-21 The refactor checks the parts by reflection and keeps the gladiator's functions (author-patterns, M4.P2.9)
`p9-gladiator-refactor` compiles against `p9-arena-parts` and `p9-sprint-parts`, both prerequisites (A268). The starter is an `ACharacter` with its own health, stamina, and sprint fields run in its own Tick; its public functions stay in the solution and pass calls to the parts, so `SprintTiresTheGladiator` and `DownedGladiatorStops` pass on both. `BuiltFromParts` checks the three parts exist and are default subobjects (`IsDefaultSubobject`) and that the gladiator declares no float or double property of its own (`TFieldIterator` excluding the parent), so it fails on the starter. The one cross-part rule, going down stops the sprint, stays on the gladiator through `OnDepleted`. Reverse cost: low.

### A834 2026-10-21 One ticking part owns stamina; the sprint only sets a rate (author-patterns, M4.P2.9)
Options: the sprint ticks and spends stamina each frame; the stamina part ticks and the sprint sets a drain rate. Two parts that tick and change the same number make the result depend on their tick order, which siblings do not promise. Chosen: the stamina part is the only part that ticks; `SetDrainPerSecond` pauses regeneration, and `OnExhausted` stops the sprint. The sprint also changes a `UCharacterMovementComponent`'s `MaxWalkSpeed` when the owner has one, so the same part works on a test actor and on a character. Reverse cost: low.

### A833 2026-10-21 Two ways to assemble: a function library at runtime and CreateDefaultSubobject in a constructor (author-patterns, M4.P2.9)
The worked quest assembles props at runtime through `UDojoP9PropKit`, a `UBlueprintFunctionLibrary` (NewObject, fill in, AddInstanceComponent, RegisterComponent, then wire loot to its own health); the refactor and the card show the constructor form; the boss uses runtime assembly from data. Kinds of prop have no class anywhere in the world. Reverse cost: low.

### A832 2026-10-21 Component arc, built away from the source's example (author-patterns, M4.P2.9)
Not used in briefs, cards, choices, distractors, or the card's items: a character split into input, physics, and graphics parts; an AI input part swapped in for a demo; the chapter's baker; or decorations, zones, and props sharing rendering and collision. Arc, all in the arena: `p9-one-prop-too-many-jobs` (concept, no pattern name: a crate, a statue, and a chest that cannot share a parent), `p9-arena-parts` (worked: health and loot parts and a kit; the learner writes MakeCrate), `p9-component-card` (the card: parts, the two ways to make them, the startup order, and four ways parts talk), `p9-sprint-parts` (stub: stamina and sprint parts), `p9-gladiator-refactor`, `p9-component-when-not` (including when a part should be an actor), and the boss (A837). No breakdown, since the boss carries the planning. Reverse cost: low.

### A831 2026-10-21 Pack 2 World 9 ids (author-patterns, M4.P2.9)
Folder `09-component`, world id `component` exactly, as the Pack 4 gate names it (A68; `PACK_GATES` in `rules.ts`); unique across packs (A73). Quest ids `p9-<slug>`, classes `UDojoP9<Name>Component`, `ADojoP9<Name>`, struct `FDojoP9PropRecipe`, tests `Dojo.P9.<Quest>.<Case>`. No enums in this world. With this world's boss the verifier's pack gate check finds both gate worlds. Reverse cost: medium once learners have progress saved.

### A830 2026-10-21 Decision block A830 to A849 from the Lead (author-patterns, M4.P2.9)
Assigned under A71; this world uses A830 to A839. Reverse cost: none.
### A908 2026-10-21 World 10 was built in this session's own worktree, on main at 54444c8 (author-patterns, M4.P2.10)
As in A769, A789, and A839: `m4-p2-world10` is checked out in this session's worktree, started from main at baa852f and rebased onto main at 54444c8, later than the 66f98b6 named in the brief. World 9 is in review and not on main, so `p10-a-class-per-weapon` requires `p8-subclass-sandbox-when-not`. When World 9 merges, the Lead may point it at `p9-component-when-not`. Reverse cost: none.

### A907 2026-10-21 Data terms taught in World 10's concept quests while Pack 1 World 6 is in review (author-patterns, M4.P2.10)
The brief asked to reuse Pack 1 World 6's data asset and data table terms. World 6 is not on main yet, and on main no concept quest teaches `data-asset`, `primary-data-asset`, `data-table`, or `table-row-base`, so A72 would warn. Options: wait for World 6; add separate concept quests for those terms; list them in World 10's own concept quests, which already explain them where they are used. Chosen: the last. `p10-a-class-per-weapon` lists `data-asset` and `primary-data-asset`, and `p10-type-object-card` lists `data-table` and `table-row-base`. No new glossary entries were added, and no `firstQuestId` was set, so World 6 can claim those terms when it merges and teaches them earlier in course order. The Asset Manager is covered by the existing `primary-data-asset` entry, which has it as an alias. Reverse cost: none.

### A906 2026-10-21 Card changes for Type Object (author-patterns, M4.P2.10)
The type-object card was audited item by item for the source chapter's examples, and for examples taken from neighboring chapters. Rewritten:
- `problem`: was a hundred monster kinds, the chapter's own example; now forty weapon classes that only set numbers.
- `unrealForm`: dropped "parent-kind inheritance", the chapter's enhancement. It now names `TObjectPtr<const>`, row handles, the class default object, and spawning with the kind set before BeginPlay.
- `intent`: enemies replaced by quivers and lanterns.
- `whenNot`: adds truly per-object numbers.
- `type-object-recall-2`: enemy replaced by weapon.
- `type-object-scenario-2`: was dungeon floor tiles, which is the Flyweight chapter's terrain example; now arena spectators.

Added `type-object-recall-4` (arrows left belong to the quiver, not the row) and `type-object-scenario-3` (behavior per kind points to Subclass Sandbox). Use cases: `p10-weapon-kinds` and `p10-quiver-kinds`. Reverse cost: low.

### A905 2026-10-21 World 10's deliberate failure: a quiver handed out with SpawnActor (author-patterns, M4.P2.10)
Options:
- Decrementing the shared row: taught openly by the card's spot-the-bug item.
- Copying the row at BeginPlay: tested by `OneRowManyQuivers`, but nothing tempts it.
- `SpawnOfKind` spawning with `SpawnActor` and setting the kind afterwards.

Chosen: the last. SpawnActor runs BeginPlay before it returns, so the given BeginPlay refills from an empty kind and the quiver is handed out empty. `HandedOutQuiversAreFull` fails, and the rest pass. The card says a spawning function gives the object its kind, without saying when. To be checked on 5.8.3 with SpawnActor in place of the deferred spawn once the engine is reinstalled; engine proof pending under A75. Reverse cost: low.

### A904 2026-10-21 The refactor finds the kind by reflection and falls back to the kind class's defaults (author-patterns, M4.P2.10)
`p10-lantern-refactor` ships `UDojoP10LanternKind` as an empty primary data asset in the starter, so the files match and the tests compile on both versions.
- `BurnsOutAfterItsFuel` uses only the public functions and default numbers, so it passes before and after.
- `OneKindTunesEveryLantern` finds a `Kind` object property of the kind class, sets `FuelSeconds` through reflection, and checks that two lanterns go out at 5 seconds. It fails on the starter.
- `NoTuningOnTheLantern` fails on any editable, non-const float, double, int, or struct property the lantern declares.

A lantern with no kind reads `GetDefault<UDojoP10LanternKind>()`, so lanterns placed before the refactor behave the same. Reverse cost: low.

Facts confirmed in 5.8:

Reverse cost: low.

### A891 2026-10-22 A failed build whose lines all fall through the filter shows its last raw lines (builder-unreal, M1.3c)
The M1.3c failure came back with an empty `compilerOutput`: UBT's crash lines ("Unhandled exception. System.IO.FileNotFoundException ...", "Abort trap: 6") contain neither "error" nor "warning", so `LogFilter` dropped every line and nobody could see why the build failed. Options: widen the filter; fall back only when it is empty. Chosen: when a build fails and nothing passed the filter, `filteredOutput` is its last 20 non-empty raw lines. Successful builds, cancelled builds (fix round 1), and failures with real compiler errors are unchanged; a timed-out build gets the tail too. Reverse cost: none.

### A890 2026-10-22 A build whose UnrealBuildTool crashed while starting is tried again, up to three times (builder-unreal, M1.3c)
Found in M1.3c: UBT 5.8.3 backs up its shared log files in `~/Library/Application Support/Epic/UnrealBuildTool/` (`Log.BackupLogFile`, renaming `Trace.uba`) before it takes its `-WaitMutex` lock, so builds started within a few milliseconds of each other race on that rename; the losers throw `FileNotFoundException` and abort with exit code 134 after about 80 ms. The integration test files, run in parallel without `--no-file-parallelism` (A612), hit this on three of four sandbox builds; the app can hit it whenever its build starts at the same instant as another UBT (the content verifier, a second app window, the editor's own build). Options: serialize every build in the app (does not help against other programs); retry. Chosen: when the build program exits non-zero after an "Unhandled exception." line and before UBT logged "Log file:" (so it never took its lock or touched the project), `UnrealBuilder` waits one second and tries again, at most three attempts in all, with a progress line saying so; a cancel stops the retries. Every other failure is reported as before. Fix round 1: after the last attempt a startup crash answers a plain pipeline error ("The Unreal build tool stopped while starting, three times in a row. Another Unreal build may be starting at the same moment; wait for it to finish, then try again."), with the raw lines kept as detail, instead of reading as a compile failure; a cancel during the one-second pause ends the build at once; `durationMs` covers every attempt and pause. The two markers ("Unhandled exception." and "Log file:") were seen on UBT 5.8.3 on macOS only; they are unverified on Windows, where engine work now happens (A76). Reverse cost: none.

### A824 2026-10-21 A lost first-run build points to Settings, not to starting again (M2.1e fix round 1, builder-ui-core)
When main no longer knows a run, the first version said "start it again, built from a clean copy each time". That is false for the first build: once the template is copied, `SandboxProjectService.create` refuses the folder with `sandbox-folder-not-empty`. Options: make the retry rebuild when a sandbox path is set; change the advice. Chosen: change the advice and drop the retry button for this error on first run: "Restart Unreal Dojo, then open Settings and use Rebuild sandbox." A lost Settings rebuild says "restart, then rebuild again". Reverse cost: none.

### A823 2026-10-21 Main logs one line per run start and answer (M2.1e fix round 1, builder-ui-core; touches shell-ipc)
The cause of the tester's hang is unknown, so the next one must be traceable. `RunRegistry` takes a log function (a no-op by default, so tests stay quiet); `src/main/index.ts` passes `console.info`. It writes `[run] <id> started` on `begin` and `[run] <id> answered ok=<true|false> [code=<code>] after <ms> ms` when the answer is recorded. With these, a stuck screen whose run shows an "answered" line was lost in the window; one with no answer line was still going in main. Reverse cost: none.

### A822 2026-10-21 The window's build view is a recovery fallback that asks main when no new output arrives (M2.1e, builder-ui-core; fix round 1)
The tester once saw the first build sit at "Starting the first full build 0:00" for 20 minutes after three events, while a UBT log said "Result: Succeeded" in 25 s. The cause was not found. What was ruled out: the listener was attached (three events arrived); the answer travels on the invoke promise, so a late subscription cannot lose it; StrictMode does not remount mid-build. One known silent case: `UnrealBuilder` passes `-WaitMutex`, so while another UBT holds the mutex (other sessions' builds were running) Build.sh waits with no output, which fits "other UBT runs were active"; a shared UBT log could also explain the "Succeeded" line. A later candidate, not confirmed as this hang's cause: M1.3c (builder-unreal) found a UBT startup race behind silent fast failures, where UBT renames its log files before it takes `-WaitMutex`, so concurrent builds can interfere at start. What was wrong in the window, whatever the cause: its clock moved only with events (hence "0:00"), and it had no way to ask main anything. Chosen: `SandboxBuildTracker` subscribes before calling, ticks its own clock every second, and marks the build quiet after 15 s without new output. It then asks `getRunStatus`, and again every 15 s while quiet. A status counts as new output only when its `eventCount` is higher than the events the window has seen; a `running` answer with nothing new keeps the notice up and changes it to "Still running; no new output for m:ss" (fix round 1: the first version cleared the notice on any `running` answer, so with real IPC it flashed away every 15 s). Each check gives up after 10 s, freeing the button and letting the next round retry; after 3 failed checks in a row the notice says the app cannot get an answer and suggests restarting. Any progress event clears that count and the warning, since it proves the connection works (re-review). The answer is applied once, from the call or a finished status, whichever comes first. Options considered: a timeout error (the build may have passed); polling from the start (traffic on every build). Reverse cost: low; Settings' rebuild uses the same hook.

### A821 2026-10-21 A fake scenario where the first build goes quiet but stays alive (M2.1e, builder-ui-core; fix round 1)
`first-build-goes-quiet` forwards only the three setup events, then stays silent but alive for 32 s (like UBT waiting on another build's mutex), finishes, and never answers `createSandbox`; `getRunStatus` reports it running (same event count) and then finished. Status answers at the normal fake speed: the first version slowed them to 2 s, which hid the flashing notice. The e2e drives it from Start through "Still running; no new output" to "built". Reverse cost: none.

### A820 2026-10-21 `getRunStatus(runId)`: main remembers each run's last event, event count, and answer (M2.1e, builder-ui-core; touches shell-ipc)
Main resolves a build on the process exit event and kills it after 15 minutes, so main answers within that time unless something else is wrong; the window could not ask. Options: re-send progress on a timer; a status query. Chosen: the query, the smallest change. `RunRegistry` keeps each run's last event and event count, and the answers of the newest 20 finished runs; `trackRun` records them, converting a failure with the IPC wrapper's own `toAppError` (now exported from `registerHandlers.ts`). `getRunStatus` returns `{ runId, state: running | finished | unknown, lastEvent, eventCount, answer }` (`RunStatus`, `RunAnswer` in `src/shared/types/run.ts`). Submit ends its run early (`runRegistry.end` in `onRecording`, so a cancel cannot land while the attempt is recorded); a run ended that way stays `running` until its answer is recorded, so a later window using this query never sees "unknown" during recording. Shell-ipc files touched: `src/shared/ipc.ts`, `src/shared/types/run.ts`, `src/main/ipc/createHandlers.ts`, `src/main/ipc/registerHandlers.ts`, `src/main/ipc/trackRun.ts`, `src/main/services/unreal/RunRegistry.ts`, `src/main/index.ts`, plus `docs/systems/app-shell.md` and `docs/systems/unreal-pipeline.md`. Reverse cost: low.
### A803 2026-10-21 Run and Submit register the run before the availability check (M1.6g fix round 1, builder-rules)
A802's check ran before the run was registered, so a `cancelRun` or `cancelAll` that arrived while it was checking found no run. Options: begin the run first, then check; check, then look at cancellation. Chosen: the first. Both handlers now run the check inside `trackRun`, so the run id is registered from the first moment; after the check, a cancelled run stops with `run-cancelled` and no build starts. The engine integration tests and the review wiring tests pass an `assertQuestOpen` that always answers open, since they drive quests directly (the score keeper on a fresh save, for A65). Reverse cost: none.

### A924 2026-10-26 World 11's deliberate failure: a seat number used as an instance number (author-patterns, M4.P2.11)
Options:
- Setting `NumCustomDataFloats` after adding instances: an obscure setting, not the pattern.
- Writing to a shared style: taught openly in the first quest and on the card.
- `BreakSeat` calling `RemoveInstance(Seat)`.

Chosen: the last. Removing an instance moves other instances in the component's list, so after the first break a seat number no longer names its instance. `BrokenSeatsLeaveTheRest` breaks seats 3, 10, and 39 and compares the drawn locations with the intact seats. The test is written to fail whether removal shifts later instances down or swaps the last one in, so it does not depend on which one 5.8 does. The reference redraws from the actor's seat state. Nothing the learner sees before the first run (concepts, rubric, brief, card, glossary) says instance numbers move; the card says each instance is one transform the component draws, and the concepts and rubric speak only of seats (fix round 1). Engine check pending (A927). Reverse cost: low.

Both bases are `UCLASS(Abstract)` with a public non-virtual entry that enforces the rules. Reverse cost: low.

### A802 2026-10-21 Run and Submit refuse a locked or unknown quest first (M1.6g, builder-rules)
`runQuest` and `submitQuest` started a build for any quest id; opening a quest already checked it. Both handlers now call `ProgressContentService.assertQuestOpen(questId)` before the run is registered, answering `quest-not-found` or `quest-locked` with the World Map's reason, so no build, run id, or progress event happens for a quest the learner cannot open. The check sits in the handler table (`createHandlers.ts`) so the unreal and review services stay unchanged. Reverse cost: none.

### A801 2026-10-21 A self-checked explain item is neither right nor wrong in the drill result (M1.6g, builder-rules)
With tests-only review an explain item is self-checked and not scheduled (A318, A460), but the drill result still counted it as a miss, so an all-right drill read "4 / 6" with two items "due for review". Options: count it as right; count it as neither. Chosen: neither. `DrillItemResult.selfChecked: true` marks it (absent otherwise; `correct` stays false and means "not graded right", never "missed"); `DrillOutcome.selfCheckedCount` reports how many, and `correctCount` and `totalCount` cover only graded items. A concept quest's self-checked explain result carries `selfChecked: true` too (it was already left out of the pass count, A315). ui-quests changes the summary copy to use `selfCheckedCount`. Reverse cost: low. Fix round 1: a self-checked item also never earns the drill reward. The session keeps the ids of its items that were scheduled due when it started, and finishing pays drill XP, streak credit, and the daily drill offer only when one of them was answered and graded (not self-checked). Before, a drill whose only due item was self-checked paid every time, because the item stays due.

### A800 2026-10-21 Self-explanations are saved with the quest draft (M1.6g, builder-rules)
`QuestProgress.selfExplanations` existed in the type and the request schema, but `saveQuestDraft` dropped it and the save schema stripped it on load, so a passed worked build reopened with its gate at "0 of 5". Now `saveQuestDraft` stores it (a draft without it keeps the saved ones), the save schema keeps it, `getProgress` returns it with the quest's progress (where the build screen reads it), and `resetQuest` clears it with the files. The field is optional, so older saves load unchanged and no save version bump is needed. Reverse cost: none.
### A856 2026-10-26 The A70 gate on follow-ups is a required argument (M3.4 fix round 1, builder-review)
`ReviewService.explainFinding` took `options = { allowPaidApi: true }`, so a future caller that left it out would ask the paid API on any attempt. Options: keep the default; make it required. Chosen: required, `explainFinding(input, options, signal?)`, the same as `gradeReflection(input, options, signal?)`, so every caller must say whether the attempt passed. Reverse cost: none.

### A855 2026-10-26 Only an AI provider's follow-up answer is kept (M3.4 fix round 1, builder-review)
A851 and A852 kept whatever came back, including the tests-only fallback after a timeout, after the A70 skip, or with no reviewer set up, and the kept copy was answered first, so it was served for good even after Claude Code was set up; a timed-out reflection grade with `retryReviewSuggested` could never be retried. Options: keep fallbacks with a time limit; keep only AI answers. Chosen: only AI answers. Inside main, `ReviewService.explainFinding` now answers `{ text, provider }` (the IPC answer stays the text), and `AttemptReviewService` keeps the explanation only when `provider` is not `tests-only`; for the reflection it keeps the grade only when `grade.provider` is not `tests-only`, and the same-sentence shortcut also skips a tests-only grade (one an older build may have kept). A fallback is still answered and shown, so the learner sees why, and the next ask tries the reviewer again. The fake does the same. The M3.4 handoff list is corrected: neither call rejects on a timeout or an unavailable provider (they fall back), and neither can be cancelled. Tests: a timeout then a real answer, for both. Reverse cost: none.

### A854 2026-10-21 M3.4 changes no renderer screen; the screens need these calls (M3.4, builder-review)
The Lead kept the renderer out of this branch except the fake and the shared types, because ui-quests is on M2.2b. What changed for the screens: a new call `getQuestAlternative(questId)`; `explainFinding` and `answerReflection` keep their signatures and return types, so the existing loot screen still compiles and works, but both now keep their answers on the attempt; `AttemptRecord` gains `explanations` and `reflection`; new AppError code `alternative-locked`. The exact list for ui-quests (M3.4b) is in the M3.4 iterations entry. Reverse cost: none.

### A853 2026-10-21 The reflection answer gets its own grader and prompt, reading the learner's code (M3.4, builder-review)
The reflection answer was graded by the explain grader with the review summary as context and no model answer, so it could not judge whether the sentence was true of the learner's own code. Options: keep the explain grader; a reflection grader over the question, the review summary, and the stored submitted files. Chosen: the second, `ReflectionGrader` with prompt `reflection-grader.md` (version 1), the explain grade's output schema, and the same tag guard (files and the sentence are data, never instructions). It grades correctness, ownership (about their code, not a textbook line), and precision, and accepts any right reason. Tests-only answers the usual self-check. Drill explain items keep the explain grader. Reverse cost: low.

### A852 2026-10-21 The reflection answer is kept on the attempt and the same sentence is not graded twice (M3.4, builder-review)
Before, `answerReflection` graded and forgot: the loot screen lost the answer on a reload, and every repeat was another provider call. Options: keep nothing; keep the latest answer; keep every answer. Chosen: `AttemptRecord.reflection = { answer, grade, at }`, the latest one. The same trimmed sentence again answers the kept grade with no call; a different sentence is graded and replaces it (improving a sentence is the point of the question); an empty one is `invalid-payload`. It is free and changes no XP or score. Unlike files, review, run, and explanations, it is kept when an attempt's details are pruned, because it is the learner's own short sentence. Under A70 the paid API grades it only on an attempt whose tests all passed; otherwise Claude Code or tests-only (self-check) answers, with `API_FOLLOW_UP_REASON`. Reverse cost: low.

### A851 2026-10-21 "Why is this a finding?" answers are kept on the attempt, and A70 covers the follow-ups (M3.4, builder-review)
The explanation cache lived only in memory (200 entries), so a restart paid for the same explanation again, and the follow-ups asked the paid API on any attempt, wider than A70 allows for the review itself. Options: keep the memory cache only; keep explanations on the attempt. Chosen: `AttemptRecord.explanations`, keyed `<findingIndex>:<finding hash>` (the hash of A742), answered before any provider is asked. They are dropped when Retry review replaces the review and when pruning drops the review, and are not kept on an attempt that has lost its review. For both follow-ups, the paid API is asked only when the attempt's stored run passed every test (`FollowUpOptions.allowPaidApi`); an attempt with no stored run counts as not passed. Claude Code (subscription) answers either way, and tests-only answers with the finding's detail and the reason. `explainFinding` keeps its string answer, so the screens need no change. Reverse cost: low.

### A850 2026-10-21 The alternative after a pass comes from main, only once the quest is passed, with no AI call (M3.4, builder-review)
The product shows "one alternative after a pass: an idiomatic Unreal version with a one-paragraph tradeoff note". The authored `alternative` (and a breakdown's `alternativeNote`) reached the renderer with every quest, and only the loot screen's markup hid it before a pass. Options: an AI-tailored comparison of the learner's code with the alternative (a new paid path, which A70 does not allow, and the authored note is already written for this); serve the authored text from main behind a pass check. Chosen: `getQuestAlternative(questId)`, which answers `QuestAlternative { questId, markdown, reviewHint? }` when the quest is passed, `null` for a kind without one, and `alternative-locked` before a pass. `reviewHint` is the AI reviewer's `alternativeHint` from the latest passed attempt; a tests-only hint is generic and left out. Follow-up, not in this branch: once the loot screen calls this, content-engine can drop `alternative` and `alternativeNote` from `LearnerQuest`, so they never reach the renderer before a pass. Reverse cost: low.

### A767 2026-10-21 World 7's deliberate failure: a reused mode object that keeps last visit's data (author-patterns, M4.P2.7)
Options: the kiosk always returning to Idle (that is the worked quest's naive-first step, not a surprise); a mode change that skips Exit and Enter (taught openly in the card); the ballista's Aiming object, made once and reused, not resetting its progress on Enter. Chosen: the reset. The obvious Aiming keeps AimProgress as a member and grows it in TickState, with no reset, so an aim interrupted at 60 percent resumes at 60 percent and fires early; `EveryAimStartsFresh` fails. The card quest (before the ballista) no longer says modes must reset on entry; the ballista's Alternative and the card's new recall item state it afterwards. ### A766 2026-10-21 State objects are UObject default subobjects, one class per file (author-patterns, M4.P2.7)
The ballista's modes are an abstract `UDojoP7BallistaState` (UCLASS(Abstract), empty virtual defaults so unhandled inputs are ignored) and three subclasses, made once with `CreateDefaultSubobject` in the ballista's constructor and held in UPROPERTY `TObjectPtr`s, with `CurrentState` also a UPROPERTY. Chosen: reused default subobjects, which also sets up the world's deliberate failure (A767). Keeping one class per file makes the quest ten files, which the stub supplies with declarations; the learner writes about a dozen short bodies. Reverse cost: medium.

### A746 2026-10-21 The stored compiler output ends in a visible character, so a second trim removes nothing (M1.7d fix round 2, builder-review)
A744 stored the trimmed compiler output cut one character past 8000. When that extra character was whitespace, or a whitespace run reached the cut, the review's second trim on a retry removed it (and the run before it), the text fitted under 8000, and the retry lost "(cut off)"; compiler logs are full of spaces and newlines, so this was common. Options: store the already formatted text and make the formatter leave formatted text alone; store the first 8000 characters plus the first non-whitespace character after the cut. Chosen: the second, in `keepForReview` (`reviewInputLimits.ts`). The stored copy then ends in a non-whitespace character, so the retry's trim removes nothing at either end (the text was already trimmed at the start), it is longer than the cut, and the formatter produces the same text and mark as the first review. A trimmed text longer than the cut always has such a character, because it ends in one. Test messages are not trimmed by the review, so A744's one-past-the-cut copy stays right for them. Tests: a retry with whitespace right after the cut, and with a whitespace run up to and across it, each gives exactly the first prompt; a direct check that the formatter gives the same result for the stored and the original output over eleven edge cases. All three fail on A744's version. Reverse cost: none.

### A745 2026-10-21 A retried review is not written onto an attempt pruned while the retry waited (M1.7d fix round 1, builder-review)
A new Submit of the same quest can prune an attempt to its summary (A604) while a slow retry of it waits on the reviewer. Options: write the review anyway (a review with no files or run behind it, which "why is this a finding?" could not explain); skip the write silently; skip it and say so. Chosen: `ProgressService.replaceAttemptReview` checks, inside its save queue, that the attempt still has `files` and `run`, and otherwise answers `attempt-not-found` ("This attempt's code is no longer stored, so its review was not kept."), so the screen shows why and nothing is saved. Reverse cost: none.

### A744 2026-10-21 One set of review input cuts, and the stored run keeps one character past each (M1.7d fix round 1, builder-review)
The review input trimmed the compiler output and cut it at 8000 characters with "(cut off)", but the stored run kept the raw first 8000 characters, so a retry lost the mark, and output with leading whitespace gave fewer real characters; test messages were stored cut at 1000 with no mark although the first review read them in full. Options: store the already formatted text; store one character past each cut and let the same formatting run again; reword A740. Chosen: the second, with the cuts in one module, `src/main/services/review/reviewInputLimits.ts` (compiler output 8000, test message 1000, the "(cut off)" mark), used by both the review input (`messageSections.ts`) and `AttemptFileRules.capRun`. The review input now also cuts a test message at 1000 characters with " (cut off)", on the first review and on a retry alike. `capRun` stores the compiler output trimmed and each message to one character past its cut, so the retried prompt is the same text as the first, marks included. A test checks a retry over output and a message longer than both cuts. Reverse cost: none.

### A743 2026-10-21 `retryReview` is a plain call, not a long run with a run id (M1.7d, builder-review)
Options: give `retryReview` a renderer-made run id like `submitQuest`, with progress events and `cancelRun`; make it a plain request. Chosen: plain, `retryReview(attemptId): Promise<CodeReview>`. There is no build or test step to stream, the screens already show "Asking again" while it is pending, and a review is bounded by the provider's own timeout (3 minutes for Claude Code, which then answers tests-only with a retry hint). The renderer calls it through `useDojoAction`. Reverse cost: low, if a cancel button is wanted later.

### A742 2026-10-21 The finding explanation cache key includes the finding's own text (M1.7d, builder-review)
Retry review replaces an attempt's findings under the same attempt id and time, so A600's key `<attemptId>:<at>:<findingIndex>` could answer an explanation of the old finding. Options: clear the explainer cache on every retry; add a hash of the finding to the key. Chosen: the key adds the first 16 hex characters of a SHA-256 of the finding (severity, title, detail, file, line). The same finding explained twice still hits the cache; a changed one never does. Reverse cost: none.

### A741 2026-10-21 A retried review replaces only the stored review; score, pass, and XP stay (M1.7d, builder-review)
A356 (the stopgap) submitted the same files again, which recorded a new attempt, raised the attempt count, and before a pass counted as another failed or passing Submit. Options: rescore the attempt with the new review (0.6 tests plus 0.4 review), which could turn a paid pass into a fail or pay XP twice; record a new attempt again; replace only the review. Chosen: replace only the review, through `ProgressService.replaceAttemptReview`. The attempt's score, pass, XP, the quest's status, and the attempt count stay exactly as Submit settled them; the loot screen keeps its loot and shows the new review beside its own review score. A learner who wants the new review to count submits again. Reverse cost: low.

### A740 2026-10-21 Submit attempts store their run result, so Retry review needs no new build (M1.7d, builder-review)
Retry review must review the same code and the same engine verdict without building again, but attempts kept only the files (A467) and the review. Options: rebuild from the stored files (slow, and the result could differ); store the run result on the attempt. Chosen: `AttemptRecord.run` holds the Submit's `RunResult`, holding exactly what the review input reads of it: the compiler output and each test message, kept as A744 says, so a retried review reads what the first one read. Like `files` and `review`, it is stored on Submits only and pruned to the quest's last five Submits (A604); the save schema reads it as optional, so older saves load, and older attempts without it answer `attempt-not-found` with "submit again". `retryReview(attemptId)` (new IPC method) rebuilds the review input from the authored quest, the stored files, and the stored run, and asks `ReviewService.reviewCode`, so A70 (the paid API reviews only all-passed runs; Claude Code any compiled one), the failover, and the fallbacks hold on a retry. The Quest screen and the loot screen call it in place of A356's resubmit. Reverse cost: low.

### A710 2026-10-21 Which world loses when two packs share a world id (M1.8e, builder-content-engine)
A73 makes a world id used in two packs a loader and verifier error. Open points decided: packs are taken in pack order and the first world keeps the id; the later world is reported on its own `world.json` ("already used by <earlier world.json> in another pack") and left out together with its quests, so those quests never claim quest ids, enter `questSources`, or reach the catalog. The verifier reports it as an error because loader problems are errors. Within one pack the existing duplicate check still applies. Options considered: drop both worlds (loses content that is fine); keep both and only warn (the catalog would still hide one). Reverse cost: low.

### A701 2026-10-21 `startDrill`'s test-out request names the pack; small renderer edits (M1.6f, builder-rules)
A world id alone cannot say which pack's world to test, and a `pack/world` key would not pass the IPC id rule (letters, digits, dot, underscore, dash), so the request becomes `{ kind: "test-out", packId, worldId }` (`src/shared/types/drill.ts`, `drillRequestSchema`). Renderer call sites changed with it, each a line or two: the World Map looks up `getTestOutStatus()[worldKeyOf(pack, world)]` and passes the pack to `ContractCard`, whose Test out button and the drill route (`drillRequestFromRoute`) now carry `pack`; the fake API keys its test-out status and saved test-outs the same way. A drill route without a pack falls back to the due drill, like any incomplete route. Reverse cost: low.

### A700 2026-10-21 Test-outs are keyed by `<packId>/<worldId>` (M1.6f, builder-rules)
World ids are only unique inside a pack (A510), but `getTestOutStatus` and `testedOutWorldIds` used the bare world id, so two packs reusing an id would share a test-out. Chosen: the key `<packId>/<worldId>` from `worldKeyOf` in `src/shared/types/progress.ts`, used by the status map, the save's `testedOutWorldIds`, the drill outcome, the unlock graph's skippable check, and the test-out term set. Saves made before this hold bare ids; they cannot be qualified without content, so instead of a save version bump, `ProgressService.qualifyLegacyTestOuts` runs when the content view is first built: a bare id becomes the key of every loaded world with that id (exactly what it meant before; today world ids are unique, so one key), an id no loaded world has is kept, and nothing is written when there is nothing to qualify. Reverse cost: low.

### A670 2026-10-21 How the A72 term-order warning counts (M1.8d, builder-content-engine)
A72 asks for a warning when a quest lists a term no earlier concept quest teaches. Open points decided: "teaches" means the term is in a concept quest's `terms[]` (the same meaning A312 uses); a concept quest teaches its own terms, so it never warns about them; every other kind (build, refactor, breakdown, boss) is checked; one warning per quest and term, so each line names one thing to fix; the warning names the concept quest that first teaches the term later, with its file, or says no concept quest teaches it; course order is pack order, then world order, then quest order; the format examples are not in the course and are skipped. Options considered for the grouping: one warning per quest listing every term (shorter, but each fix is hidden in a long line); one per term. Reverse cost: none.

### A660 2026-10-21 How A72's course-order term gate is built (M1.6e, builder-rules)
Per A72 a term gates a quest only when a concept quest teaching it comes earlier in course order. Built in `taughtTerms.ts`: course order compares pack order, then world order, then quest order; each term's teacher is its earliest concept quest, so a term taught in World 1 and taught again later still gates from World 1 on; a concept quest is still never gated by its own terms (A312). Test-out uses the same idea per world: a world's test-out terms are its terms taught in that world or an earlier one, so a term World 2 teaches never blocks World 1's test-out (A322). Checked on the real content: passing World 1's quests in course order opens every one of them with no drill, including f1-training-dummy, f1-score-keeper, and the dash breakdown. Reverse cost: none.

### A657 2026-10-21 Glossary and card changes for Event Queue (author-patterns, M4.P2.6)
Added `parallel-for` (`ParallelFor`, category framework, Epic's 5.8 API page checked live, signature confirmed in `Async/ParallelFor.h`), first taught by `p6-gate-tallies`. The existing `tqueue` entry is reused; its first quest stays Pack 1 World 3. The event-queue card lists `p6-knockout-herald` and `p6-gate-tallies` as use cases and gains `event-queue-recall-4` (taking the batch first) and `event-queue-scenario-3` (when-not, multiple-choice). Two older card items were reworded because they leaned on the source chapter: `event-queue-scenario-1` used identical breaking sounds merged into one, the chapter's main audio example, and is now about combining a frame's hits on one dummy into a single floating number; `event-queue-recall-2` described head and tail indices wrapping around an array. In fix round 1 the card's `problem` and `unrealForm` were also rewritten around this world's own cases (a knockout mid-teardown, gate reports from other threads, damage numbers combined per target), with no sound, audio engine, or ring buffer wording left, and `event-queue-recall-2` became a fill-blank on the herald's tick group. Reverse cost: low.

### A656 2026-10-21 World 6's deliberate failure: a burst lands in one frame (author-patterns, M4.P2.6)
Options: walking the live mailbox while handlers post (a ranged-for loop over a changing `TArray` stops the test run, so the worked quest gives the batch loop and asks only for the handover); a pointer kept in a queued event (the worked quest's marked lines already teach the copy); the gate board taking every waiting report in one call. Chosen: the budget. The obvious ApplyReports dequeues until empty; `AFewReportsPerTick` fails ("taken 10, expected 4") and so does the tick count in `ReportsFromManyThreads`. Checked on 5.8.3 (see M4.P2.6 in iterations). The card quest no longer mentions budgets or "a few per tick", so the surprise holds; the when-not quest states the rule afterwards. Reverse cost: low.

### A655 2026-10-21 Event Queue arc, built away from the source's examples (author-patterns, M4.P2.6)
Not used anywhere in briefs, cards, choices, or distractors: a blocking sound-playing call, merging identical sound requests into the loudest, a tutorial system listening on a central bus, or a hand-written head and tail ring buffer. Arc: `p6-later-not-now` (concept, no pattern name: a delegate runs listeners at the sender's moment, and when that moment is wrong), `p6-knockout-herald` (worked: fighters post knockout snapshots, then leave; the herald in `TG_PostUpdateWork` takes the batch and announces through a native multicast delegate), `p6-event-queue-card`, `p6-gate-tallies` (stub: worker threads submit through `TQueue<..., EQueueMode::Mpsc>` from `ParallelFor`, and the game thread applies at most `ReportsPerTick` per tick), `p6-keg-refactor` (a recursive keg chain 200 calls deep becomes detonations posted and blown one wave per tick, deepest chain 1), `p6-event-queue-when-not` (react now when safe, a sender that needs an answer, a shared queue is a global, quiet loops, bound the queue, the engine already queues). Unreal facts checked against 5.8 source: `EQueueMode::Mpsc` and `Spsc`, `TQueue::Enqueue` and `Dequeue` in `Containers/Queue.h`, `ParallelFor` in `Async/ParallelFor.h`, `AsyncTask` in `Async/Async.h`. Reverse cost: medium.

### A654 2026-10-21 The worked quest's gap is the handover, not the loop (author-patterns, M4.P2.6)
If the learner wrote the herald's whole Announce, the most likely first attempt (a ranged-for over the live mailbox while a listener posts) would trip the engine's array-changed check and stop the whole test run instead of failing one test. Options: let it crash; give the loop over a local batch and ask for the handover. Chosen: the handover. The naive version (copying without emptying) fails cleanly by announcing a knockout twice. Reverse cost: low.

### A653 2026-10-21 The keg refactor changes when the chain resolves, on purpose (author-patterns, M4.P2.6)
The starter blows the whole row in one frame through recursion; the refactor spreads it over ticks. Options: keep it instant with an explicit worklist (that removes the recursion but is not this pattern); queue detonations and blow one wave per tick. Chosen: one wave per tick. `EveryKegBlowsOnce` passes on the starter, while `NoExplosionInsideAnother` and `OneWavePerTick` fail on it. The Alternative names the worklist as the right fix when the chain must resolve instantly. Reverse cost: low.
