### A650 2026-10-21 Decision block A650 to A659 from the Lead (author-patterns, M4.P2.6)
Assigned by the Lead under A71; this world uses A650 to A657. Reverse cost: none.

### A604 2026-10-21 Reviews are pruned with files: only a quest's last five Submits keep them (M1.7c fix round 1, Lead direction)
Directed by the Lead: stored code reviews follow the same limit as stored files (A467). `AttemptFileRules.pruneOlderDetails` counts a quest's attempts that hold a review or files, newest first, and removes both from all but the latest five; score, pass, and XP stay on every attempt. "Why is this a finding?" on an older attempt answers `attempt-not-found` ("That attempt has no code review."). Reverse cost: none.

### A603 2026-10-21 The reviewer reads the capped files the attempt stores (M1.7c fix round 1, Lead direction)
Directed by the Lead: `QuestSubmitService` applies `AttemptFileRules.capFiles` once after the run and gives that copy to both the code reviewer and the recorder, so every finding points at code the explainer can show later. The runner still builds the full files. `capFiles` is idempotent (a cut file cut again is unchanged), so the recorder's own cap changes nothing. Reverse cost: none.

### A602 2026-10-21 A bad or refused code review after a finished engine run falls back to tests-only (M1.7c fix round 1, Lead direction)
Directed by the Lead: for the code review only, `review-invalid-output` and `review-refused` no longer fail the call. Tests-only answers with the error's message, "press Retry review to ask again", and `retryReviewSuggested: true`, and Submit records the attempt as usual. The provider is not marked unavailable (the next Submit asks it again). Breakdown and explain grading still surface these errors, since nothing expensive is lost there. Reverse cost: low.

### A601 2026-10-21 Once recording starts, a Submit is finished and cannot be cancelled (M1.7c fix round 1, builder-review)
A cancel during `recordCodeAttempt` saved the attempt while `trackRun` reported `run-cancelled`. Options: change `trackRun` for every operation; skip the cancel check after completion; end the run in the registry before recording. Chosen: the third, for Submit only. `submitQuest` takes an `onRecording` callback that the handler wires to `runRegistry.end(runId)`; `QuestSubmitService` calls it after the review and right before recording. A cancel after that answers `run-not-found` ("It may have finished already."), the signal never aborts, and the Submit answers its saved outcome. `trackRun` still ends the run in `finally` (ending twice is harmless). Quitting no longer waits for that save, which is written atomically. Reverse cost: low.

### A600 2026-10-21 The finding explanation cache key includes the attempt's time (M1.7c fix round 1, builder-review)
Attempt ids restart at `attempt-1` after `resetProgress`, so `<attemptId>:<findingIndex>` could serve an explanation of an earlier, different review. Options: clear the cache on reset (needs a hook from progress into review); add the attempt's `at` timestamp to the key. Chosen: the key is `<attemptId>:<at>:<findingIndex>`, which is unique across resets with no new dependency. Reverse cost: none.

### A469 2026-10-21 Submit's engine check uses the tests-only reviewer (M1.7c, builder-review)
The integration test must run on any machine with the engine, without a Claude Code login or an API key, and must not spend money. Chosen (as the Lead asked): `tests/integration/submitQuest.test.ts` builds the same `createReviewHandlers` wiring the app uses, with a `ReviewService` whose settings choose `tests-only`, a real `ProgressService` on an in-memory disk, and the real content and unreal services, then submits `f1-score-keeper` with its reference solution. It is opt-in through `DOJO_ENGINE_ROOT`, like the M1.3b check (A187), and runs under the shared engine lock. Reverse cost: none.

### A468 2026-10-21 Submit runs through `QuestRunService.runQuest`, built in the composition root (M1.7c, builder-review)
Options: give `QuestSubmitService` the runner and quest file writer directly; reuse the run path M1.3b built for `runQuest`. Chosen: the second. `index.ts` passes `(request, onProgress, signal) => getUnrealServices().questRunService.runQuest(request.questId, request.files, onProgress, signal)` into `createReviewHandlers`, so Submit gets the same prerequisite fill, sandbox checks, loop, and progress events as Run, and the IPC layer's `trackRun` adds the run id, cancel, and `run-cancelled`. Every Submit dependency (review service, content catalog, progress service, runner path) is built in `index.ts`. Reverse cost: none.

### A467 2026-10-21 Submit attempts keep their files, capped, for the quest's last five Submits (M1.7c, Lead direction)
"Why is this a finding?" needs the code the review saw; the quest's draft can change after Submit. Directed by the Lead: `AttemptRecord.files` stores the submitted files on build, refactor, and boss Submits only (`AttemptInput.isSubmit`), and `explainFinding` reads only those, never the draft. Caps (`AttemptFileRules`, picked within the Lead's example): each file is cut at 64 KB with a one-line note saying it was cut; files are kept in name order until the attempt reaches 256 KB, and the rest are left out; only a quest's latest five Submits that have files keep them, older ones drop `files` (score, review, and XP stay). Worst case per quest is about 1.25 MB, and only quests the learner has submitted grow. Reverse cost: low.

### A466 2026-10-21 `recordAttempt` stores the Submit's code review on the attempt (M1.7c, Lead direction)
Directed by the Lead: builder-review adds `review?: CodeReview` to `AttemptInput` in this task and writes it into `AttemptRecord.review`; builder-rules was told. The save schema (`progressSchema.ts`) now reads `review` and `files` on attempts as optional fields, so older saves load unchanged, and a reload keeps them (before, zod's plain object would have dropped them). `CodeAttemptRecorder` passes the review and files of every Submit. Reverse cost: low.

### A465 2026-10-21 Review handlers arrive through `getReviewHandlers()`; the code attempt recorder lives in ai-review (M1.7c, builder-review)
Options: add the review service and the progress pieces to `HandlerDependencies` one by one; give the IPC layer one `ReviewHandlerService` (the four review methods and Submit) built by `createReviewHandlers`. Chosen: the second, as a function so the review service is still built on first use. Scoring and recording a Submit (`CodeAttemptRecorder`) sits in `src/main/services/review/` and uses progress's `PassRules`, `toRuleQuests`, and `ProgressService.recordAttempt` without changing them; if the Lead prefers it inside `ProgressContentService`, it moves there unchanged. Four handler test files gained the new dependency as a "not used" fake. Reverse cost: low.

### A464 2026-10-21 New AppError code `attempt-not-found` (M1.7c, builder-review)
`explainFinding` and `answerReflection` look up an attempt by id. Options: reuse `invalid-payload` or `quest-not-found`; add a code. Chosen: `attempt-not-found` in `src/shared/types/errors.ts`, used when the attempt is not in the save, has no code review (concept, breakdown, or a failed build), or the finding index is past the end. Reverse cost: none.

### A463 2026-10-21 Superseded by A466 and A467 (M1.7c, builder-review)
The first M1.7c commit used the quest's draft as the files and stored no review on the attempt while the Lead was asked who changes progress. The Lead answered; A466 and A467 replace this entry.

### A462 2026-10-21 A tests-only review counts as no review score (M1.7c, builder-review)
progress-and-rules says the code score is 0.6 tests plus 0.4 review, with review = tests when tests-only. The tests-only reviewer's own score is the share of tests passed, but its fallback answers carry that score only by coincidence. Chosen: `CodeAttemptRecorder` passes `reviewScore: null` to `PassRules` whenever the review's provider is `tests-only`, so the rules' own "review = tests" path decides. Confirmed by the Lead, and checked against `docs/systems/progress-and-rules.md` ("score = 0.6 tests + 0.4 review (review = tests when tests-only)"): with review = tests the score is the test score alone. Reverse cost: none.

### A461 2026-10-21 `submitQuest` reviews build and refactor quests only for now (M1.7c, builder-review)
Bosses pass through their integration build (A317), whose rubric is `integrationRubric` and whose files span several build quests; that flow is not specified yet. Chosen: `submitQuest` refuses any other kind with `invalid-payload` and a message to use the quest's own Submit. Reverse cost: low, once the boss integration build is designed.

### A460 2026-10-21 Breakdowns and explain items are graded by the review service (M1.7c, replaces A318)
The tests-only stand-in for `ProgressContentService` is replaced by graders that call the review service built from the settings service. The service picks Claude Code, the API, or tests-only per call and falls back on its own (A154, A300, A301), so a missing reviewer still never blocks a breakdown or a drill. Explain items self-checked by tests-only are still not scheduled, as A318 set. `createTestsOnlyGraders` stays for tests. Reverse cost: one line.

### A557 2026-10-21 Decision numbers A550 to A559 for Pack 2 World 5 (author-patterns, M4.P2.5)
A540 to A547 are World 4's; nothing on main or any branch uses A550 or above. Reverse cost: none.

### A556 2026-10-21 Glossary for Command: one new entry, one existing entry reused (author-patterns, M4.P2.5)
Added `scoped-transaction` (`FScopedTransaction`, category editor workflow, Epic's 5.8 API page checked live, header confirmed at `Editor/UnrealEd/Public/ScopedTransaction.h`). A new Gameplay Ability System entry was drafted and dropped: the glossary already has `gas` with the same aliases, so the quests use `gas` and its `firstQuestId` is set to `p5-command-card`. The command card lists `p5-drill-orders` and `p5-course-planner` as use cases and gains `command-recall-4` (hold a waiting order's target weakly, check it when it runs) and `command-scenario-3` (when-not, multiple-choice). Reverse cost: low.

### A555 2026-10-21 World 5's deliberate failure: a new edit after an undo must end the old future (author-patterns, M4.P2.5)
Options: an order whose dummy is gone (the worked quest's marked lines already teach the weak-pointer check, so not a surprise); a waiting order that crashes on a null target (would stop the test run); the planner's redo tail. Chosen: the redo tail. The obvious Perform appends without cutting the history back, so redo can resurrect an abandoned edit and the second undo takes back the wrong one. To keep the surprise, the card quest (before the planner) describes undo and redo but not the cut; the when-not quest states it afterwards. Checked on 5.8.3 with the cut removed (see M4.P2.5 in iterations). Reverse cost: low.

### A554 2026-10-21 Commands are value structs here; the class form is shown, not built (author-patterns, M4.P2.5)
The project rule is one class per file, and a solution must have exactly the starter's files, so a quest with a command base class and several command subclasses would need eight or more files. Options: many-file quests; value commands (a `USTRUCT` naming the move or edit, with an enum where needed). Chosen: values for all three quests (`FDojoP5DrillOrder`, `FDojoP5CourseEdit`, `FDojoP5ScoreAdjustment`), which are easy to queue, store, and inspect, and the card's example shows the `UObject` subclass form with the performer handed in. A later combo world can build the class form. Reverse cost: medium.

### A553 2026-10-21 Command arc, built away from the source's examples (author-patterns, M4.P2.5)
Not used: gamepad buttons mapped to jump and fire, an actor handed to button handlers, units moving on a strategy board with undo, or the chapter's sentences and numbers. Arc: `p5-actions-as-values` (concept, no pattern name; Enhanced Input Actions as the engine's half), `p5-drill-orders` (worked: orders queued as values naming move and dummy, carried out one per beat on a timer, weak targets checked when they run), `p5-command-card`, `p5-course-planner` (stub: course edits with before and after, a history with an applied count, undo and redo), `p5-referee-refactor` (snapshot undo replaced by recorded score adjustments, so goals scored in between survive an undo), `p5-command-when-not` (nothing varies, undo is all or nothing, the redo tail, snapshots have their place, Gameplay Abilities and Input Actions already exist, no command per frame for held input). Unreal facts checked: `ke` imports enum parameters by name through `ImportEnumFromBuffer` (5.8 `EnumProperty.cpp`), so `ke * Place 0 EO_Hurdle` works. Reverse cost: medium.

### A552 2026-10-21 The referee refactor fixes a bug through the refactor (author-patterns, M4.P2.5)
As in Worlds 2 and 3, the refactor keeps the public behavior for the cases that worked (quiet corrections undo newest first; `QuietCorrectionsUndo` passes on the starter) and fixes the case the old shape could not handle (goals between corrections). Reverse cost: low.

### A551 2026-10-21 Pack 2 World 5 has no boss yet; any boss will follow A67 (author-patterns, M4.P2.5)
Reverse cost: none.

### A547 2026-10-21 Decision numbers A540 to A549 for Pack 2 World 4 (author-patterns, M4.P2.4)
Main uses A500 to A503, A510, A511, and A520; no branch uses A530 or above. Chosen: A540 to A549, leaving A530 to A539 free as a gap. Reverse cost: none.

### A546 2026-10-21 Glossary and card additions for Double Buffer (author-patterns, M4.P2.4)
Added `render-thread` (category framework, the existing label that fits best; Epic's 5.8 "Threaded Rendering" page, title checked live), first taught by `p4-read-while-writing`; aliases include `FSceneProxy` and "game thread", none shared with another entry. The double-buffer card lists `p4-straw-fire` and `p4-drum-pad` as use cases and gains `double-buffer-recall-4` (what the back copy holds right after a swap) and `double-buffer-scenario-3` (a when-not multiple-choice item: a fuse that should sweep in one pass). Reverse cost: low.

### A545 2026-10-21 World 4's deliberate failure: a swapped-back copy that is not emptied (author-patterns, M4.P2.4)
Options: writing in place on the straw floor (that is the worked quest's whole lesson, so not a surprise); a held reference across the swap (would read freed or moved memory, a crash risk); the drum pad's back copy collecting new hits on top of the batch it held before. Chosen: the drum pad. The obvious version swaps and does not clear, so hits from two frames back return. Checked on 5.8.3 (see M4.P2.4 in iterations). To keep the surprise, the card quest (before the drum pad) explains swap cost and references but not the stale back copy; the when-not quest states the rule afterwards. Reverse cost: low.

### A544 2026-10-21 The standings refactor's honest alternative (author-patterns, M4.P2.4)
In the standings round, computing the leader once before an in-place loop would also fix the designer's case, because each team's own score is touched only once. Options: invent a round where only a full second copy works; keep the simple round and say so. Chosen: keep it, and the Alternative section says exactly that, explaining why the second copy is still the general fix (the next rule that reads another team's score breaks the in-place loop again). The rubric asks for the second copy because the quest is the refactor into the pattern. Reverse cost: low.

### A543 2026-10-21 Double Buffer arc, built away from the source's examples (author-patterns, M4.P2.4)
Lessons from D13 reviews applied from the start: no stage of actors reacting to each other's slaps, no framebuffer tearing as the motivating story, no per-object two-slot array with a shared index, no numbers from the chapter. Arc: `p4-read-while-writing` (concept, no pattern name), `p4-straw-fire` (worked: a straw floor where fire spreads one square per step, read from the finished floor, write the next, `Swap`), `p4-double-buffer-card`, `p4-drum-pad` (stub: hits arriving mid-frame collect in a back copy that swaps to the front at frame start), `p4-standings-refactor` (team standings whose catch-up bonus depended on which team was scored first), `p4-double-buffer-when-not` (independent updates, order as the design, readers that need a write at once, stale back copies, memory and swap cost). Unreal facts used: `Swap` on `TArray` from `Templates/UnrealTemplate.h` (5.8 source), render-thread scene proxies, and `GetWorldTimerManager().SetTimer` for the floor's step. Reverse cost: medium.

### A542 2026-10-21 Walk-order switches stay as editable properties (author-patterns, M4.P2.4)
The straw floor has `bWalkBackward` and the standings have `bScoreInReverse`. Options: test order independence with a hidden test hook; expose a property a designer can flip. Chosen: the property, because the author rules ask for experiments as an A/B on one setting, and flipping it in the Details panel before and after the fix is the visible proof. Their comments say they exist to prove the point. Reverse cost: low.

### A541 2026-10-21 Pack 2 World 4 has no boss yet; any boss will follow A67 (author-patterns, M4.P2.4)
As A490. Reverse cost: none.

### A540 2026-10-21 Pack 2 World 4 ids (author-patterns, M4.P2.4)
Folder `04-double-buffer`, world id `double-buffer`, quest ids `p4-<slug>`, classes `ADojoP4<Name>`, enum `EDojoP4StrawState` with values `ESS_` (A66, A267), tests `Dojo.P4.<Quest>.<Case>`. Reverse cost: medium once learners have progress saved.

### A365 2026-10-21 The fake's World 1 is built from content/ at build and test time, with no committed copy (M2.2 fix round 4, Lead decision)
A committed `worldOneFixture.json` with a drift test made main's `npm test` fail whenever an author edited World 1. Options: build the fixture from `content/` whenever the app or the tests are built; keep the copy and turn the drift test into a verifier warning. Chosen (Lead): build it. `tools/fake/fakeWorldPlugin.ts` is a Vite plugin (registered in `electron.vite.config.ts` for the renderer and in `vitest.config.ts`) that serves `virtual:dojo-fake-world`; it runs `tools/fake/print-fake-world-fixture.ts` with tsx (the build config itself cannot resolve the app's `@shared` paths) and embeds the JSON in the fake's lazy chunk. If World 1 cannot be built it warns and serves an empty world, so a content mistake never stops the real app from building. The e2e spec reads the same output for the answers it types. What still couples the fake to content: its build judge checks the training dummy's file names and gaps, so renaming that quest's files would fail the fake's tests (wording changes no longer do). `tsconfig.node.json` includes the virtual module's type file. Reverse cost: low.

### A364 2026-10-21 Small edits to ui-core files to open the quest screens (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
The M2.1 screens disabled the ways in until the quest screens existed (A245). Changed: World Map stages open their quest on click (locked stages do not); Studio "Start drill" opens a due drill; a contract's "Test out" opens that world's test-out drill; Studio "Continue" opens the quest; the frame scrolls to the top when the route's query changes (so Next starts at the top); the rail lights World Map on quest and loot screens and Studio on drills. Two assertions in `e2e/ui-core-screens.spec.ts` that expected the disabled buttons now expect them enabled. Options: ask ui-core to make these edits; make them here. Chosen: here, because they are one line each and only make sense with the quest screens. Reverse cost: none.

### A363 2026-10-21 The fake's first review times out when Claude Code is chosen (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
To show and test "Retry review" (A300) the fake needs a review that falls back. Options: a separate fake scenario; a rule inside the existing scenarios. Chosen: with Settings' review provider set to Claude Code, the fake's first code review answers tests-only with the timeout reason and `retryReviewSuggested`; every later review is a normal Claude Code review. Auto, the default, never times out, so other flows are unaffected. Reverse cost: none.

### A362 2026-10-21 Drill keys and endings (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
Cmd/Ctrl+Enter checks the current item and, once checked, moves on; every choice is a native radio (arrow keys), the Parsons lines take Enter to add, Alt+Up and Alt+Down to move, Delete to remove. A miss says "Due for review" and when the item comes back; a mastery drop says the new level "for now, due for review". The Parsons fallback's finish card returns to its quest, every other drill to the Studio. Reverse cost: none.

### A361 2026-10-21 Loot motion and what shows when (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
The spec asks one motion per reward moment. Chosen: the score counts up once (1.5 times the reward duration token), unlocked or upgraded cards flip in (the reward token and easing), and the chest lid opens (ui-core's Chest); everything else is still, and reduced motion zeroes all three through the tokens. The alternative (build) or alternative note (breakdown) shows only after a pass, with the quest's pattern chips (A64). Next goes to the next quest by order in the same world, else the map. Reverse cost: none.

### A360 2026-10-21 Build screen layout at small windows (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
Three columns at 1100 wide leave the editor about 270 pixels. Options: tabs for brief and runs; fold the brief. Chosen: the brief column starts folded to a narrow strip below a 1400 pixel window (a button opens it), the right column is 14 to 16 rem with Run and Submit sticky at its top, Monaco wraps long lines (no sideways scroll to lose your place), and the worked-level reasons can be hidden once read. Autosave: 3 seconds after the last change, and at once on editor blur, window blur, Cmd/Ctrl+S, Run, Submit, and leaving. Reverse cost: low.
Fix round 1: the brief now starts folded below a 1600 pixel window (at 1440 the editor was about 420 pixels wide) and folds itself when the worked-level gaps open; a revealed hint scrolls into view; buttons in the right column wrap instead of widening it, and the column never scrolls sideways.

### A359 2026-10-21 Self-explanation keys and the worked-level gate (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
`QuestProgress.selfExplanations` is keyed by "the marked line's key from the quest content" (A89), but content only gives a line number per file. Chosen: `<file name>:<line>`, for example `DojoF1TrainingDummy.h:13`. Each box needs three words; the gate opens when the learner presses "Open the gaps", and then shows the model reasons. Before that the editor is read-only and Submit is disabled with the reason, but Run works, because the training dummy's brief asks the learner to try the console command before the first gap. A reload opens the gate when every saved explanation counts. Reverse cost: low (the key format is the only thing saved).

### A358 2026-10-21 The Seer Stone offer explains itself but cannot be taken yet (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
After the rules' failed-submit count the build screen offers the Parsons fallback (works, through `startDrill`) and viewing the reference solution. The contract has no method to view a solution or spend a stone. Chosen: the Seer Stone button is disabled with a reason naming M3.3 (the Seer Stone flow) and showing the stones held. Follow-up for app-shell and progress-and-rules: a `viewSolution(questId, useSeerStone)` method answering the reference files and the price. Reverse cost: none.

### A357 2026-10-21 Revealed hint texts last for the window only (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
`revealHint` hands a hint's text out once, and `getQuest` leaves hint texts out (A222), so after a restart the learner sees how many hints were bought but not their words. Chosen for now: the window remembers revealed texts per quest (`useRevealedHints`), and the hint panel says how many were revealed in an earlier session. Follow-up for app-shell: `getQuest` (or a `getRevealedHints(questId)` method) should include the first `hintsRevealed` texts, which are already paid for. Reverse cost: none.
Superseded in fix round 3 by M1.6c (A500 to A502): paid hint texts now come back with the quest as `revealedHints`. The build screen shows those, asks `revealHint(questId, hintIndex)` for the hint after the ones shown, so a stale screen or a double click gets that hint again with `alreadyRevealed` instead of buying another, and the window-only store (`useRevealedHints`) is gone. The fake keeps `revealedHints` indexes on the quest's save entry and answers the same way.

### A356 2026-10-21 Retry review submits the same files again (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
A300 asks for a "Retry review" button but the contract has no method to review an attempt again. Options: wait for a method; submit the same files again. Chosen: submit again with the files of the submit that fell back. The contract records that as another attempt; a repeat of a passed quest pays nothing, so the loot screen keeps the first attempt's loot (XP, cards, skills) and shows the new review and score when the first attempt passed, and shows the retry's own loot when it did not (`mergeRetriedReview.ts`). Side effect: the attempt count rises by one and, before a pass, the retry counts as a failed or passing submit like any other. Follow-up for ai-review and app-shell: a `retryReview(attemptId)` method that reviews the stored attempt without recording a new one. Reverse cost: low.
Fix round 1: after a retry the loot screen keeps the first attempt's score and pass label as well as its loot; the new review shows its own review score beside the reviewer's name.
2026-10-21 (M1.7d, builder-review): the follow-up landed. Retry review now calls `retryReview(attemptId)`, which reviews the stored attempt again with no new attempt (A740, A741); `mergeRetriedReview.ts` was replaced by `withRetriedReview.ts`.

### A355 2026-10-21 Breakdown board details (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
`BreakdownPlan` has no positions. Options: add positions to the shared type; keep them in the window. Chosen: the window; pieces start on a grid about twice as tall as wide, sorted by kind, and keep where the learner dragged them until the screen closes. The keyboard path is the side panel: add a piece by kind, pick it in the list, type its name and required purpose, and draw arrows with a from, meaning, and to form (dragging between card handles does the same with the chosen meaning). Submit needs at least one piece and a name and purpose on every piece; the revision note needs four words. In the compare step both boards sit side by side and the learner's stays editable; pattern chips appear there (A64). A boss opens on its breakdown with the same board; the piece picker and the integration build are left for the boss task. Reverse cost: low.
Fix round 1: below a 1300 pixel window the spec column starts folded to a strip with a toggle (as the build brief, A360), so the board keeps at least about 450 pixels at 1100x700; in the compare step the two boards stack below 1300 wide and sit side by side above it. After a check, Parsons decoys are marked and a real line is marked misplaced only when it falls outside the longest in-order run of the placed lines (decoys removed, identical lines interchangeable, A314). The fake breakdown grader matches required pieces one to one, best shared words first, and ignores words found in most required pieces.
Fix round 2 (Lead decision): stacked boards drew at 36 to 44% zoom below 1300 wide, too small to read. Now (1) the revise panel lists the reference plan as text (each piece's kind and description, and every arrow in words), which is also the screen reader's path to the plan; (2) below 1300 wide each board takes the full width at 20rem high, one above the other, with the revise panel after them (the page scrolls); (3) each board measures its box and picks the column count that shows its pieces largest (for example 4 by 2 for eight pieces in a wide box). An e2e check fails if either board is drawn below 0.6 zoom at 1100x700.
Fix round 4: the piece name field takes focus only for a piece just added from the panel, Submit plan clears the selection, and the compare step opens at its top, so it no longer jumps to the piece form (1100x700) or past the revision note (1440x900). In the revise panel the note and Grade come before the reference plan as text.

### A354 2026-10-21 Concept quest flow (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
`submitConcept` grades every answer in one call, so the questions cannot be graded one by one. Chosen: all questions on one page, Submit once every one has an answer, then each question shows its feedback in place (the explain item's model answer included), then the loot. The predict step reveals the right answer and the explanation in the window from the quest's own answer key (keys reach the renderer, A222); the guess is sent with the submission and never scored. The example editor's Check syntax calls `checkSnippetSyntax`; nothing in it is saved. Reverse cost: low.

### A353 2026-10-21 Monaco is wrapped by hand and loads only what the editor uses (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
`@monaco-editor/react` loads Monaco from a CDN unless configured, and the CSP is `script-src 'self'`. Options: configure its loader with the local package; a small wrapper of our own. Chosen: our own (`components/quests/monaco/`): the editor API, the features we use (hover, suggest, find, folding, comments, brackets, multicursor, word operations, indentation, snippets, read-only message), the C++ language, the codicon font, and the editor worker through Vite's `?worker` (no blob URLs), in a lazy chunk so the app's start never loads it. The theme reads the color tokens. `@monaco-editor/react` stays in devDependencies, unused (package.json is not ours to edit). Measured: the Monaco chunk is about 6 MB and the worker 0.6 MB in the unminified build; first open of a build quest shows a quiet placeholder while it loads. Reverse cost: low.

### A352 2026-10-21 TermText ships with a temporary linker behind the seam (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
M1.8's TermLinker is not on main yet. Options: plain Markdown with no hover terms until it merges; a temporary linker. Chosen: `components/TermText.tsx` renders Markdown and links inline code and bold text that name a glossary term or alias exactly (identifiers case sensitive, plain words in any case, as A223). Prose words are not linked yet. When the TermLinker merges, TermText swaps its matching for the remark plugin and the `glossary-term` element; nothing outside the file changes. `TermText` and `TermHoverCard` are in `components/` because ui-core's spec lists them as shared components; ui-core may take them over. Opening a hover card counts one hover through `recordTermHover`. Reverse cost: none.
Fix round 1: M1.8 merged, so the temporary linker is gone. TermText passes `termLinker` from `src/shared/content/termLinker.ts` (with the alias table from `getGlossaryAliases`) as a remark plugin and maps the `glossary-term` element it emits to `TermHoverCard`, so prose, bold, and inline code link exactly as the verifier and the editor match them. Before the alias table loads, the text renders without links.

### A351 2026-10-21 The fake serves the real Pack 1 World 1 (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
The fake runs in the renderer and cannot read content files with the main-process parser. Options: hand-written quest fixtures; a fixture generated from the real content. Chosen: `tools/write-fake-world-fixture.ts` writes `worldOneFixture.json` with the parser's own output (quests as the renderer receives them, hint texts kept apart for `revealHint`), and a unit test fails when it drifts from `content/`. A new fake scenario `quests` puts the real World 1 on the map in place of the invented one (everything open but the boss); in the other scenarios an invented stage opens the real quest of its kind under its own id and title. Reverse cost: none.
Fix round 1: main's A390 made the fake map's first world mirror the real World 1 with real `f1-*` ids, all passed in the returning save. The `quests` scenario now reuses that mirror (`fixtures/questFlowFixture.ts`) and resets it: every World 1 stage available except the boss, and World 1's quests, attempts, rematches, and pattern card use cases removed from the save (cards left with no use case are dropped), so first passes pay XP, hints cost, and failed submits count.
Fix round 4: the committed fixture and its drift test are gone; the fixture is built from `content/` at build and test time (A365).

### A350 2026-10-21 Quest, drill, and loot routes carry their values in the hash query (M2.2, builder-ui-quests)
The hash router had no parameters. Options: path segments (`#/quest/f1-training-dummy`); a query (`#/quest?id=f1-training-dummy`). Chosen: the query, because `routeFromHash` already ignored it, so no route matching changed. `hashForRoute` takes optional parameters, `routeParametersFromHash` and `useRouteParameters` read them. The loot screen gets the attempt from a small store (a reload of `#/loot` says there is nothing to show), since loot is only shown right after an attempt. Reverse cost: low.

### A413 2026-10-21 Prerequisites are followed transitively (Lead's decision, logged by builder-unreal, M1.3b fix round 2)
A filled boss or piece can itself compile against earlier quests: in a recreated sandbox the World 2 target rack failed because the World 1 boss's director includes `f1-practice-bell`, which nothing had written. Now the run walks every prerequisite's own "Compiles against earlier quests:" line (a code quest's brief, a boss's spec), transitively, each quest once, never the running quest itself, and fills the whole list by the A401, A408, and A409 rules; piece overrides still win. The progress stream's first line lists every quest checked. Reverse cost: low.

### A412 2026-10-21 Short commands run in their own process group and are killed as a tree on the time limit (builder-unreal, M1.3b fix round 2)
A snippet that includes a named pipe made clang wait forever: the 20 s timer killed `xcrun` only, `clang -cc1` kept stderr open, and the runner waited for the pipes to close, so the check never answered and left clang running. `NodeCommandRunner` now starts each command in its own process group (macOS) and on the time limit kills the whole tree with the platform's `ProcessTreeKiller` (taskkill on Windows), drops the pipes, and answers at once with `exitCode: null`. The toolchain and registry queries share this runner. A test runs a real child that leaves a blocking grandchild on stderr. `createProcessTreeKiller` in `createUnrealPlatform.ts` is the one place that picks the killer. Reverse cost: none.

### A411 2026-10-21 The snippet check fails on clang's exit code, maps header errors to snippet lines, and reports timeouts (builder-unreal, M1.3b fix round 1)
The review found that `ok` ignored clang's exit code, so an error inside an engine header (MakeShared of an incomplete type, Cast on a plain struct, a bad Sort lambda, `#include "/etc/hosts"`), a stale precompiled header, and the 20 s timeout all read as a clean pass. Now `ok` needs exit code 0 and no error. A header error is reported on the snippet line from the first "in instantiation of ... requested here" note located in the snippet, else from "In file included from Snippet:N", else line 1, with the header's message and file name. A non-zero exit with no message about the snippet is a line 1 error quoting clang's first error line (fix round 2: not the first output line, which can be an "In file included from" line). A killed run is a line 1 error saying it took longer than 20 seconds. Header warnings stay dropped. Reverse cost: low.

### A410 2026-10-21 Each snippet check uses its own files and removes them (builder-unreal, M1.3b fix round 1)
Options: one check at a time (a queue); a per-check file name. Chosen: per-check names (`Snippet-<uuid>.cpp`, `SnippetPrelude-<uuid>.h`, `Snippet-<uuid>.rsp` under `Intermediate/DojoSnippets`), removed when the check ends, so two quick checks never read each other's code and nothing piles up. Reverse cost: none.

### A409 2026-10-21 Boss prerequisites: boss code without tests, piece overrides win (Lead's decision, logged by builder-unreal, M1.3b fix round 1)
With A215's boss layout (code, `*.Test.cpp`, and `<boss-id>/<piece-id>/` overrides), a quest that compiles against a boss gets: the boss's own code (never its tests) in `Quests/<boss-id>/`, and each override file in its piece's folder. Overrides win over the plain piece reference regardless of the order of the dependency line. Bosses cannot be tested out, so this is the reset case: when the boss's files must be filled and nothing of the boss is saved, its piece overrides replace the learner's piece files in the sandbox for the build, and the learner's own piece code stays in the progress save's `savedFiles` (the next run of that piece writes it back). A boss with saved files gets those back, without overrides; a boss folder with any file is the learner's and is left alone. The A215 layout reader moved from `tools/verifier/bossSolution.ts` to `src/main/services/unreal/bossSolution.ts` (one implementation; it now takes a folder reader so the app passes its own file system), and the verifier imports it from there. Every filled file and its source is named in the progress stream. Proven on the engine with a fixture boss until World 2's boss solution lands. Reverse cost: low.

### A408 2026-10-21 A passed prerequisite's missing files come from the learner's saved code first (Lead's decision, logged by builder-unreal, M1.3b fix round 1)
Amends A401. For a prerequisite the learner passed, only the files missing from its sandbox folder are written: from the progress save's `savedFiles` for that quest when there is any, else from the reference solution. Files already in the sandbox are never touched. A prerequisite the learner has not passed (tested out or never done) still gets its whole reference, as in A401. Reverse cost: low.

### A407 2026-10-21 The dependency line is read by matching known quest ids (Lead's decision, logged by builder-unreal, M1.3b fix round 1)
The first version read quest ids only from `Quests/<id>/` paths on the "Compiles against earlier quests:" line, so "f1-popup-target as extended by f1-boss-training-yard" missed ids written without a path. Now every quest id in the content catalog that appears on the line as a whole word counts, in order of first appearance; an id that is only part of a longer one does not match. Reverse cost: low.

### A406 2026-10-21 Reference solutions ship in the packaged app as `solutions/` (builder-unreal, M1.3b; package.json change allowed by the Lead)
`package.json` `build.extraResources` gains `{ "from": "tools/solutions", "to": "solutions" }`, and the app reads `process.resourcesPath/solutions` when packaged and the source tree's `tools/solutions` in development, the same way the content root resolves. Tradeoff: reference solutions are readable on disk in the installed app, as the content's answer keys already are (A222); viewing one inside the app still costs XP or a Seer Stone. Options: encrypt or pack them; ship them as plain files. Chosen: plain files, because anyone who wants the answer can already read the content folder, and the learner gains nothing in the game by reading them outside it. Verified with `npx electron-builder --mac dir`: `Unreal Dojo.app/Contents/Resources/solutions/<quest-id>/` holds all four World 1 solutions. No dependency changed. Reverse cost: none.

### A405 2026-10-21 `rebuildSandbox` is wired; `openEditor` waits for M1.4 (builder-unreal, M1.3b)
`rebuildSandbox(runId)` runs `SandboxProjectService.rebuild` on the engine and sandbox from settings under the run registry, with progress and cancel like any run. `openEditor` was not small: it needs `EditorLauncher` with the MCP flags, the macOS stale-editor rule (A45), the hard-kill grace period, and on this Mac the editor window does not open until the Metal toolchain is installed. Options: a bare launch now; leave it to M1.4 with the fast loop it belongs to. Chosen: M1.4. Reverse cost: none.

### A404 2026-10-21 The packaged app needs `tools/solutions/` as an extra resource (builder-unreal, M1.3b; done in A406)
A65 makes the app read reference solutions, which `docs/systems/content-engine.md` says are "never loaded by the app" and which are not in `package.json`'s `extraResources`. Options: copy solutions into the content folder; ship `tools/solutions` next to `content` and `resources`. Chosen: ship `tools/solutions` as-is, so the verifier and the app read the same files; the Lead allowed the `package.json` entry, and A406 records it (packaged path `<resources>/solutions/<quest-id>/`). A88's "view reference solution" can read the same folder. Reverse cost: low.

### A403 2026-10-21 The snippet syntax check reuses UBT's own clang response file (builder-unreal, M1.3b)
A89 left the exact command to this task. Options: a hand-made clang command with guessed include paths; a tiny build of the snippet through UBT (a full build, 10 s or more); the response file UBT wrote for a sandbox source. Chosen: the third. `DojoTestActor.cpp.o.rsp` (always in the sandbox) holds the module's flags, include paths (relative to `Engine/Source`, so clang runs from there), and the shared precompiled header; the check drops `-c`, `-o`, `-MD`, colors, `-Werror`, and the donor source, then adds a prelude header, `-fsyntax-only`, and the snippet, through `xcrun clang++`. About 0.5 s. Reflection needs code UHT generates, so the prelude blanks `UCLASS`, `UINTERFACE`, and the `GENERATED_*` macros (`UPROPERTY` and `UFUNCTION` are already empty outside UHT), `#include "*.generated.h"` lines are blanked without moving line numbers, `Super::` is blanked with as many spaces (without GENERATED_BODY it would fall through to `AActor`'s own `Super`, `UObject`, and report a false error; found in the engine run), `-Wno-pragma-once-outside-header` is added because scratch buffers are often headers, `-Wno-infinite-recursion` because a blanked `Super::BeginPlay()` reads as a call to itself, and messages about `Super`, `ThisClass`, `StaticClass`, or `.generated.h` are dropped. Only diagnostics in the snippet itself are returned. The sandbox must have been built once (`sandbox-not-ready` otherwise). Windows (`cl /Zs` with the `.obj.rsp`) is not built yet and answers `not-implemented` (A402). Reverse cost: low.

### A402 2026-10-21 No Windows snippet check yet (builder-unreal, M1.3b)
The Windows response file does not name `cl.exe`, and nothing can be verified on this Mac. Options: guess the MSVC path from vswhere now; answer `not-implemented` until the Windows run. Chosen: `not-implemented` with a plain message ("use Run on a build quest for now"); the renderer already shows that error. Reverse cost: one class.

### A401 2026-10-21 Which prerequisite files come from the reference, and which files (builder-unreal, M1.3b; amended by A407, A408, A409)
A65 says a skipped or reset prerequisite is filled with its reference solution. The progress save has no "tested out" flag per quest, and a quest reset only clears the saved draft, not the sandbox. Options: track test-out and reset per quest; decide from what the run can see. Chosen: for each quest on the brief's "Compiles against earlier quests:" line (A268), the reference solution goes in when the learner has not passed that quest themselves (tested out, or never done) or when its sandbox folder is missing any file the reference has; a passed quest with all its files keeps the learner's code. The whole reference folder is written (header and `.cpp`), not only the header the line names, because the later quest links against the classes too. The prerequisite's own tests are not written; only the run quest's tests run. Each filled quest is one `writing` progress line naming the files and the reason. A missing reference solution is a pipeline error. Reverse cost: low.

### A400 2026-10-21 The engine folder is saved with the sandbox, through the settings service (builder-unreal, M1.3b; updated in fix round 1)
`runQuest` gets only a quest id and files, so it needs the engine and sandbox from settings. Options: add an engine path to every run request; save it at sandbox creation. Chosen: `createSandbox` also saves `enginePath`, through `SettingsServiceUnrealSettings` (the pipeline's `UnrealSettingsStore` over the app's `SettingsService`, A426; the first version's temporary electron-store adapter is deleted), and runs, the snippet check, and rebuilds read both; before setup they answer `sandbox-not-ready`. Reverse cost: none; the settings service takes the keys over.

### A520 2026-10-21 A missing boss integration is an error for pack bosses; the format example boss stays a warning (M1.8c, builder-content-engine)
Both Pack 1 bosses now ship A67 integrations, so `MISSING_BOSS_INTEGRATION_IS_ERROR` is true (A215). The format example boss `example-boss-lighthouse` has none, and flipping the rule for it would fail every verifier run on main. Options: write a lighthouse integration now (about 500 to 700 lines of C++ and a two-round test, like the Pack 1 bosses, which is more than this small task); delete the example boss; keep the example as a warning. Chosen: the example stays a warning, marked "format example" in the message, until it gets an integration as its own follow-up. Pack bosses get no exemption. The A68 gate check is not part of M1.8c: builder-rules adds it in M1.6d. Reverse cost: none.

### A511 2026-10-21 The verifier warns about a pack gate whose world is missing or has no boss (M1.6d, builder-rules)
A68 asks the verifier to report a gate naming a world id that does not exist. Options: tell content-engine to add it; add a check through the verifier's check extension point. Chosen: the second, as one new file, `tools/verifier/checks/packGateCheck.ts`, reading `PACK_GATES` from `rules.ts` so the rule and the check cannot drift, plus one line registering it in `tools/verifier/runVerifier.ts` (content-engine's folder; the only edit there). It reports each gate world that is missing, whose pack is missing, or that has no boss: a warning naming the pack's `pack.json` (or `packs` when the pack itself is missing), and an error with `--release`, like the coverage rules (A205). An empty content folder has nothing to gate and stays clean. Today it prints two warnings (Communication and Component are not written yet). Reverse cost: none.

### A510 2026-10-21 The Pack 4 gate names `foundations/communication` and `patterns/component` (M1.6d, builder-rules)
Per A68 the gate moves from world order to world id. World ids follow the existing folders (`objects-and-reflection`, `memory-and-ownership`, `time`, `update-method`, one world per pattern per A491): Pack 1 world 5 in product.md is "communication (delegates, interfaces, timers, Tick)", so its id is `communication`; the Pack 2 world for the Component pattern is `component`. Authors of those worlds use exactly these ids, or change `PACK_GATES` with them. Each required world is named with its pack id because world ids are only unique inside a pack. The gated pack stays named by order (Pack 4, Systems Lab, has no folder or id yet; pack order is fixed by product.md). A required world with no boss in the loaded content keeps Pack 4 locked, with "the component world's boss (not released yet)" in the locked reason, so nothing unlocks until both worlds exist and both bosses are passed. Supersedes the order part of A131. Reverse cost: low.

### A503 2026-10-21 Two small edits outside progress-and-rules for the revealed hints (M1.6c, builder-rules)
The new required `QuestProgress.revealedHints` field broke the renderer's fake progress fixture, so `src/renderer/src/data/fake/fixtures/progressFixture.ts` gains one line (`[0]` where it set one hint revealed, else `[]`). The shared contract changed in three small places: `revealHint(questId, hintIndex?)` in `src/shared/ipc.ts`, `hintIndexSchema` (0 to 2, optional) in `src/main/ipc/requestSchemas.ts`, and the optional `revealedHints` on build and refactor `LearnerQuest` in `src/shared/types/catalog.ts`. `getQuest` and `getWorld` now answer through `ProgressContentService`, which reads the catalog and adds the revealed texts. Options: ask each owner first; make the edits and name them here. Chosen: the second, since each is a type ripple of this task. Reverse cost: none.

### A502 2026-10-21 Revealed hint texts come back with the quest, and only those (M1.6c, builder-rules)
A paid hint vanished after a restart because its text only ever arrived in the `revealHint` answer. Options: a new `getRevealedHints(questId)` method; add the revealed texts to the quest data. Chosen: the second (one fewer call, and the quest screen already loads the quest). `getQuest` and `getWorld` add `revealedHints` (texts in reveal order) to build and refactor quests; an unrevealed hint still never leaves main (A222). Reverse cost: low. Fix round 1: the revealed indexes are read through `ProgressService.getRevealedHintIndexes(questId)`, one quest at a time, which answers an empty list on a refused save (A133), so `getQuest` and `getWorld` keep working when the save cannot be opened.

### A501 2026-10-21 `revealHint` is idempotent with an optional hint index (M1.6c, builder-rules)
Options: keep `revealHint(questId)` revealing the next hint (a second call after a reload charges for a new one); add an optional index. Chosen: `revealHint(questId, hintIndex?)`. With an index already revealed, it answers that hint again with `alreadyRevealed: true` and changes nothing, so a reload or double click never charges twice. With the next index, or no index, it reveals the next hint as before. A skipped index is refused (hints stay in order). `xpCost` stays the hint's price; the answer says whether it was already paid. Reverse cost: low.

### A500 2026-10-21 Revealed hints are stored as indexes; save version 2 (M1.6c, builder-rules)
`QuestProgress` gains `revealedHints: number[]` (indexes in reveal order); `hintsRevealed` stays as its length so the XP rules and the renderer keep reading a count. The save version goes from 1 to 2 with `migrateSave.ts`: a version 1 quest gets `revealedHints` = 0 to hintsRevealed - 1, since hints were always revealed in order, and is written back as version 2 on the next save. Options: a zod default (cannot derive from the count); a versioned migration step. Chosen: the migration, the first one, which later schema changes extend. An older app opening a version 2 save refuses it without overwriting (A133). Reverse cost: low.

A470 to A477 are World 2's (on its branch); A390 and A420 to A429 are used on main. Chosen: A490 to A509, unused on main and on every patterns branch. Reverse cost: none.

The update-method card lists `p3-patrol-drone` and `p3-spark-swarm` as use cases and gains `update-method-recall-4` (when children born mid-walk first move) and `update-method-scenario-3` (a when-not multiple-choice item: listen instead of polling). Reverse cost: low.

### A494 2026-10-21 World 3's deliberate failure: sparks born during the walk must wait a step (author-patterns, Pack 2 World 3)
Options: removal during the walk (an obvious `RemoveAt` skips a neighbor, but a ranged-for loop would trip the engine's array-changed check and stop the test run, so it is asked for as a plain requirement instead); newborn sparks moving in the step they were born. Chosen: newborns. The obvious index loop to `Sparks.Num()` moves the children in the same step; checked on 5.8.3, that version fails `NewSparksWaitAStep` ("moved 3, expected 1"; "child at 200, expected 100") and also `LaunchBurstsToTheEnd`, because the children burst again in the same one-second step. Both failures name the same mistake, so the pair is kept. Reverse cost: low.

### A493 2026-10-21 The arena refactor keeps the director as a roster (author-patterns, Pack 2 World 3)
Tests must compile against both the starter and the refactor, and the starter only behaves when a director lists the things. Options: delete the director (tests could not include it); keep it as a roster with no Tick and no per-thing state. Chosen: keep the two lists (the brief says a later scoreboard reads them). Tests: `LookUnchanged` (with a director) passes on the starter; `EachThingUpdatesItself` (no director) and `DirectorRunsNobody` (director does not tick, nothing runs twice) fail on it. These tests tick the world (`FDojoTestWorld::Tick`), with a one-frame tolerance for when ticking starts. Reverse cost: low.

### A492 2026-10-21 Update Method mini-arc (author-patterns, Pack 2 World 3)
Arc: `p3-per-frame-behavior` (concept, no pattern name: slicing behavior into frames, resume state, sequential order, prerequisites, idling), `p3-patrol-drone` (worked: an actor's own Tick with a stored heading and overshoot folded back, even past both ends in one frame), `p3-update-method-card`, `p3-spark-swarm` (stub: one actor walks a plain struct array, newborns wait a step, burst sparks are swept after the walk), `p3-arena-refactor` (a director that ran every brazier and sweeper through parallel arrays hands each its own Tick), `p3-update-method-when-not` (listen instead of polling, timers for schedules, stop idle ticking, one walk for thousands, never trust placement for order). Mechanics: patrol, firework, arena hazards. Reverse cost: medium.

A472 planned Update Method and Double Buffer as later orders inside World 2. The Lead's instruction for this task is "World 3, the next pattern in Part 3 order". ### A490 2026-10-21 Pack 2 World 3 has no boss yet; any boss will follow A67 (author-patterns, Pack 2 World 3)
As A380 and A470. A boss in this world would be a reference integration with two-round tests per A67; the natural one combines Update Method with Game Loop and Observer (an arena run with a round clock), and is better as its own task. Reverse cost: none.

### A477 2026-10-21 Decision numbers A470 to A489 for Pack 2 World 2 (author-patterns)
The Lead offered A390 upward, but A390 and A420 to A429 are already used on main by other sessions. Chosen: A470 to A489, a range nobody had touched, so parallel sessions do not collide. Reverse cost: none.

### A476 2026-10-21 One new glossary entry, and the time terms point at World 2 (author-patterns, Pack 2 World 2)
Added `physics-substepping` (category physics, Epic's 5.8 "Physics Sub-Stepping" page, title checked live), used by the card and the when-not quest. `firstQuestId` set to `p2-frame-time` on `tick`, `deltatime`, and `tick-groups`, the first quest that teaches them today; a Pack 1 world that teaches Tick later moves them. The game-loop card lists its two builds as use cases and gains `game-loop-recall-4` (what to do with time the cap refuses) and `game-loop-scenario-3` (a when-not multiple-choice item). Reverse cost: low.

### A475 2026-10-21 Builds feed time through Advance; the refactor calls Tick directly (author-patterns, Pack 2 World 2)
Frame-rate tests need exact control over each frame's length. Options: tick the whole test world with chosen deltas; call a public `Advance(DeltaSeconds)` that Tick forwards to; call the actor's `Tick` directly. Chosen: the two builds expose `Advance` (Tick only forwards and draws), and the refactor keeps the starter's shape, so its tests call `Tick` directly. Every delta in the tests is a power-of-two fraction or an exact multiple of the step (1/64, 1/8, 1/4 against a 1/32 or 1/16 step; 1/30 and 1/120 against 1/60), so the expected results are exact and the tests compare angles and distances to the last bit where the quest promises "to the last digit". Reverse cost: low.

### A474 2026-10-21 World 2's deliberate failure is a capped bank that keeps the refused time (author-patterns, Pack 2 World 2)
Options: an uncapped bank (the blade already runs one, so the failure would be unannounced in a worked quest); floating-point drift (not reliable enough to fail the same way for everyone); a hitch that the cap limits but whose extra time is kept. Chosen: the replay ghost. Its brief says a hitch must not turn into a sprint, and the test makes one `Advance` call carrying three seconds, then one normal frame. A real frame never reaches Tick that long by default (World Settings' Max Undilated Frame Time caps it at 0.4 s), so the brief says the guard is for direct calls and projects that raised the cap (corrected in fix round 1). The obvious version (no cap) plays all 32 steps at once; a cap without dropping the leftover plays a second burst on the next frame. Both fail only `HitchDoesNotTurnIntoASprint` (checked on 5.8.3 with the uncapped version). The blade's worked starter has no cap, and the card does not mention one, so the ghost is where the learner meets it; the when-not quest then states the rule. Reverse cost: low.

### A473 2026-10-21 Game Loop mini-arc: what the learner builds when the engine owns the loop (author-patterns, Pack 2 World 2)
Unreal runs the outer loop, so the learner cannot build it. Options: teach only DeltaSeconds; have the learner write the loop's time policy, a fixed-step bank inside the frame. Chosen: the time policy, which is the hand-written Unreal form named in the research. Arc: a frame-time concept quest that never names the pattern; the swinging blade (worked, a damped pendulum stepped at 1/32 s with display blending); the card quest that names Game Loop; the replay ghost (stub, a recorded run replayed by fixed steps with playback rate and a catch-up cap); the boulder refactor (a per-frame drag tuned at 60 fps moved onto a 1/60 s step without changing the 60 fps roll); and a when-not quest (no second loop, no sleeping on the game thread, DeltaSeconds for ordinary rates, physics substepping and timers first). Three mechanics: trap physics, replay, rolling hazard. Reverse cost: medium.

### A472 2026-10-21 World 2 is "Time" and holds Game Loop now, Update Method and Double Buffer later (author-patterns, Pack 2 World 2)
Following A370: World 2 is the Time world. Options: one world per pattern; the three time patterns in one world. Chosen: one world, `02-time`, with the Game Loop arc at orders 1 to 6; Update Method and Double Buffer append later at order 7 and up. Because the fade check covers the whole world, the Game Loop builds go worked then stub, and later builds in this world must be stub or blank. Quest ids `p2-<slug>`, classes `ADojoP2<Name>`, tests `Dojo.P2.<Quest>.<Case>` (A372). Reverse cost: low.

Time does not depend on Observer in the teaching order. Options: make World 2 follow the end of World 1; make it follow lesson zero only. Chosen: `p2-frame-time` requires `p1-architecture-lens`, so a learner can start Time without finishing Observer, and the world map's own world order still suggests World 1 first. ### A470 2026-10-21 Pack 2 World 2 has no breakdown or boss yet (author-patterns, Pack 2 World 2)
As with A380: a boss for Time fits once Update Method and Double Buffer are in the world (a replay and ghost-racer boss from the research's combos needs Command too). Left for a later task. Reverse cost: none.

### A491 2026-10-21 The MCP port is checked before the editor starts (builder-unreal, M1.4 fix round 1)
Unreal's MCP server does not fail when its port is taken, so the client could end up talking to another editor and report another sandbox's verdict. Options: check after launch that the server answers for our project; check before launch that nothing listens. Chosen: before launch, `PortProbe` (a one-second TCP connect to 127.0.0.1) runs after our own editor is closed; if anything accepts, `launch` throws `editor-launch-failed` "Port N is already in use, so the editor's MCP server cannot start there." with "Close the other Unreal editor or program using it, or change the MCP port in Settings." and starts nothing. A program that grabs the port in the second between the check and the editor's start is not caught. Reverse cost: none.

### A490 2026-10-21 One RunResult builder for both loops (builder-unreal, M1.4)
`HeadlessRunner` had its own private function that fills a `RunResult`. Options: copy it into the fast runner; move it to `runResultOf.ts` and use it in both. Chosen: move it, so `ok` means the same in both loops. Reverse cost: none.

### A489 2026-10-21 Fast loop built and tested, wired into the app after M1.3b merges (builder-unreal, M1.4; wired in M1.4b, A530 to A535)
M1.3b's fix round is editing `createUnrealServices.ts`, `createHandlers.ts`, and `src/main/index.ts`. Options: wire now and conflict with it; build beside it and wire after it merges. Chosen: build beside it. After the rebase onto M1.3b: `createUnrealServices` creates the one `FullBuildLedger`, returns it as `buildLedger`, and passes `PrerequisiteFiller` a listener that calls `recordSandboxWrite(questId, files, replaceFolder)` after each folder it writes (a partial write keeps the folder's other files). `RunnerFactory` takes the fast loop pieces as an optional second argument, so today's call stays valid. Left for the wiring step: pass the pieces in `createUnrealServices` (with a `NodePortProbe`); answer `openEditor` with `EditorLauncher`; report `editorConnected` and a stale editor in `getEnvironmentStatus`; record sandbox create and rebuild in the ledger (`recordSandboxBuild`); call `recordSandboxWrite(questId, null)` when a quest reset removes its sandbox files (nothing calls `QuestFileWriter.remove` yet); and call `stopEditorBeforeQuit(app, editorLauncher)` in `src/main/index.ts`. That hook holds the quit while the editor closes (up to the 15 second grace, longer than the run registry's 5 seconds) and disposes the launcher, so `LoopSelectingRunner`'s background `reopen()` cannot start an editor once quit began; the run registry's own `before-quit` handler and this one should be checked together at wiring, since either one's `quit()` re-fires the other (fix round 1). Reverse cost: none.

### A488 2026-10-21 Cancel during a fast run takes effect between steps (builder-unreal, M1.4)
Options: abort the HTTP request and call `StopTests`; let the call in flight finish and stop before the next step. Chosen: the second. RunTests answers in about 0.1 s on the Mac, a Live Coding compile cannot be stopped from outside, and an aborted call would leave the editor busy for the next run. Reverse cost: low.

### A487 2026-10-21 Closing the editor: exit request, 15 seconds, then kill; no kill after a clean exit (builder-unreal, M1.4)
Order (fix round 1): the exit request and the 15 second kill are scheduled first, then the client is closed at once, without waiting behind a queued call, so a call in flight rejects instead of holding the close for up to 10 minutes. `ProcessTreeKiller` and `RunningProcess` gain `requestExit`: SIGTERM to the editor process on macOS, `taskkill /PID <id> /T` without `/F` on Windows (asks its windows to close; unverified on Windows, where an unsaved-changes prompt could hold it until the kill). After 15 seconds the tree is killed. Options for after a clean exit: kill the process group anyway to clear helpers; leave it. Chosen: leave it, because the zen storage server the editor may start is shared with any other editor on the machine. Reverse cost: none.

### A486 2026-10-21 Every fast loop failure falls back to the headless loop with a reason (builder-unreal, M1.4)
Options: report editor problems as run errors; fall back. Chosen: fall back when the editor stops answering, any editor tool call fails, Live Coding answers `unavailable`, `busy`, or `needs-full-build`, or RunTests does not list the quest's tests (new test code the editor has not loaded). A Live Coding compile that fails with errors is a real compile failure and is returned as one. The learner always gets a verdict; the cost is a slower run. Reverse cost: low.

### A485 2026-10-21 CompileLiveCoding's answer is read defensively (builder-unreal, M1.4)
Its answer format could not be seen on the Mac, where it only says "Live Coding is not available in this build configuration." Options: wait for Windows; read it defensively now. Chosen: look for an `ELiveCodingCompileResult` name (`Success`, `NoChanges`, `InProgress`, `CompileStillActive`, `NotStarted`, `Failure`, `Cancelled`) in a `result` or `status` field, then for words in the text; anything unrecognised counts as needing a full build. Unverified on Windows; the first Windows run should log the raw answer and tighten `LiveCodingAnswer.ts`. Reverse cost: one file.

### A484 2026-10-21 Only an editor the app opened is used for the fast loop (builder-unreal, M1.4)
Options: also detect an editor the learner opened by hand on port 8000; only use the app's own. Chosen: only the app's own. The stale-editor rule needs the launch time, the client needs the port, and an editor opened by hand may be on another project. Reverse cost: medium (process discovery per platform).

### A483 2026-10-21 Windows: the open editor is closed before a full build and reopened after (builder-unreal, M1.4)
With Live Coding active, UnrealBuildTool refuses to build modules the editor has loaded. Options: fail the build with "close the editor"; close and reopen it automatically. Chosen: close, build, reopen in the background, so the next small change is fast again. The Mac leaves the editor open (it can build alongside, A45) and marks it stale instead. Unverified on Windows. Reverse cost: low, one branch in `LoopSelectingRunner`.

### A482 2026-10-21 Windows fast loop rule: no new or removed files and no reflected header changed (builder-unreal, M1.4)
The spec says "the diff since the last successful full build touches no header of a reflected type". Options: any header change needs a full build; only headers with reflection macros do; parse which types changed. Chosen: a changed `.h` whose old or new text contains a reflection macro needs a full build; plain headers and `.cpp` files go through Live Coding; any added or removed file, changed tests, or no full build of this quest yet this session also needs one. Live Coding can still refuse a plain layout change, which A486 then catches. Reverse cost: one regular expression.

### A481 2026-10-21 Mac stale-editor rule kept in an in-memory full-build record (builder-unreal, M1.4)
`FullBuildLedger` records each successful full build: when it finished and, per quest, the files and tests it compiled. On the Mac the fast loop runs only when the run's files and tests equal that record and the build finished before the editor opened; any build after the editor opened makes it stale until it restarts. Fix round 1: the record is per quest, but `PrerequisiteFiller` and a quest reset change other quests' folders, so the editor could run tests against code it did not load (Mac) or Live Coding could compile a rewritten reflected header unchecked (Windows). The ledger now also tracks every quest's files as the app wrote them and as they stood at the last full build; `recordSandboxWrite` updates the first, and any difference forces the headless loop on both platforms until the next full build (a rewrite with identical content is not a difference, so a quest with prerequisites can still use the fast loop). On both platforms the quest's record must also come from the latest full build (`builtAt` equals `lastFullBuildAt`): a later build can absorb a sandbox write to this quest's folder (for example its reference with a new `UPROPERTY`), and on Windows Live Coding would otherwise compare the draft against a header the editor no longer has (re-review; the Lead preferred this over diffing against the built files). Options: in memory; on disk. Chosen: in memory, so after an app restart the first run of a quest is a full build (about 25 s), which is always correct. Reverse cost: low.

### A480 2026-10-21 RunnerFactory hands out a loop-choosing runner (builder-unreal, M1.4)
Choosing the loop needs async checks (the MCP health call, the engine version), but `RunnerFactory.forContext` is synchronous and M1.3b calls it. Options: make `forContext` async and change its callers; return an `IRunner` that chooses when `run` starts. Chosen: the second (`LoopSelectingRunner`), which also gives one place that announces the loop and reason once and records builds. Reverse cost: low.

### A381 2026-10-21 One cross-pack prerequisite, and Pack 2 stays open from the start (author-patterns, M3.1)
Pack 2's first quest is always available (progress rules), so a learner can reach it after Pack 1 World 1 only, before Pack 1 World 5 teaches delegates. Options: gate Pack 2 behind Pack 1 World 5; teach the delegate forms inside Pack 2; both. Chosen: Pack 2 World 1 teaches the delegate forms itself (`p1-multicast-delegates`), and that quest lists `f1-ufunction-specifiers` as a prerequisite, because `AddDynamic` needs UFUNCTION handlers. The verifier treats a prerequisite outside the pack as satisfied for reachability, but that is only the quest graph: the unlock rule also needs every listed term at Familiar, so the bounty board, and every quest after it, opens only once `isvalid`, `beginplay`, and `tobjectptr` (Pack 1 World 1 terms) are Familiar, and in practice a learner arrives with World 1 done. Corrected in fix round 1; the first version said only the prerequisite mattered. Reverse cost: one line.

### A380 2026-10-21 Pack 2 World 1 has no breakdown or boss yet (author-patterns, M3.1)
M3.1 asks for lesson zero and the Observer arc. Options: add a breakdown and boss now; leave them for a later task. Chosen: later. The natural boss is a Part 4 combo, and every combo needs a pattern this world does not have yet; a world-1-only Observer boss (an arena event board with several listeners) can be added as its own task. Reverse cost: none.

### A379 2026-10-21 Design words are not glossary entries (author-patterns, M3.1)
Lesson zero teaches coupling, decoupling, and the three speeds. Options: add `coupling` and similar entries; keep them in the card. Chosen: keep them in the card, because a glossary entry needs an Epic docs link and Epic has no page for these. Lesson zero lists only `forward-declaration`, which its card and example use (fix round 1 removed `coding-standard`, which the quest never taught). No new glossary entries were needed for the Observer arc: every delegate term already existed. `firstQuestId` is set on `multicast-delegate`, `dynamic-multicast-delegate`, `broadcast`, `adddynamic`, `unbinding-delegates`, `blueprintassignable`, and `endplay` (added in fix round 1) to `p1-multicast-delegates`, the first quest that teaches them today; when Pack 1 World 5 lands, its author moves them. Reverse cost: seven fields.

### A378 2026-10-21 Observer card additions and use cases (author-patterns, M3.1)
Options for `useCaseQuestIds`: the two builds; the builds plus the refactor. Chosen: the two build quests (`p1-bounty-board`, `p1-tutorial-sign`), since the verifier's use-case rule counts build quests. Added to the card: `observer-recall-4` (fill-blank on AddUniqueDynamic) and `observer-scenario-3`, a when-not item written as multiple-choice as the authoring rules ask. Existing items untouched. Reverse cost: none.

### A377 2026-10-21 The refactor's tests check structure through reflection (author-patterns, M3.1)
A refactor must keep behavior, so a behavior test passes on the starter, and something must still fail until the refactor is done. Options: tests that call the new member directly (the starter would not compile); reflection checks. Chosen: reflection. One test finds a BlueprintAssignable `OnPowerChanged` multicast delegate property and walks every property of the generator to fail on any that points at a lamp or an alarm, which is the dependency direction itself as a test. Another reads the bound objects through `FMulticastDelegateProperty::GetMulticastDelegate` to prove a destroyed lamp unbinds. The starter compiles, passes `BehaviorUnchanged`, and fails the other two. Reverse cost: low.

### A376 2026-10-21 The world's deliberate failure is a listener wired twice (author-patterns, M3.1)
Options: broadcasting before the state changes (most learners would not write it that way); forgetting to unbind (already taught in the worked build); a listener wired from the Details panel and again from the level Blueprint. Chosen: wired twice. The obvious `AddDynamic` adds a second copy and the handler runs twice; the fix is `AddUniqueDynamic`. On 5.8 a duplicate `AddDynamic` also trips an `ensure` inside the engine, so the failing test's report opens with that message before the count assertion; it is accurate and is left in, and the first hint mentions it. Verified: the reference solution with plain `AddDynamic` fails only `WiredTwiceHearsOnce`. The brief warns that one test fails on purpose and the hints name the cause only from the first rung. Reverse cost: low.

### A375 2026-10-21 Tests spawn listeners deferred, so designer fields exist before BeginPlay (author-patterns, M3.1)
FDojoTestWorld runs BeginPlay as an actor spawns, so a test cannot fill a designer-picked list before the actor binds in BeginPlay. Options: a public bind function the test calls (an API only tests need); `SpawnActorDeferred` plus `FinishSpawning`, which is how a placed actor gets its Details values before play. Chosen: deferred spawn in the tests, with a small helper in each test file's namespace. The tutorial sign also has a public `Watch` because the level Blueprint is meant to call it. Reverse cost: low.

### A374 2026-10-21 Two builds go worked then stub; the refactor is off the ladder (author-patterns, M3.1)
The fade check requires a world's first build to be worked or faded and its last to be stub or blank, and skips refactors. With exactly two builds, Options: worked and faded (fails the check); worked and stub; faded and blank. Chosen: worked (the bounty board, two small gaps and five marked lines) then stub (the tutorial sign, declarations given). The refactor is marked stub. Reverse cost: low.

### A373 2026-10-21 The pattern is named only from the card quest on (author-patterns, M3.1)
The mini-arc order says "predict and card" first, but the author rule says to name a pattern right after it is built. Options: card first and break the naming rule; build first. Chosen: a delegates concept quest that never says Observer, then the worked build, then the card quest that names what was just built, then the second build, the refactor, and the when-not quest. Starters before the card do not name the pattern in class comments. The reference solutions do, as game-dev-rules asks of class comments, because a solution is only shown after an attempt. Reverse cost: low.

### A372 2026-10-21 Pack 2 ids and names (author-patterns, M3.1)
Following A261. Chosen: pack `patterns`, world folder `<nn>-<world-id>`, quest ids `p<world>-<slug>` (`p1-bounty-board`), classes `ADojoP<world><Name>`, tests `Dojo.P<world>.<Quest>.<Case>` with structs `FDojo_P<world>_<Quest>_<Case>`. "P" for patterns the way "F" is foundations; later packs would take M and S. Reverse cost: medium once learners have saved progress.

### A371 2026-10-21 Two Unreal forms across the two builds (author-patterns, M3.1)
Options: both builds on dynamic delegates; both native; one of each. Chosen: one of each, in different mechanics. The bounty board (arena combat) uses a native multicast delegate with AddUObject and RemoveAll in EndPlay; the tutorial sign (interaction and tutorials) uses a dynamic multicast delegate in a BlueprintAssignable property with AddUniqueDynamic and RemoveDynamic, including removal from inside the handler. The refactor (power and devices) uses the dynamic form so its structure can be checked by reflection (A377). Reverse cost: low.

### A370 2026-10-21 Pack 2 World 1 holds lesson zero and the Observer arc (author-patterns, M3.1)
The product lists Pack 2 worlds as lens, time, communication, and so on, with Observer in the communication world, but M3.1 asks for lesson zero and Observer together in World 1, and a world with only one concept quest has no builds to fade and no boss. Options: World 1 lens only and Observer in World 3 with World 2 empty for now; one World 1 with both. Chosen: one world, `01-lens-and-observer`. Observer has no pattern prerequisite in the teaching order (Event Queue and Component depend on it, and both stay later), it is the pattern Unreal hands over most completely, and it gives lesson zero's "flexibility is a bet" an immediate payoff and an immediate "when not". World 2 stays Time; World 3 becomes Command and Event Queue, so Pack 2 World 4 is still behavior and modes and the Pack 4 gate (A131) keeps its meaning. Cost: the Observer to Event Queue contrast spans two worlds; World 3 should open with an Observer recall item. `docs/product.md` and the Pack 2 brief in `docs/systems/content-authoring.md` are updated to match. Reverse cost: move six quest files to a World 3 folder and renumber.
2026-10-21 (author-patterns, fix round of M4.P2.3): superseded in part by A491. World 1 stays lesson zero plus Observer, but World 2 holds Game Loop only and every later pattern is its own world, so World 3 is Update Method, not Command and Event Queue.

### A443 2026-10-21 Each starter check runs in the sandbox a learner would have at that quest (M1.8b, builder-content-engine; Lead request)
Round 2 removed every boss and restored the original pieces before the starter checks. A later quest that uses a boss's extended piece (the World 2 target rack calls `ResetForRound`, which only the World 1 boss's override of the pop-up target has, A340) then fails to build in its starter check for the wrong reason. Options: keep every boss and override in place for every starter check (then a piece's own starter breaks the boss and every later quest, so piece checks become inconclusive); set the sandbox per check. Chosen: per check, the sandbox holds the quest's starter, the solutions of everything it needs (prerequisites followed through every kind of quest, the quests on its "Compiles against earlier quests:" line, and a boss's pieces), and the overrides of every boss among those; everything else is removed. Quests whose files did not change are not rewritten, so UBT recompiles only what moved. The solution pass still builds everything with every override. Reverse cost: low.

### A442 2026-10-21 Two quests may not share a file name (M1.8b, builder-content-engine)
Found while adding bare-name include checks: with every `Quests/*` folder on the include path (A181), `#include "DojoExLantern.h"` resolves to whichever folder the compiler searches first when two quests both have a `DojoExLantern.h`. Options: allow it and hope; report it. Chosen: an error on the later quest ("rename one"). File names count from build and refactor quests' files and from a boss's top-level integration code; a boss's piece overrides do not, since they replace the piece's files in the piece's own folder. Reverse cost: none.

### A441 2026-10-21 A bare include of another quest's file is a dependency (M1.8b, builder-content-engine; Lead request)
The dependency check matched only `Quests/<id>/` paths, but A181 puts every quest folder on the include path, so `#include "DojoF1TrainingDummy.h"` from another quest compiles. Chosen: each code quest's file names map to its id, and a bare `#include "<name>"` of another quest's file needs the same A268 line (naming that quest with its `Quests/<id>/` path) and prerequisite as a path include. Engine headers never match because no quest owns them. Reverse cost: none.

### A440 2026-10-21 Ctrl+C and SIGTERM cancel `--engine` (M1.8b, builder-content-engine; Lead request)
