With M1.8's reference and unlock checks, reading one pack alone would report every cross-pack prerequisite as missing and miss duplicate ids. Options: keep reading one pack and skip cross-pack checks; read everything and filter the output. Chosen: read everything, report only files under `packs/<id>/`; under `--engine` only that pack's quests are judged, though every solution is still built. Reading all content takes well under a second. Reverse cost: low.

### A205 2026-10-21 Coverage rules are summary warnings, and `--release` makes them errors (M1.8, builder-content-engine)
The spec says glossary and pattern coverage warn "until the curriculum is complete, error at release". With 191 terms and no packs yet, one line per term would bury every real problem. Options: one warning per id; one summary warning per rule; hide warnings by default. Chosen: one summary warning per rule (count plus the first 12 ids), exit code unaffected; `--release` lists every missing id as its own error. `ContentProblem` gained `severity` (absent means error). Reverse cost: low.

### A204 2026-10-21 Fade ladder details (M1.8, builder-content-engine)
The spec says first worked or faded, last stub or blank, never a step back. Open points decided: only `kind: build` quests are on the ladder (a refactor starts from working code, so its scaffold means something else); the first and last rule applies to worlds with two or more build quests, since one quest cannot be both the start and the end of a fade; the no-step-back rule always applies. Reverse cost: low.

### A203 2026-10-21 What "reachable from a pack's first quest" means (M1.8, builder-content-engine)
Options: graph reachability only (any quest with no prerequisites is a root); unlock semantics with one root per pack. Chosen: unlock semantics (a quest unlocks when all its prerequisites have), and inside a pack only the first quest of the first world may have no prerequisites, so every other quest hangs off it. Cycles are reported with their path; a quest stuck behind a cycle is reported once. Pack quests may require quests in other packs but never examples. Reverse cost: low.

### A202 2026-10-21 The TermLinker and matcher live in `src/shared/content/` (M1.8, builder-content-engine)
The spec puts content-engine code in `src/main/services/content/`, but the TermLinker runs inside react-markdown in the renderer, which cannot import main-process code. Options: put it in the renderer (ui-core's folder, and main's Monaco data would duplicate the matching); put it in shared. Chosen: `src/shared/content/` (owned by content-engine) for the alias table, matcher, and plugin; `GlossaryService` stays in main. The plugin emits `glossaryTerm` nodes with `data.hName = "glossary-term"`, so react-markdown renders a `<glossary-term entryId>` element that the renderer maps to its HoverCard. Reverse cost: low.

### A201 2026-10-21 Which spellings count as identifiers for case matching (M1.8, builder-content-engine)
The spec says identifiers such as `AActor` match case sensitively and plain words such as "actor" do not, but gave no rule. Chosen: an identifier has an uppercase letter after its first character, or one of `_ : ( ) < > # .` or a digit. So `AActor`, `BeginPlay`, `UPROPERTY`, `TObjectPtr<>`, `UE_LOG` are identifiers; `Actor`, `Tick`, `garbage collection` are plain words and match "actor", "tick". Options considered: a per-entry flag in the glossary (more authoring work, easy to forget); the rule above. Reverse cost: low.
2026-10-21 (M1.8 round 2): superseded in detail by A213; the rule in use is app-shell's `isIdentifierText`, which also requires no spaces.

### A200 2026-10-21 Normal glossary terms also link inside inline code, whole span only (M1.8, builder-content-engine; queued from the M0.4 re-review)
Options: never link inside inline code; link any spelling found inside a span; link only when the whole span is one spelling. Chosen: the third, for every entry, with a trailing `()` ignored. `` `AActor` `` and `` `BeginPlay()` `` get hover cards (that is where identifiers appear in prose), while code-shaped spans like `` `AActor* Owner` `` are left whole. Code-only entries (A62) link only this way. Reverse cost: low.

### A89 2026-10-21 What waits for M0.2b, and four renderer features placed (M0.2, builder-shell; fix round 1)
`src/shared/types/content.ts` is still empty on this branch (M0.4 is writing it). Options: invent placeholder content types in app-shell; leave content-shaped methods out. Chosen: leave them out and add them in M0.2b after M0.4 merges: `listPacks`, `getPack` or `getWorld`, `getQuest`, `getGlossary`, the flat glossary term list for Monaco, `getPatternCards`, and `startDrill` (its items carry content shapes such as questions and Parsons blocks). Fix round 1 placed four features the first version missed:
- Concept scratch buffer syntax check (`ui-quests.md`): contract method now, `checkSnippetSyntax({ questId, code })` answering `SyntaxCheckResult` (diagnostics with line, column, severity, message). A renderer-only check could only test brackets; a real check needs the platform compiler with the sandbox's include paths (for example `clang -fsyntax-only` on macOS, `cl /Zs` on Windows), so main owns it. Stub until unreal-pipeline builds it alongside M1.3; the exact command line is that task's decision.
- Test-out per world (`ui-core.md`, `product.md`): eligibility is a contract method now, `getTestOutStatus()` answering `{ eligible, testedOut, reason? }` by world id (no content shapes needed). Taking the test-out goes through `startDrill`, in M0.2b, with a request shaped `{ kind: "due" } | { kind: "test-out", worldId } | { kind: "parsons-fallback", questId }`; `DrillOutcome.testedOutWorldIds` already reports a pass.
- Parsons fallback after two failed submits (`ui-quests.md`, `product.md`): the offer itself is renderer-only (failed submits come from `PlayerProgress.attempts` for the quest, the threshold from `RulesSnapshot.failedSubmitsBeforeOffers`, the block from the quest content). Taking it is a one-item drill session through `startDrill({ kind: "parsons-fallback", questId })` in M0.2b, answered with `answerDrillItem` and `finishDrill`, so the Parsons block is scheduled like any other item. Whether that session grants drill XP is builder-rules' call.
- Worked-level self-explanations (`product.md`): stored, not graded. `QuestDraft` and `QuestProgress` gain optional `selfExplanations` (marked line key to sentence; the key format comes from the quest content in M0.4), saved through `saveQuestDraft`. The gate (every marked line explained before the gap unlocks) is renderer-only.
Reverse cost: low while only stubs exist.

### A167 2026-10-21 `detectEngines` and `checkToolchain` answer through IPC now; `pickEngineFolder` waits for the dialog (builder-unreal, M1.1)
Options: leave all engine methods as stubs; wire the two that need no UI. Chosen: wire the two. `HandlerDependencies` gains `getEngineLocator`, a function so the locator is built on the first call and an unsupported operating system fails that call with a plain `AppError` instead of crashing startup. `pickEngineFolder` needs Electron's `dialog`, which belongs with the app-shell; `EngineLocator.inspectFolder` is ready for it. Reverse cost: none.

### A166 2026-10-21 PlatformPaths holds paths only; the toolchain check and registry are separate injected objects (builder-unreal, M1.1)
The spec put `toolchainCheck()` and `killTree(pid)` on PlatformPaths. Options: one object per platform that does paths and runs processes; paths as pure math, with the toolchain checker, registry reader, file system, and process runner as their own interfaces. Chosen: the second. Path objects then need no fakes at all, and each checker is tested with a scripted command runner. `createUnrealPlatform.ts` is the one place that branches on `process.platform`. `killTree` arrives with the builder in M1.3. Reverse cost: low.

### A165 2026-10-21 Visual Studio check requires the C++ game workload and 17.14 or later for every engine (builder-unreal, M1.1)
Options: check only that Visual Studio exists; check the workload; check the workload and the minimum version per engine. Chosen: workload (`Microsoft.VisualStudio.Workload.NativeGame` plus the MSVC x64 tools) and 17.14 or later, which is what UE 5.8 needs (research 9.1). A learner on 5.4 to 5.7 with an older Visual Studio 2022 would be told to update even though their engine might build. Simple now; making it per engine is a small change if that case shows up. Xcode has no minimum-version check yet (Epic's per-engine Xcode table was not checked). Reverse cost: low.

### A164 2026-10-21 Registry read through `reg query`, including source builds (builder-unreal, M1.1)
Options: a native registry module; `reg query`. Chosen: `reg query`, no native module and no package.json change. Reads the launcher key `HKLM\SOFTWARE\EpicGames\Unreal Engine` and also `HKCU\Software\Epic Games\Unreal Engine\Builds`, where engines built from source register. Only the default (64-bit) registry view is read. Unverified on a real Windows machine. Reverse cost: none.

### A163 2026-10-21 Default install folders on macOS include `~/Applications` (builder-unreal, M1.1)
The launcher's documented default is `/Users/Shared/Epic Games`, but this Mac's launcher installed UE 5.8 into `~/Applications/UE_5.8`. Options: shared folder only; both. Chosen: both. The launcher list finds it anyway; the folder scan is the backup when the list is missing. Reverse cost: none.

### A162 2026-10-21 Engines older than 5.4 are left out of detection; a manual pick of one is refused (builder-unreal, M1.1)
Options: list every engine with a "too old" flag; leave old ones out. Chosen: leave them out of `detectEngines` (the first-run screen should only offer engines that work) and answer `engine-version-unsupported` with a plain message when the learner picks one by hand. Reverse cost: low, one filter.

### A161 2026-10-21 `ToolchainCheck` gains `problems` (each an `AppError` with `blocksBuild`) and `toolVersion` (builder-unreal, M1.1)
The M0.2 shape had one message and one action. The Mac can have a blocking problem and a non-blocking one at once (the missing Metal toolchain does not stop builds or headless tests, only the editor window). Options: one message only; a list. Chosen: a list, blocking first; `ok` is false only for blocking problems; `message` and `suggestedAction` still summarize, so a simple chip needs nothing else. Changes `src/shared/types/engine.ts` (app-shell's file, a small additive change). Reverse cost: low.

### A160 2026-10-21 `EngineSource` gains `launcher`; the launcher's install list is read first on both platforms (builder-unreal, M1.1)
Options: treat engines from `LauncherInstalled.dat` as `default-folder`; give them their own source. Chosen: their own source, because the launcher list also finds engines in non-default folders (as on this Mac). Search order is launcher list, registry, default folders; a folder found twice keeps the first source. Plugin entries in the list (`QuixelBridge_5.8`, `FabPlugin_5.8`) share the engine's folder and are skipped by name (`UE_<version>` only). Reverse cost: none.

### A139 2026-10-21 Effort XP only for code attempts (M1.6, builder-rules; the Lead's decision)
The spec says "Fail but compiled: 5% effort XP, once per quest" without naming kinds. Options: every kind (a failed concept or breakdown counts as "compiled"); only kinds with code. The Lead chose: effort XP is paid only for build, refactor, and boss attempts that compiled; failed concept and breakdown attempts pay nothing. `EFFORT_QUEST_KINDS` in `rules.ts`. Reverse cost: none.

### A138 2026-10-21 Where the save keeps the XP potion, cosmetics, and chests (M1.6, builder-rules)
The spec's save file had no place for an active XP potion, held cosmetics, or unopened chests. Options for chests: store unopened chests and open them on the loot screen; open each chest the moment it is earned. Chosen: open at once. The reward is rolled and applied in the same save write that completes the daily quest, and the attempt or drill outcome carries `chest` so the loot screen can animate the opening. Nothing unopened is stored, so a crash cannot lose or duplicate a chest. The active potion is `PlayerProgress.xpPotionUntil` (ISO end time, or null): a new potion restarts the 30 minutes, and an expired one is ignored, not cleared. Held cosmetics are `studio.cosmetics` (ids from `STUDIO_COSMETICS` in `rules.ts`, given in order, never repeated). Freezes, armor, and Seer Stones already had fields (`streak.freezes`, `spree.armor`, `seerStones`). Reverse cost: low. Storing unopened chests later needs one list field and an `openChest` method.

### A137 2026-10-21 A first boss pass adds a module to the studio (M1.6, builder-rules)
product.md says each shipped mechanic adds a module to the engine room but no rule says when. Options: every build pass; every boss pass; leave it to the UI. Chosen: the first pass of a boss adds the boss id to `studio.modules`, alongside the freeze and armor the boss earns. Reverse cost: low.

### A136 2026-10-21 Rules read a narrow `RuleQuest`, not the content types (M1.6, builder-rules)
`src/shared/types/content.ts` is empty on this branch (M0.4 is in review). Options: wait; copy the content types; define the few facts the rules need. Chosen: `RuleQuest` (id, title, kind, difficulty, xp, pack and world ids with their orders, order, scaffold, prerequisites, terms, patterns, skills) plus `contentFacts.ts` mirroring `QuestKind` and `ScaffoldLevel`. After M0.4 merges, `contentFacts.ts` re-exports from `content.ts` and a small mapper turns a loaded `Pack` into `RuleQuest[]`. Reverse cost: low. Fix round 1: with M0.4 merged, `contentFacts.ts` now re-exports `QuestKind` and `ScaffoldLevel` from `@shared/types/content`.

### A135 2026-10-21 Ten progress IPC methods wired now; the rest wait for content (M1.6, builder-rules)
Wired: `getProgress`, `getRules`, `setPlayerName`, `saveQuestDraft`, `resetQuest`, `recordTermHover`, `queueDrillItem`, `chooseDailyOffer`, `resetProgress`, `getDrillSummary`. Not yet: `getQuestAvailability`, `revealHint`, `submitConcept`, `submitBreakdown`, `reviseBreakdown`, `getDailyQuest`, `answerDrillItem`, `finishDrill`, because they need quest facts, hint text, questions, or drill items from the content loader; the service methods behind them exist and are tested. `revealHint` with no hints left answers `invalid-payload` (no closer error code exists). `HandlerDependencies` gained `progressService`. Reverse cost: none.

### A134 2026-10-21 Rematches replay passed quests and are found when the World Map loads (M1.6, builder-rules)
Options: a due item surfaces any quest using it; only passed quests. Chosen: passed non-boss quests that use the term or pattern, from a different world than the item was earned in when one exists, else the same world, ties in map order. Found in `getQuestAvailability` from today's due items. A rematch pays 50% only once it is due. Reverse cost: low. Fix round 1: a rematch is flagged on the map only once its due time has come, and passing it reviews the due terms and patterns the quest uses (quality from the score), so they move to their next interval and the same rematch cannot surface again that day; a second replay pays 0. Fix round 2: rematches are built only from items whose scheduled due time falls by the end of today. "Drill me" items and hover-injected terms never surface or pay a rematch, because they stay due after the replay and could be farmed.

### A133 2026-10-21 Save file keeps a backup and quarantines a corrupt file (M1.6, builder-rules)
Options for corrupt-file recovery: start fresh; keep a `.bak` of the last good save and fall back to it. Chosen: every write copies the current file to `progress.json.bak` before the rename; a bad main file is renamed to `progress.json.corrupt-<time>` (kept, never deleted) and the backup is loaded; both bad gives a fresh save. A save from a newer app version is refused with `save-failed` instead of being overwritten. Reverse cost: none. Fix round 1: only a file that is not JSON or fails the schema is quarantined. A file the disk will not read (EBUSY, EPERM, EACCES, any code other than ENOENT) is refused with `save-failed` and left alone, and on Windows reads, renames, and copies retry those codes for about 1.5 seconds first. A refused save (newer version or unreadable) no longer stops the app: `ProgressService.openOrRefuse` gives a refused service, handlers still register, the window opens, every progress call answers the `save-failed` error, `getRules` still answers, and nothing is written.

### A132 2026-10-21 Daily quest details (M1.6, builder-rules)
Offers with no candidate (no available concept, no short build) are left out, so a day can have fewer than three. The chest picks with equal odds among rewards the learner can still hold (freeze below 2, armor below 2, stone below 2, an unowned cosmetic); the potion is always possible. Cosmetics are eight placeholder ids in `rules.ts` until ui-core designs them. A drill offer matches any finished drill; concept and build offers need their quest passed. A new save has an empty daily date so the first day gets offers. Reverse cost: low. Fix round 1: a short build is a build quest with `scaffold: worked` and `addsNewClass` false (`RuleQuest.addsNewClass`). The content front matter has no such field yet, so the content mapper must supply it; until then the mapper should treat it as true (no short builds offered) rather than guess.

### A131 2026-10-21 Pack gates are written by pack and world order (M1.6, builder-rules)
"Pack 4 unlocks after Pack 1 world 5 and Pack 2 world 4 bosses" is stored as `PACK_GATES` in `rules.ts` by order, not by id, so renaming pack folders does not break it. The gate locks every quest of Pack 4, including its first quest. Reverse cost: none.

### A130 2026-10-21 Test-out is checked after every drill (M1.6, builder-rules)
Options: a special "world test-out drill"; check after any drill. Chosen: after any finished drill, every world whose terms (all terms of its quests) are at least proficient is tested out. In a tested-out world the term rule is waived, worked and faded build quests are marked skippable, and a skippable quest counts as a met prerequisite. Bosses never skip. Reverse cost: medium once learners rely on it.

### A129 2026-10-21 Order of XP modifiers and the potion (M1.6, builder-rules)
Hint cost comes off the first-pass XP and can never take it below 0. The spree bonus is 5% of what is left after hints. The XP potion adds 50% to everything earned while active, including drill XP (10 becomes 15) and effort XP. Reverse cost: none.

### A128 2026-10-21 Skill points on first pass and rematch only (M1.6, builder-rules)
Options: points on every pass; first pass only; first pass and rematch. Chosen: first pass and rematch, matching XP so repeats cannot farm points. Cards count each quest once per pattern anyway. Reverse cost: none.

### A127 2026-10-21 Mastery lives on items; a skill shows its weakest item (M1.6, builder-rules)
The spec has mastery on skills but says it comes from the scheduler, whose items are terms, cards, Parsons blocks, and scenarios; unlocks also need terms at familiar. Chosen: each review item has its own mastery in `memory` (attempted on first sight, familiar after 1 success in a row, proficient after 3 in a row, mastered at an interval of 30 days or more; any miss drops one level). A skill's mastery is the lowest of its items (items carry `skillId` from the drill that reviewed them), so one missed item shows the skill as due for review. Every miss of a scheduled item drops mastery, not only misses inside mixed drills, because every drill is mixed. Reverse cost: medium. Fix round 1: a successful review on a calendar day before the item is due is logged but does not advance repetitions, interval, ease, or mastery, so only spaced recalls count (three reviews at one moment stay at familiar). A miss before the due day still counts.

### A126 2026-10-21 Streak counts active days in local time (M1.6, builder-rules)
The count goes up by one per calendar day (learner's local time) with a completed drill or passed quest. A gap of up to 3 days keeps it; each freeze covers 3 more days and is spent automatically; without enough freezes the streak restarts at 1 and the unused freezes stay. Milestones 7, 30, 100 are celebrated each time the count reaches them and recorded once. Reverse cost: low.

### A125 2026-10-21 Only the first Submit of a never-passed build or refactor moves the spree (M1.6, builder-rules)
A first-try pass adds 1; a first-try miss resets to 0 unless armor absorbs it. Later submits of the same quest neither add nor break (the first miss already counted), and repeats of passed quests do not count, so the spree cannot be farmed. Reverse cost: low.

### A124 2026-10-21 Armor is capped at 2 and comes from first boss passes and chests (M1.6, builder-rules)
The spec gives no cap. Options: 1; 2 like freezes and stones; none. Chosen: 2. Reverse cost: none.

### A123 2026-10-21 SM-2 ease changes on every review (M1.6, builder-rules)
Original SM-2 is unclear on whether a miss changes ease. Chosen: the standard ease formula runs on every review (so misses lower ease, never below 1.3); the next interval uses the ease before this review's change. A miss restarts at 1 day. Reverse cost: none until FSRS replaces it.

### A122 2026-10-21 Quality of plain drill answers and low misses (M1.6, builder-rules)
The spec maps scores to quality but drill items are right or wrong. Chosen: right is 4, wrong is 1; scored items use the table, and a miss scoring 50 or more is 2, lower is 1. Reverse cost: none.

### A121 2026-10-21 How rematch and solution views pay (M1.6, builder-rules)
A rematch pass pays 50% of the full first-pass XP after the difficulty multiplier, and counts only once its due time has come. After viewing the solution without a stone, passing before the rematch is due pays 0; the rematch (3 days later) pays 50%. With a stone, the pass pays in full and the rematch is still scheduled. The solution is offered after two failed submits. Reverse cost: low.

### A120 2026-10-21 Progress types extended for the rules (M1.6, builder-rules)
`src/shared/types/progress.ts` changes: `PlayerProgress` gains `memory` (per item SM-2 state, mastery, skill, world earned in), `drillQueue`, `rematches`, `testedOutWorldIds`, `xpPotionUntil`, `studio.cosmetics`; `DailyQuestState` gains `completedDates` (for the month badge); `QuestProgress` gains `seerStoneSpent` and `effortXpGranted`; `QuestAvailability` gains `skippable`; `DrillOutcome` gains `chest`; `RulesSnapshot` gains `maxArmor`, `hintsPerQuest`, `rematchAfterDays`, `xpPotion`. Nothing was removed or renamed. Reverse cost: low while no UI reads them.

### A89 2026-10-21 Content methods and `startDrill` wait for M0.2b (M0.2, builder-shell)
`src/shared/types/content.ts` is still empty on this branch (M0.4 is writing it). Options: invent placeholder content types in app-shell; leave the methods out. Chosen: leave them out and add them in M0.2b after M0.4 merges: `listPacks`, `getPack` or `getWorld`, `getQuest`, `getGlossary`, the flat glossary term list for Monaco, `getPatternCards`, and `startDrill` (its items carry content shapes such as questions and Parsons blocks). Everything else in the specs is in the contract now. Reverse cost: none.

### A88 2026-10-21 No "view reference solution" method yet (M0.2, builder-shell)
`docs/product.md` and ui-quests say a learner can view a reference solution (costs the quest's XP or a Seer Stone, schedules a rematch), but `docs/systems/content-engine.md` says `tools/solutions/` is "never loaded by the app". Options: add `viewSolution` reading `tools/solutions/`; add it reading a solution shipped inside the quest file; leave it out. Chosen: leave it out until the Lead settles where solutions live in the shipped app. The Seer Stone count is already in `PlayerProgress`. Reverse cost: one method.

### A87 2026-10-21 Breakdown is two calls: `submitBreakdown` then `reviseBreakdown` (M0.2, builder-shell)
The ui-quests flow shows a reference plan after Submit, asks for a revision note, then grades. Options: one call with the plan and the note; two calls. Chosen: two. `submitBreakdown(plan)` answers only which reference plan to show (the renderer already has the plans from the quest); `reviseBreakdown(revisedPlan, revisionNote)` is graded and recorded. Most of the score comes from the revision, so grading before it would waste a review. Reverse cost: low.

### A86 2026-10-21 Settings carry `hasApiKey` and `firstRunCompletedAt`; `completeFirstRun` method (M0.2, builder-shell)
The API key never leaves main, so the renderer needs a flag to show "key stored" in Settings. First run needs a durable "done" mark so the Title screen knows to skip it even if the engine later disappears (that case shows an error chip instead). Options: derive "first run done" from engine and sandbox paths; store a timestamp. Chosen: timestamp. Reverse cost: none.

### A85 2026-10-21 `BuildProgressEvent` gains `runId`, `loop`, `loopReason`, and two phases (M0.2, builder-shell)
The unreal-pipeline spec's event has no run id and five phases. Added: `runId` (the task asked for it), `loop` and `loopReason` (the spec says the loop choice and its reason go into the stream), and phases `preparing` (template copy, loop choice) and `reviewing` (AI review inside Submit), because sandbox setup and Submit stream on the same channel. Reverse cost: low while only stubs exist.

### A84 2026-10-21 Two extra shared type files and `src/main/errors/` (M0.2, builder-shell)
Options: put engine and error types inside `settings.ts` and `run.ts`; give them their own files. Chosen: `src/shared/types/errors.ts` (`AppError`, `AppErrorCode`, `isAppError`) and `src/shared/types/engine.ts` (engine installs, toolchain, sandbox folder, environment status), both re-exported from `index.ts`. `AppErrorException` (what services throw) lives in `src/main/errors/` because every service uses it, not just IPC. Spec layout updated. Reverse cost: none.

### A83 2026-10-21 Payload validation with zod schemas typed against the shared types (M0.2, builder-shell)
The Lead added zod (4.6.5, `dependencies`) in the M0.1 fix round and asked for it at the IPC boundary. Options considered first: hand-written checks (the first version of this branch); zod. Chosen: zod. `src/main/ipc/requestSchemas.ts` has one schema per request type, each declared as `z.ZodType<SharedType>`, so a schema that drifts from its shared type is a compile error; a mapped type forces one schema per method argument. Objects are strict (unknown fields rejected); ids allow only letters, digits, dot, underscore, and dash; learner file names must be plain names with a C++ or text extension (no folders, no `..`), at most 50 files of about 1 MB each; names and keys are trimmed. The handler receives zod's parsed copy, not the raw payload. Problem text (path plus zod's message, at most three issues) goes in `detail`; zod messages never include the value sent, so an API key is not echoed. Reverse cost: low.

### A82 2026-10-21 Long operations take a renderer-made run id (M0.2, builder-shell)
Options: the call returns a run id at once and the result arrives as a final event; the renderer makes the id (`crypto.randomUUID()`) and the call's promise resolves with the result. Chosen: the second. The renderer can match progress events and cancel before the call returns, and the result keeps its type on the promise instead of travelling as an untyped event. The run services must reject a run id already in use (`run-already-exists`). Reverse cost: low while only stubs exist.

### A81 2026-10-21 `DojoInvokeApi` plus `DojoApi`, channels typed as `dojo:<method>` (M0.2, builder-shell)
`onBuildProgress` is a subscription, not an invoke channel, so the request methods are `DojoInvokeApi` and `DojoApi` adds the subscription. `IPC_CHANNELS` is checked against `` `dojo:${method}` `` for every method, so a channel can never drift from its method name. The progress channel is `dojo:event:buildProgress`. The preload builds its forwarding functions from `IPC_CHANNELS`, so a new method needs no preload edit. Reverse cost: none.

### A80 2026-10-21 Failed calls reject with a plain `AppError`; methods keep `Promise<Value>` (M0.2, builder-shell)
Options: every method returns `Promise<Result<Value>>` and the renderer checks `ok` on each call; main answers an `{ ok, value | error }` envelope over IPC and the preload rejects with the plain `AppError`. Chosen: the second. Throwing straight from `ipcMain.handle` loses the code and suggested action (Electron rewrites it into "Error invoking remote method"), so the envelope stays on the wire, but the renderer API reads like normal async code and the M0.1 `getAppVersion` call and ui-core's work in progress need no change. Verified in the built app: the rejected value arrives in the page with `code`, `message`, `suggestedAction`, and `detail` intact (plain object, not an `Error`). Renderer rule: `catch (error) { if (isAppError(error)) show it }`. Reverse cost: medium once screens use it.

### A98 2026-10-21 Short feedback motion is allowed; expressive motion stays for reward moments (M0.3 fix round 1, Lead decision)
The spec said "motion only on reward moments". Options: that wording as written (no hover, press, or panel transitions at all; the option not taken); allow short feedback transitions of 150 ms or less on hover, press, and panel open, and keep anything longer or expressive for rewards (chosen by the Lead). Why: instant state changes feel broken in a desktop app, and a sub-150 ms fade reads as responsiveness, not decoration. In tokens: `--duration-instant` (80 ms) and `--duration-fast` (140 ms) are for feedback; the dialog entrance and every hover use them; `--duration-base`, `--duration-slow`, `--duration-reward` are reserved for reward moments. All drop to zero under reduced motion. Reverse cost: low, change the two feedback tokens to 0ms.

### A97 2026-10-21 Placeholder screens list what each will hold (M0.3, builder-ui-core)
The task asked for empty screens with their name. Options: name only; name, one-line purpose, and the spec's list of what a later task builds there. Chosen: the second, so a playtest of the frame already explains the app and reviewers can see the layout with real text lengths. Each list is copied from this spec's Screens section. Reverse cost: none.

### A96 2026-10-21 Title screen sits inside the frame, with the rail (M0.3, builder-ui-core)
Options: a full-bleed title screen with no rail (more like a game); the same frame as every other screen. Chosen: the frame for now, so every route is one click away while screens are placeholders. Revisit when the real Title and First-run screens are built. Reverse cost: low.

### A95 2026-10-21 shadcn/ui components written by hand, restyled, on `radix-ui` 1.7.0 (M0.3, builder-ui-core)
Options: run `npx shadcn add` (needs the network registry and rewrites `global.css`); copy the new-york sources by hand onto the Dojo tokens. Chosen: by hand, for button, badge, dialog, input, kbd, separator, tooltip, on the `radix-ui` 1.7.0 that M0.1 added. File names follow shadcn (lowercase) and `dialog`/`tooltip` keep their parts in one file, an exception to "one component per file" because the parts are one compound component. Reverse cost: none.

### A94 2026-10-21 Hash router written in the app, no router library (M0.3, builder-ui-core)
Options: react-router 7, wouter, TanStack Router, a 60-line hash router. Chosen: the hash router (`app/router/`). Seven flat routes need no nesting, loaders, or params; hash URLs work under `file://` in the packaged app; it adds no dependency; it is unit tested. Switch to wouter or react-router if routes ever need params (`#/quest/:id` may). Reverse cost: low, the route table stays.

### A93 2026-10-21 Pack accents and neutrals (M0.3, builder-ui-core)
Chosen values: foundations amber `#f6b53f`, patterns violet `#b394ff`, mechanics cyan `#3fd5e8`, systems green `#8ad95f`, each with a 12% soft tint and a near-black deep shade for text on the accent. Background "sumi ink" `#0a0b0f` with surfaces stepping up to `#1f232d`; text "warm paper" `#f4f1ea`, muted `#a9acb6` (about 8.7:1), faint `#8a8e9c` (lightened in fix round 1 from `#7f8392`, which was 4.16:1 on overlay). Contrast on ink, surface, raised, overlay: muted 8.68, 8.19, 7.59, 6.93; faint 6.02, 5.69, 5.27, 4.81. Outcome colors `pass #45d39b`, `fail #ff6b6b`, `warn #ffd166` are kept off the pack hues so a passed test never reads as "systems". Options considered: shadcn neutral grays (stock look), a cool blue-black (generic dashboard), the warm ink and paper pair (chosen, gives the dojo feel without decoration). Foundations amber doubles as the app's signature accent outside a pack. Reverse cost: edit `tokens.css` only.

### A92 2026-10-21 Fonts: Bricolage Grotesque, Instrument Sans, JetBrains Mono, bundled (M0.3, builder-ui-core)
Options for display: Space Grotesk (overused), Unbounded (too loud for long headings), Chakra Petch (sci-fi cliché), Bricolage Grotesque (chosen: characterful, variable weight, reads well large). Body: Inter (stock), IBM Plex Sans (static weights only), Instrument Sans (chosen: calm, variable). Code: JetBrains Mono (clear C++ glyphs). All OFL. The Latin and Latin Extended variable woff2 files (taken from the `@fontsource-variable/*` 5.3.0 releases) and their licenses are committed in `src/renderer/src/assets/fonts/` with a `fonts.css`, instead of adding three npm packages, so `package.json` is untouched (Lead rule: no new dependencies without asking). Vite bundles them; nothing is fetched at runtime and the CSP is unchanged. About 160 KB of woff2. Reverse cost: replace the folder and the `--font-*` tokens.

### A91 2026-10-21 Tokens are Tailwind theme variables with Tailwind's defaults reset (M0.3, builder-ui-core)
Options: plain `:root` variables mapped with `@theme inline` (as the scaffold did); `@theme static` with the default palette, type scale, radii, and shadows reset. Chosen: the second. Every token becomes a utility and a variable at once, `static` keeps unused ones in the CSS so the gallery can read them, and resetting the defaults means a stray `bg-blue-500` or `text-gray-400` fails to style instead of sneaking in. Pack-following tokens (`pack`, `ring`, `shadow-glow`) use `@theme inline` so a `data-pack` wrapper changes them for its subtree. Reverse cost: low.

### A90 2026-10-21 Screenshots at exact sizes through CDP device metrics, with reduced motion (M0.3, builder-ui-core)
This Mac's screen is 853 points tall (A17), so the OS will not open a 1440x900 window. Options: resize the BrowserWindow (capped by the screen); override the page size through `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride`. Chosen: the override, which renders the page at exactly 1100x700 and 1440x900 on any screen. Screenshots come out at the machine's pixel ratio (2x here). The spec also emulates `prefers-reduced-motion` so durations are zero and no picture catches a transition midway; side effect: the gallery screenshots show the duration tokens as 0s. Like the M0.1 smoke test, pictures go to `test-results/` and are copied to `docs/iterations/` only with `SAVE_PROOF=1`. Reverse cost: none.

### A118 2026-10-21 Smoke test allows 12 points of slack on the height when the screen sets it (QA harness fix rounds 2 to 4, tester)
On the M5 Air the logical screen is 1470x956. The work area changes while the test runs: the Dock resizes as Electron icons from other sessions come and go, so macOS can size the window to a work area of 853 that has grown to 858 by the time the test reads it, and the window stays 853. A 1 point tolerance (round 2) still failed 2 of 20 with a 5 point gap; a plain range (round 3) passed but also let a wrong 1440x800 or 1440x720 window through. Chosen (round 4): when the work area is what limits the height (between 700 and 900), the height must be within 12 points of min(900, work area height) and never below 700; 12 covers the 1 point rounding and the 5 point Dock change with room, while 800 and 720 still fail. A window that gets its full 900 is still checked exactly, and the width check is unchanged (exact, 1440 here). Reverse cost: one constant in `e2e/support/windowSize.ts`.

### A117 2026-10-21 Windows screen size: raise the resolution in CI and let the smoke test accept the minimum size (M0.6 fix round 1, tester)
Hosted Windows runners have a 1024x768 desktop and the window's minimum is 1100x700, so the A17 check (1440x900 capped by the work area) would expect 1024 wide while Electron keeps the window at 1100. Options: only raise the runner's resolution; only relax the test; both. Chosen: both. The test now expects 1440x900 shrunk to the work area but never below the 1100x700 minimum, which is true on any screen. `ci.yml` also runs `Set-DisplayResolution -Width 1920 -Height 1080 -Force` (pwsh, Windows only) so the screenshot shows the window at its real size. That step is `continue-on-error`, because the cmdlet is unverified on the windows-2025 image and the relaxed test makes it a nice-to-have, not a requirement. Reverse cost: delete the step; the test change stays correct either way.

### A116 2026-10-21 CI triggers, Node version, and shell (M0.6, tester)
Options for triggers: every push on every branch; push to `main` plus pull requests. Chosen: push to `main`, every pull request, and a manual button. Task branches live in local worktrees and arrive as pull requests, so running on every branch push would run each commit twice. Node is pinned to 24.22.1, the same as this Mac (A01), instead of "24", so CI and local runs match. Every step runs in bash (Git Bash on Windows) so one command line serves both runners. Reverse cost: a few lines in `ci.yml`.

### A115 2026-10-21 Release job takes targets and architectures from the `package.json` config, with no flags of its own (M0.6, tester)
The M0.1 fix round put the packaging spec's architectures into the `build` config (dmg arm64 and x64, nsis x64). Options: also pass `--mac dmg --arm64 --x64` and `--win nsis --x64` in the release workflow (the first draft of this task, written before that fix); run plain `npx electron-builder --publish never` on each runner. Chosen: plain, so the config in `package.json` is the one place that says what ships and CI cannot drift from `npm run package`. Reverse cost: one matrix entry per platform.

### A114 2026-10-21 Workflow linter: action-validator through npx, actionlint wasm as a second opinion (M0.6, tester)
Options: actionlint (the standard, a Go binary; npm wrappers either download it from GitHub at install time or are third-party), the `actionlint` 2.0.6 wasm build on npm (actionlint 1.6 era, from 2022), `@action-validator/cli` 0.6.0 (schema check, runs as wasm through `npx`). Chosen: `npx --yes @action-validator/cli@0.6.0 <file>` as the documented check, plus the actionlint wasm build run once from a scratch folder to catch expression mistakes the schema cannot see. Both passed on the real files and both failed a deliberately broken copy. Neither is added to `devDependencies`. Reverse cost: none; switch to the real actionlint when it can be installed.

### A113 2026-10-21 CI does not run `npx playwright install` (M0.6, tester)
The research sketch in `research/tooling-and-stack.md` section 8 runs `npx playwright install --with-deps`. Playwright's `_electron` launches the Electron binary and never uses its own browsers, so the step would download about 500 MB for nothing. Options: keep it; drop it. Chosen: drop it. Reverse cost: one step, if a browser-based test is ever added.

### A112 2026-10-21 CI fetches the Electron binary in its own step and caches it (M0.6, tester)
npm 11 does not run install scripts (A19), so `npm ci` leaves Electron without its binary; Electron downloads it the first time it is launched, which would happen inside the e2e test and count against its 30 second launch timeout. Options: approve Electron's install script in `package.json`; let the first test download it; run `node node_modules/electron/install.js` as a step after `npm ci`. Chosen: the step, with its download folder (`electron_config_cache`) cached by `actions/cache` keyed on `package-lock.json`. A failed download then shows as its own red step, and `package.json` stays untouched. The release job does not cache: the local run showed electron-builder 26 ignores `ELECTRON_CACHE` and downloads the Electron builds it packages into its own default cache, and tags are rare enough that the download does not matter. Reverse cost: delete one step.

### A111 2026-10-21 Release workflow reruns `ci.yml` before packaging (M0.6, tester)
Options: package inside `ci.yml` on tags; a separate `release.yml` that packages straight away; a separate `release.yml` that calls `ci.yml` (`workflow_call`) and packages only after it passes. Chosen: the last. An installer is never built from a commit whose checks failed, and the check steps exist in one file only. Reverse cost: low.

### A110 2026-10-21 Every GitHub action pinned to a full commit SHA, newest release at least 14 days old (M0.6, tester)
Same two-week rule as A08. Pins: `actions/checkout` v7.0.1 (`3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1`, 2026-07-17), `actions/setup-node` v7.1.0 (`949feb2413d6458794dcd2491c4babbbce0c15c1`, 2026-10-07), `actions/upload-artifact` v7.0.2 (`cf430e030ddbb5b0abf93d22962f4752f3646cd9`, 2026-10-07), `actions/cache` v6.1.0 (`55cc8345863c7cc4c66a329aec7e433d2d1c52a9`, 2026-06-23). Each SHA was resolved from its tag with `git fetch` against the action's repository. The research sketch's `@v4` tags are two majors behind and tags can be moved, which a SHA cannot. Reverse cost: edit the SHAs.

### A109 2026-10-21 Validation with zod schemas and one plain-words error formatter (M0.4, builder-content-engine)
The first version used a hand-written field reader; the Lead then directed zod (added to dependencies in M0.1 fix round 1). Chosen: one zod schema per shape in `src/main/services/content/schemas/`, strict objects so unknown fields are reported, typed against the shared types (`z.ZodType<Question>` and so on) so schema and type cannot drift. `validateWith` passes a per-parse error map that rewrites zod's messages in plain words with the field path and tells "missing" apart from "wrong". Cross references between parsed pieces (plan edges and `covers`) are checked after the schemas pass, so a broken edge never hides a bad `covers` id. Reverse cost: low.

### A108 2026-10-21 Verifier edge behavior: empty folder, missing files, exit codes, filters (M0.4, builder-content-engine)
A content folder with no `glossary.json`, `patterns.json`, or `packs/` passes cleanly (the M0.4 proof); a content folder that does not exist is an error. Exit 0 clean, 1 on content problems, 2 on a bad command line. `--engine` exits 2 with "arrives in M1.8" instead of pretending to run. `--pack` reads only that pack (glossary and patterns are always read); `--quest` parses everything (duplicates need the whole set) but reports only that quest's file plus any file whose problems quote that id (so a duplicate flagged on a second file still shows), and says so when nothing matches (fix round 1). Options considered for the missing glossary: error now; error at release (M1.8 rule). Chosen: not an error until M1.8 adds the release checks. Reverse cost: none.

### A107 2026-10-21 Folder naming rules the parser enforces (M0.4, builder-content-engine)
A pack folder name must equal its `pack.json` id. A world folder is `<nn>-<world-id>`: the number is the world order and the rest must equal `world.json`'s id, so `world.json` has no `order`. A world folder holds only `world.json` and `<nn>-<slug>.md` files; anything else is reported. Quest order comes from front matter `order`, unique inside a world; the file number is for humans and not checked against it. Options: derive everything from names; keep everything in manifests. Chosen: names for packs and worlds (the spec already names folders that way), front matter for quests (authors renumber files less often than they reorder). Reverse cost: low.

### A106 2026-10-21 What the parser rejects versus what M1.8's verifier checks (M0.4, builder-content-engine)
Parser (blocks loading): wrong or missing fields, unknown front matter fields (typos), sections missing, repeated, unknown, or out of order, hints not exactly 3 (the type is a 3-tuple), a brief without `## See it in the editor`, plan `covers` ids and edges that point nowhere, ids repeated inside a quest, duplicate ids across files. A file with any problem is left out; the rest still loads. Verifier in M1.8 (content rules, not shape): rubric sizes, non-empty `concepts`, terms and patterns existing, prerequisites, reachability, fade ladder, every required piece in each plan. Reverse cost: low; moving a rule is a few lines.

### A105 2026-10-21 Question shapes match author-foundations' `patterns.json`; every question has an id (M0.4, builder-content-engine)
author-foundations had already written 22 pattern cards on branch `m0-5-glossary`. Options: define shapes from the spec and ask them to change; adopt their shapes. Chosen: adopt theirs exactly (`acceptedAnswers` on fill-blank, `distractorNotes` on which-pattern, `bugLine` and `fix` on spot-the-bug, `modelAnswer` and `rubric` on explain-in-one-sentence), so their files parse as they are: all 191 glossary entries and 22 pattern cards pass. Every question needs an `id` so the drill scheduler can log reviews per item. `DrillQuest` is in the `Quest` union with the common front matter plus `items` (tagged with their source), built at runtime only. Reverse cost: low.

### A104 2026-10-21 YAML through gray-matter for front matter and section blocks (M0.4, builder-content-engine)
The first version added the `yaml` package; the Lead directed gray-matter (4.0.3, pinned in M0.1) and no other new dependency, so `yaml` was removed and no package file is changed by M0.4. gray-matter parses the front matter, and each ` ```yaml ` block inside a section is parsed by handing it to gray-matter as front matter (`parseYaml.ts`), which keeps one YAML engine (its js-yaml 3) and its error line (`mark.line`) for messages. An options object is always passed so gray-matter's string cache never returns a shared data object. Repeated keys are an error. Dates written without quotes become dates and fail as "expected text", which is the safe outcome. Options considered: import js-yaml directly (an undeclared transitive dependency); `matter.engines.yaml.parse` (not in gray-matter's type definitions). Reverse cost: low.

### A103 2026-10-21 Format examples are not compiled and have no reference solutions (M0.4, builder-content-engine)
CLAUDE.md says every build quest ships a reference solution. Options: write solutions for the 5 code examples and compile them with `--engine`; treat them as format references only. Chosen: format references. They are not in any pack, their ids start with `example-`, and the verifier only parses them. Their tests use the sandbox's `FDojoTestWorld` and the `Dojo.` test naming from M1.2a so authors copy the right shape. If the Lead wants them compiled, M1.8 can add solutions and run them with `--engine`. Reverse cost: one task.

### A102 2026-10-21 Marked lines for the self-explanation gate use an `// @explain:` comment (M0.4, builder-content-engine)
Options: a `markedLines` list of line numbers in front matter (breaks whenever a line is added above); a marker comment at the end of the line. Chosen: the comment `// @explain: <model reason>`. The parser strips it from the starter and records `{ line, reason }`, so the learner never sees it and the numbers never drift. Reverse cost: low.

### A101 2026-10-21 Examples live in `content/examples/` and the verifier parses them (M0.4, builder-content-engine)
The spec put the examples in `docs/systems/content-authoring.md`, and the task put them in `content/examples/`. Chosen: files in `content/examples/`, listed by name in content-authoring.md and parsed on every verifier run through `ContentLoader.loadExamples()`, so the examples authors copy can never stop parsing. They ship inside `content/` in the installer (a few KB) but the app never loads them. Reverse cost: none.

### A100 2026-10-21 Structured data inside sections is one fenced YAML block (M0.4, builder-content-engine)
Options: all structured data (questions, pieces, plans, Parsons) in front matter; a JSON file next to each quest; one ` ```yaml ` block per data section; custom Markdown conventions. Chosen: a YAML block per section. Front matter stays short and scannable, each kind's data sits in its fixed section order, YAML allows multi-line text and code (`|`), and errors point to the section and file line. Prose sections are plain Markdown, code sections are one ` ```cpp ` block, and hints and rubrics are bullet lists because authors write those as lists anyway. Reverse cost: medium once packs exist; a converter script would do it.

### A19 2026-10-21 npm install scripts left unapproved; Electron downloads its binary on first use (M0.1, builder-shell)
npm 11.19 blocks package install scripts until they are approved. Options: approve them in `package.json`; leave them. Chosen: leave them. Electron 44 fetches its binary on first run when it is missing, esbuild and fsevents ship prebuilt binaries, and electron-winstaller is only for Squirrel (not used). Everything passed without them. Reverse cost: one `npm install-scripts approve` line if a later package needs it.

### A18 2026-10-21 `npm run test:e2e` does not build first (M0.1, builder-shell)
Options: make `test:e2e` run `electron-vite build` itself; keep it as plain `playwright test` against `out/`. Chosen: plain. CI already runs `npm run build` before it (research section 8), and rebuilding inside every e2e run doubles the time. The smoke test launches the project folder (not `out/main/index.js`) so Electron reads `package.json` like the packaged app does. Reverse cost: one script line.

### A17 2026-10-21 Smoke test checks the window size against the screen (M0.1, builder-shell)
On this MacBook Air the usable screen is 853 points tall, so macOS shrinks a 1440x900 window to 1440x853. Options: shrink the default size; assert exact 1440x900; assert 1440x900 capped by the work area plus the 1100x700 minimum. Chosen: the third. The spec's size stays and the test still catches a wrong size on a large screen. Reverse cost: none.

### A16 2026-10-21 React Fast Refresh loaded as a module so the CSP stays `script-src 'self'` in dev (M0.1, builder-shell)
The React Vite plugin normally adds an inline script in dev, which the CSP blocks. Options: loosen the CSP in dev with `'unsafe-inline'`; import the plugin's `@vitejs/plugin-react/preamble` module at the top of `main.tsx`. Chosen: the import; it is empty in production. The dev renderer was checked in Electron with no CSP errors. Reverse cost: one line.

### A15 2026-10-21 CSP string (M0.1, builder-shell)
Chosen: `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: blob:; font-src 'self' data:; worker-src 'self' blob:; connect-src 'self'`, as a meta tag in `src/renderer/index.html`. Inline styles are needed by Tailwind, Motion, and React Flow; `worker-src blob:` is for Monaco's workers. Rejected: a response header set from main (does not cover `file://` loads as simply). The renderer never calls the network; the AI and MCP calls run in main. Reverse cost: none.

### A14 2026-10-21 Window code lives in `src/main/window/createMainWindow.ts` (M0.1, builder-shell)
The spec layout put the window in `main/index.ts`. Options: keep it there; give it its own file. Chosen: own file, so `index.ts` stays a short composition root and the window rules (size, security, link handling) are in one readable place. Spec layout and architecture updated. Reverse cost: none.

### A13 2026-10-21 `content/` and `resources/` ship as extraResources, outside the asar (M0.1, builder-shell)
The spec said `files` should include `out/`, `content/`, `resources/`. Options: put all three inside the asar; put `out/` in the asar and copy `content/` and `resources/` next to it. Chosen: the second. The sandbox template must be copied to disk as real files, and the UE build tools cannot read inside an asar. Code reads them through `process.resourcesPath` when packaged and the project folder in dev. Reverse cost: low.

### A12 2026-10-21 Renderer libraries are devDependencies (M0.1, builder-shell)
Options: everything in `dependencies`; only main-process runtime packages in `dependencies`. Chosen: the second. Vite bundles React, Monaco, React Flow, Motion, and the rest into the renderer, so shipping their `node_modules` copies again is waste; `externalizeDeps` keeps `dependencies` external for main and preload. Result: the dmg went from 153 MB to 129 MB. unified, remark-parse, remark-gfm, and unist-util-visit are not imported by any code yet; they stay in `dependencies` because the content parser in `src/main/services/content/` (M0.4, content-engine spec) parses quest markdown into sections in the main process. The renderer's TermLinker uses the same packages, and Vite bundles them there anyway. If M0.4 ends up parsing only in the renderer or in tools/, they move to `devDependencies`. Fix round 1 added `radix-ui` (renderer, devDependencies) and `gray-matter`, `zod`, `fast-xml-parser`, `remark-parse` (main, dependencies), pinned by the A08 rule: radix-ui 1.7.0, gray-matter 4.0.3, zod 4.6.5, fast-xml-parser 5.11.2, remark-parse 11.0.0. Rule for later tasks: a package the main process imports goes in `dependencies`, a package only the renderer imports goes in `devDependencies`. Reverse cost: none.

### A11 2026-10-21 Sandbox on from the start (M0.1, builder-shell)
The spec said sandbox on "where Monaco workers allow it". Monaco's workers are ordinary web workers and do not need Node, so sandbox is on for the only window. If Monaco ever fails under it, the build-editor task logs it and turns it off for that window only. Reverse cost: one flag.

### A10 2026-10-21 ESM app with a CommonJS preload (M0.1, builder-shell)
Options: CommonJS everywhere and `await import('electron-store')`; `"type": "module"` for the whole package. Chosen: ESM (electron-store and many current packages are ESM only, as the research recommends). Sandboxed preload scripts must be CommonJS, so the preload alone is built as `out/preload/index.cjs`. Reverse cost: low.

### A09 2026-10-21 electron-store 11.0.2 instead of 12.0.0 (M0.1, builder-shell)
12.0.0 came out on 2026-10-12, under the two-week rule. 11.0.2 (2025-10-05) is also ESM, supports Electron 30+, and has the same API the settings service needs. Options: 12.0.0 now; 11.0.2 now and bump later. Chosen: 11.0.2; bump when the settings task starts after 2026-10-26. Reverse cost: a version bump.

### A08 2026-10-21 Exact version pins, newest release that is at least 14 days old (M0.1, builder-shell)
Options: caret ranges; exact pins. Chosen: exact pins (`--save-exact`) so every worktree and CI runner installs the same tree, choosing the newest release in the decided major line published on or before 2026-10-07. Versions: electron 44.7.0, vite 8.3.3, @vitejs/plugin-react 6.1.2, react and react-dom 19.3.0, typescript 5.9.3, tailwindcss and @tailwindcss/vite 4.3.3, motion 14.0.0, lucide-react 1.52.0, zustand 5.0.15, vitest 5.0.3, @playwright/test 1.63.0, electron-builder 26.17.0, tsx 4.23.15, monaco-editor 0.57.0, @monaco-editor/react 4.7.0, @xyflow/react 12.12.0, react-markdown 10.1.0, unified 11.0.5, unist-util-visit 5.1.0, remark-gfm 4.0.1, @modelcontextprotocol/sdk 1.32.1, @anthropic-ai/sdk 0.131.0, @types/node 24.19.1, @types/react and @types/react-dom 19.3.0, clsx 2.1.1, tailwind-merge 3.7.0, class-variance-authority 0.7.1. Several are older than the research's numbers (which were "latest" on 2026-10-21). Reverse cost: bump when needed.

### A07 2026-10-21 electron-vite 6.0.0 pinned although it is only 7 days old (M0.1, builder-shell)
D01 requires electron-vite 6 with Vite 8, and 6.0.0 (2026-10-14) is the only release that supports Vite 8; 5.0.0 supports Vite 5 to 7 only. Options: electron-vite 5 with Vite 7 (breaks D01 and the Vite 8 pin); 6.0.0 now. Chosen: 6.0.0, the one exception to the two-week rule. typecheck, test, build, e2e, and package all pass with it. Reverse cost: low; drop to 5.0.0 plus Vite 7 if a 6.0.0 bug appears.
### A46 2026-10-21 Naming: keep the `Dojo` class prefixes; the map is `L_Sandbox` (Lead's decision, M1.2a fix round 1)
Options: drop the `Dojo` prefix from `FDojoTestWorld` and `ADojoTestActor` per the no-game-name rule; keep it. Chosen by the Lead: keep it, because `Dojo` is the module and project prefix and the authoring rules already use `ADojoM3...` class names. The map is renamed from `DojoSandbox` to `L_Sandbox` per the asset naming rules (`L_` for levels), regenerated with `Tools/CreateSandboxMap.py`, and the default and startup map settings point at it. Reverse cost: low.

### A45 2026-10-21 On macOS, the live editor is used only while its loaded code is current (builder-unreal, M1.2a)
Found: `CompileLiveCoding` on UE 5.8.3 for Mac returns "Live Coding is not available in this build configuration" (Live Coding is an MSVC feature). UBT can build while the editor is open (5 s), but the open editor keeps running the code it loaded at launch. Options: restart the editor after each build to use MCP for tests; use the headless loop on Mac; drop the fast loop. Chosen: on macOS `RunnerFactory` uses `LiveEditorRunner` only when no build has finished since that editor launched and the run needs no build (for example re-running tests on unchanged code, where MCP `RunTests` answers in about 0.1 s). Otherwise it uses `HeadlessRunner` and marks the open editor stale, offering a restart. An automatic restart costs about the same as a headless run (about 20 s), so it buys nothing. Windows keeps the full fast loop. Reverse cost: low, one branch in `RunnerFactory`.

### A44 2026-10-21 MCP call shape and port, corrected from the spec (builder-unreal, M1.2a)
Verified on UE 5.8.3: `call_tool` takes `toolset_name`, `tool_name`, `arguments` (the spec said `toolset`, `tool`); toolset names are qualified (`AutomationTestToolset.AutomationTestToolset`, `LiveCodingToolset.LiveCodingToolset`); results are JSON inside a `returnValue` string inside the MCP text content. The server port is set with `-ModelContextProtocolPort=<n>`; during the proof another session's editor already held 8000. Chosen: the port is an app setting (default 8000), passed on the editor command line, and the client uses it. The headless command uses `-ExecCmds="Automation RunTests Dojo; Quit"` (verified) instead of the spec's `-TestExit` form. Reverse cost: none.

### A43 2026-10-21 Test world: CreateWorld with begun-play set, no game mode (builder-unreal, M1.2a)
Options: research route 2 as written (CreateWorld, InitializeActorsForPlay, BeginPlay); add a game instance and game mode the way PIE does; use CQTest's `FActorTestSpawner`. Chosen: route 2 plus `SetBegunPlay(true)`; teardown routes `EndPlay` to each actor and calls `SetBegunPlay(false)` by hand (fix round 1: `UWorld::EndPlay` may not exist before 5.8). Reading 5.8 engine source showed `UWorld::BeginPlay` only starts actors through a game mode, so without the flag no actor would get `BeginPlay`; without `EndPlay` the report carries a warning. A game mode needs a game instance and more teardown; CQTest adds a second test style for learners to read. Reverse cost: low, one helper file.

### A42 2026-10-21 Template targets use `BuildSettingsVersion.Latest` and `EngineIncludeOrderVersion.Latest` (builder-unreal, M1.2a)
Options: pin `V7` and `Unreal5_8` as Epic's 5.8 templates do; use `Latest`. Chosen: `Latest`, because the pinned 5.8 values do not exist in 5.4 to 5.7 and one template must build on all of them. Cost: a future engine may add warnings the template has not seen. Reverse cost: none, or SandboxProjectService can write pinned values per engine.

### A41 2026-10-21 Template ships `EngineAssociation: ""` and lists the three 5.8 plugins (builder-unreal, M1.2a)
Options: a marker string such as `{{ENGINE}}`; empty; a real version. Chosen: empty, which the engine treats as "ask which engine" if someone opens the template by hand, and which SandboxProjectService overwrites. The plugins `ModelContextProtocol`, `AutomationTestToolset`, `LiveCodingToolset` (exact names from their `.uplugin` files) are listed enabled; SandboxProjectService removes them for engines older than 5.8, where they do not exist. Reverse cost: none.

### A40 2026-10-21 The sample quest ships in the template as the sandbox smoke test (builder-unreal, M1.2a)
Options: keep the sample out of the shipped template; ship it. Chosen: ship it as `Quests/SampleHealth/USampleHealthComponent` with test `Dojo.Sample.Health.TakesDamage`, so the first-run build has something to compile and the first test run proves the whole chain. Named "Sample" so it cannot clash with a content pack's own health component class (all quests share one module, so two `UHealthComponent` classes would not compile). Content authors should give quest classes names unique within the module. Reverse cost: delete two folders.

### A80 2026-10-26 CI no longer runs on push to main until the Windows unit tests pass
Every push failed the same known way (6 Windows unit test files, see problems.md), sent Mohammad a failure email, and used about 40 minutes of the free Actions pool. Options: keep running on push and mute the emails; pause the push trigger; mark the Windows job as allowed to fail. Chosen with Mohammad: pause the push trigger in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; manual runs (`workflow_dispatch`), pull requests, and the release workflow still run it. The Windows Lead turns the push trigger back on in the same change that makes the Windows tests pass. Reverse cost: two lines.

### A79 2026-10-26 Push to GitHub in batches to stay inside the free Actions minutes
The private repository runs CI on every push to main, on Windows and macOS. GitHub's free plan gives a monthly pool of Actions minutes for private repositories, and macOS minutes count ten times and Windows twice, so one run of about three and a half minutes each costs about 40 minutes of the pool. Pushing after every merge could use the pool within weeks; after that, CI simply stops until the next month (no charge unless Mohammad adds a payment method, which would be his call). Options: push after every merge; push in batches; drop macOS from CI. Chosen: the Lead pushes main in batches (a few times a day, or when another machine needs the work), and CI stays as built. Reverse cost: low.

### A78 2026-10-26 The project lives in a private GitHub repository, pushed from GitHub Desktop
Mohammad published the repository privately at https://github.com/mshehab22/unreal-dojo (private, so not publishing under the CLAUDE.md rule). Git on the Mac has no GitHub login and the GitHub CLI is not installed, so the Lead commits locally and Mohammad pushes with GitHub Desktop ("Push origin") when he wants the remote current; only `main` is pushed, feature branches stay local until merged. The Windows desktop clones this repository for the engine work (A76). Update the same day: the GitHub CLI 2.103.3 is installed at `~/.local/bin/gh` (official release, checksum verified), Mohammad signed in with `gh auth login`, and git uses it as its credential helper for github.com, so the Lead pushes `main` and the branches other machines need. The five content branches waiting for engine proof are on GitHub. The official github plugin for pull requests and issues remains an option. Reverse cost: low.

### A77 2026-10-26 Test runs stay in the background: app windows from tests open hidden and never take focus
Mohammad uses the Mac while sessions work, and every e2e run opened a full-size app window that came to the front and took over the screen. Options: run e2e only when he is away; move the windows off-screen; launch them hidden. Chosen: the main process creates its window hidden, unfocusable, still painting, with no Dock icon, whenever the e2e harness sets a test flag (M0.1b, builder-shell), with a documented opt-out for watching a run by hand. Until that lands, no session or reviewer runs e2e or launches the app; merges skip e2e and log it. Nobody opens the windowed Unreal editor (A643 already). Reverse cost: low.

### A76 2026-10-26 Engine work moves to the Windows desktop; the Mac keeps app and content work without Unreal
Mohammad will not reinstall UE 5.8 on the Mac and plans to use the app on Windows. Options: reinstall on the Mac; move all work to Windows; split by need. Chosen: split. Every engine step (reference and starter runs, boss mutations, `--engine` verifier runs, the integration suite, the real-engine learner flow, Live Coding, and the long list of "unverified on Windows" items) moves to the Windows desktop, which becomes the engine verification machine (replaces A03). Work that needs no engine (app code, screens, content writing, reviews without the engine) can continue on the Mac. Getting the repository to Windows needs Mohammad: a private GitHub repository (free; private is not publishing) after his `gh auth login`, or a saved SSH connection from this app to the Windows machine. Until then, A75's rule stands: content waits for its engine proof. Reverse cost: low.

### A75 2026-10-26 Work continues while Unreal Engine is missing from the Mac; content waits for its engine proof
UE 5.8 is no longer at `~/Applications/UE_5.8` (the folder was removed around 2026-10-24; the Epic Games Launcher is still installed), so no engine run can happen on this Mac. Options: stop all engine-dependent work until an engine is back; merge everything with engine steps logged as unverified; split by risk. Chosen: split. Every non-engine step continues. Code branches whose only engine step is the integration suite merge after a passing review, with that suite logged as unverified in problems.md and rerun before any release once an engine is back. Content worlds and build-quest changes do not merge without an engine proof, because every reference solution must be compiled and tested (CLAUDE.md); they wait as "ready, engine proof pending". Reinstalling UE 5.8 through the Epic Games Launcher is free and is Mohammad's call; the Windows desktop can also run the proofs. Reverse cost: low.

### A74 2026-10-21 The Pack 1 author also owns Pack 3 (content-mechanics)
Pack 1 is fully written, and no author-mechanics session exists. Starting one needs Mohammad's words, and the Lead never waits on him. Options: leave Pack 3 idle until he starts a session; hand content-mechanics to the author-foundations session, which already knows the Unreal C++ ladder, the A67 boss shape, and the engine proof loop. Chosen: author-foundations owns content-mechanics too, one world at a time, with Pack 1 fix requests first. The ownership table in plan.md names it. Reverse cost: low (a new session can take Pack 3 over at any world boundary).

### A73 2026-10-21 World ids are unique across all packs
From the M1.6f review: the content catalog, `getWorld`, and the drill bank look worlds up by bare id, so two packs reusing a world id would hide one of them (its test-out drill could not start). Options: make world ids unique across packs; make every lookup pack-aware. Chosen: unique across packs, enforced as a loader error and a verifier error; M1.6f's pack and world keys stay as a safety net. No world on main shares an id today. Reverse cost: low.

### A72 2026-10-21 A term gates a quest only when a concept quest earlier in course order teaches it
From the M2.4 learner flow: World 1's builds list terms (createdefaultsubobject, tarray, actor-component, timer-manager, multicast-delegate) that only later worlds teach, so A312's rule ("terms any concept quest teaches must be Familiar") locked World 1's first build for a fresh learner. Options: remove those terms from the builds; teach them early; gate only on terms taught earlier. Chosen: a quest is gated only by terms taught by a concept quest that comes before it in course order (earlier pack, earlier world, or lower order in the same world). A term taught later still links and can be drilled, but does not lock. The verifier warns when a quest lists a term no earlier concept quest teaches, so authors can decide whether to teach it sooner. Reverse cost: low.

### A71 2026-10-21 Decision number ranges are assigned by the Lead
Parallel branches picked their own ranges and collided (A89, A470 to A474, A490, A491). Chosen: the Lead assigns each task a block of ten in its brief and records it in the table below; builders never pick a block themselves. Duplicates already on main stay until a renumbering pass (problems.md). Reverse cost: none.

Assigned blocks from now on: M1.7c A600 to A609; M1.4c A610 to A619; Pack 1 World 4 fixes A620 to A629; Pack 2 World 5 fixes A630 to A639; M2.4 A640 to A649; Pack 2 World 6 A650 to A659; M1.6e A660 to A669; M1.8d A670 to A679; Pack 1 terms and World 5 A680 to A699; M1.6f A700 to A709; M1.8e A710 to A719; Pack 1 World 6 A720 to A739; M1.7d A740 to A749; M1.8f A750 to A759; Pack 2 World 7 A760 to A779; Pack 2 World 8 A780 to A799; M1.6g A800 to A809; M2.2b A810 to A819; M2.1e A820 to A829; Pack 2 World 9 A830 to A849; M3.4 A850 to A869; M3.2 Pack 3 World 6 A870 to A889; M1.3c A890 to A899; Pack 2 World 10 A900 to A919; Pack 2 World 11 A920 to A939; M2.2c A940 to A949; M3.4b A950 to A969; M0.1b A970 to A979; M2.1f A980 to A989; W1 Windows progress tests A990 to A999; W2 Windows prerequisite filler A1000 to A1009; W3 Windows review cleanup A1010 to A1019; W0 Windows CI and setup A1020 to A1029; W6 World 5 Interactor A1050 to A1059; W7 Pack 3 World 6 fixes A1060 to A1069; W8 Windows integration suite A1070 to A1079; W9 hover card A1080 to A1089 (A1030 to A1049 unused); next free block A1090; M5.1 friendly errors, speed and a safe save A1190 to A1219.

### A70 2026-10-21 The paid API reviews a submit only when every test passed
From the M1.7c review: the spec reviews every submit that compiled, which with the learner's API key spends money on attempts whose tests failed. Options: review every compiled submit with any provider; never review failing submits; review failing submits only with the subscription-backed Claude Code. Chosen: Claude Code (subscription, no per-call cost) reviews any compiled submit, since findings on failing tests teach; the Anthropic API provider reviews only all-tests-passed submits, and a submit with failing tests falls back to tests-only grading with the reason shown. Recorded in `docs/systems/ai-review.md` by builder-review. Reverse cost: low.

### A69 2026-10-21 Strategy, Factory Method, and Facade get Pack 2 worlds 20 to 22, after Nystrom's 19
With one world per pattern (A491), the three patterns added beyond Nystrom's book need places. Options: interleave them where they fit thematically; append them after the 19 in card order. Chosen: append as worlds 20 to 22 in card order, after the combos world's prerequisites, so Part 3's teaching order stays intact. Reverse cost: low (folder renames).

### A68 2026-10-21 The Pack 4 gate names its Pack 2 world by id, pointed at the Component world
Pack 2 now has one world per pattern (A491), so "Pack 2 world 4" no longer means the Component world and will keep shifting as worlds are added. Options: keep the gate by order (A131) and renumber it each time; store the gate by world id. Chosen: the gate becomes "Pack 1 world 5 boss and the Pack 2 Component world's boss", stored by world id, and the verifier reports a gate whose world id does not exist. Supersedes the "by order" part of A131. Reverse cost: low.

### A67 2026-10-21 Every boss ships a compiled reference integration with a test that plays two rounds
The Pack 1 World 1 boss went through four text-only review rounds, each finding new gaps (targets that never drop, a bell that never rings in round 2, keys counted between rounds, no round start or round timer). Build quests never had this problem because their reference solutions are compiled and tested. Options: keep reviewing boss text by reading; require a reference integration. Chosen: each boss ships a C++ reference integration of one of its plans in `tools/solutions/<boss-id>/`, with automation tests that drive simulated time through round 1 and round 2 and assert the boss's observables (score, limits, bell, reset). The verifier's `--engine` builds and runs it like any build quest. The Blueprint plan stays text, checked against the C++ one. Reverse cost: low.

### A66 2026-10-21 Quest code follows the game-dev-rules naming, including enum value prefixes
From the M2.3 review: `EDojoF1TargetState` used Epic-style values (Lowered, Raised, Broken), while the game-dev-rules skill asks enum values to carry the enum's initials. Options: follow Epic's coding standard in quest code; follow Mohammad's game-dev-rules. Chosen: game-dev-rules, since the app teaches the conventions Mohammad wants to write by, and CLAUDE.md applies the skill to this project. Reverse cost: a rename across quests, tests, and solutions.

### A65 2026-10-21 A skipped or reset prerequisite's files are filled with its reference solution
From the M2.3 review: later quests (the score keeper, the boss) compile against earlier quests' learner files. Test-out skips worked and faded quests, and a reset removes a quest's files, so a later starter could fail to compile through no fault of the learner. Options: forbid cross-quest dependencies; fill missing prerequisite files with the reference solution; refuse to start the later quest. Chosen: fill with the reference solution and tell the learner which files came from the reference. Content declares each quest's cross-quest file dependencies; unreal-pipeline implements it. Reverse cost: low.

### A64 2026-10-21 Pattern chips stay hidden until the quest is passed
From the M2.3 review: `patterns[]` in front matter feeds the verifier's use-case counts and card unlocks, but showing a "State" chip in a brief names the pattern before it is built, against the author rule. Options: drop `patterns[]` from early quests; hide the chips until pass. Chosen: keep the data, hide the chips until the quest is passed (and in the breakdown until the reference plan is shown). Recorded in `docs/systems/ui-quests.md`. Reverse cost: none.

### A63 2026-10-21 Only `src/main/services/unreal/` spawns Unreal processes
Reviews found the verifier's `--engine` and the QA engine smoke each had their own Unreal spawning code, each with its own Windows bugs (no shell for `.bat` throws EINVAL on Node 24; cmd quoting breaks on `C:\Program Files`; timeouts kill only the top process). Options: fix each copy; route every Unreal build and test through M1.3's `UnrealBuilder` and `AutomationTestRunner`. Chosen: one implementation. The verifier and the smoke rebase onto M1.3 once it merges and call those classes. Reverse cost: low.

### A62 2026-10-21 Ordinary-word glossary terms link only inside inline code
From the M0.5 review: terms such as `check` and `ensure` are also everyday words ("check the flags"), and the TermLinker matches plain words case-insensitively, so prose would fill with wrong hover links. Options: drop those terms from linking; link them only in code spans; keep them linked everywhere. Chosen: an optional `linkMode: "code-only"` field on GlossaryEntry, honored by the M1.8 TermLinker; generic aliases (override, virtual, verify, assert, meta, Remove, dictionary) are removed. Reverse cost: low.

### A61 2026-10-21 The drill runner shuffles multiple-choice and which-pattern choices
From the M0.5 review: 23 of 26 authored answers sat at index 1, so position gave the answer away. Options: rely on authors spreading answers; shuffle at runtime; both. Chosen: both. Authors spread `answerIndex`, and the drill and concept screens shuffle choice order per presentation (the stored answer index refers to the authored order). Recorded in `docs/systems/ui-quests.md`. Reverse cost: none.

### A60 2026-10-21 The M0.1 scaffold from the background agent was merged; the duplicate session run is parked
When the repository was not yet trusted, M0.1, M0.5, and M1.2a ran as background agents; after trust, the original sessions also ran. For M0.1 the agent's branch was already reviewed and merged; the session branch `claude/m0-1-scaffold-de80f2` is kept unmerged for reference. For M0.5 and M1.2a the agents were stopped and the sessions kept. Reverse cost: none.
### A24 2026-10-21 Which glossary entries are code-only, and which aliases were cut (author-foundations, M0.5 fix round 1)
A62 named `check` and `ensure` and asked for any similar entries. Options: only those two; every entry whose term is an English word (Actor, Level, World, Tick); only entries whose term is a common verb or word that prose uses in a different sense. Chosen: the third, `linkMode: "code-only"` on `check-macro`, `ensure-macro`, `cast` ("cast a spell"), `destroy` ("destroy the crate"), `category-specifier` ("a category of"), and `super`. Actor, Pawn, Level, World, Tick, and similar stay linked in prose because prose in this app uses them in the Unreal sense. Aliases removed: `override` and `virtual` (super), `verify` and `assert` (check), `meta`, `Remove`, `dictionary`, `physics asset` (it named a different thing than physics simulation). Reverse cost: one field per entry.

### A23 2026-10-21 Glossary `category` stays a free display label (author-foundations, M0.5)
Options: a fixed enum in the types; a free string with a documented set. Chosen: a free string, keeping the 19 labels from the seed (`framework`, `reflection`, `collision`, `components`, `networking`, `memory`, `input`, `object model`, `build`, `debugging`, `editor workflow`, `animation`, `math`, `AI`, `containers`, `strings`, `UI`, `cpp idiom`, `physics`). Authors reuse one before adding a new one (`docs/systems/content-authoring.md`). Why: the UI only shows it as a chip and filter today, and later packs will need new labels without a type change. Reverse cost: low; the M0.4 verifier can turn the current set into an enum.

### A22 2026-10-21 Pattern card ids, order, and the three extra patterns (author-foundations, M0.5)
Options for ids: book chapter titles as written, kebab-case. Chosen: kebab-case (`game-loop`, `subclass-sandbox`), with Factory named `factory-method` because that is the variant the cards teach (a creation function plus `TSubclassOf`), not Abstract Factory. Order in `content/patterns.json` follows Part 3 of `research/patterns-inventory.md`, then Strategy, Factory Method, Facade. The inventory has no sections for those three, so their cards were written from scratch in the same Unreal-flavored form. Why: ids appear in quest front matter and drill item ids, so they must be stable and typeable. Reverse cost: low now, rising once quests reference them.

### A21 2026-10-21 Glossary docs links are locale-free and pinned to 5.8 (author-foundations, M0.5)
Options: keep the seed's mix of `/en-us/` and locale-free links with no version; pin each link with `?application_version=5.8`. Chosen: locale-free and pinned. Every one of the 125 distinct links was fetched and returns a page titled "Unreal Engine 5.8 Documentation".
